# Requirements pipeline: final design

18 September 2026, corrected 19 September 2026. The lead architect's final design for the requirements distiller and the backlog implementor. It takes each component from the candidate that is best in use (0.2), grafts the ideas all three judges named, removes every fatal flaw any judge found, and settles every open question. The triage change list (`design/corrections/change-list.md`, changes C-001 to C-073; the consolidation (`design/consolidation/decision.md`, C-074 to C-082) then removed every mechanical agent but one per gap between judgement spans) corrected it on merit alone (R8); a section changed by the triage cites the change id.

- **Status:** ready to build. The build plan is section 11 and `design/backlog.json`. The six decisions that need Sam are in section 9.2 and `design/decisions-for-sam.md`.
- **Inputs:** `analysis/sam-requirements.md` (R1 to R8; Sam added R7, full-stack slices, and R8, build the right thing, while this design was being written, and no candidate or judge saw either), the audits and triage under `design/corrections/`, `analysis/rules-probe.md`, `analysis/00-synthesis.md` (cited S §n), the analyst files A to H and G1 to G5, the three candidates (`design/candidates/{reuse,quality,codex}.md`) and the three verdicts (`design/judging/judge-{1,2,3}.md`).
- **Citations** are `file:line` on `main` at `d07af6b9` unless marked `FA:` (`.claude/workflows/frontend-alignment.js` on `feat/NO_JIRA-frontend-alignment` at `2becd3e7`) or named to another branch.
- **Register:** GDS plain British English. Size is stated as increments, files, commands, agents and risk, never time.

## 0. What this design is

### 0.1 In one paragraph

The pipeline is two thin skills driving two workflows over one requirement-shaped backlog, with one deterministic writer holding every piece of state.

- The **requirements-distiller** skill is a new skill (C-041); its lineage is recorded per persona in 5.9's "Cut from" column, and `journey-builder` stays in place, frozen. It runs `.claude/workflows/backlog-distil.js`, a thin interpreter over `tim backlog advance --distil` (C-002). That workflow turns any mix of sources into verified single-requirement atoms, each the thinnest end-to-end slice of behaviour someone can observe (pass 1), reconciles and adjudicates them, combines related atoms into buildable increments (pass 2), and renders a decision-led report Sam rules from.
- The **backlog-implementor** skill is a new skill that carries forward build-orchestrator's one-tier driver, landed check, stop taxonomy and handover. It runs `.claude/workflows/backlog-build.js`, written new as a thin interpreter over `tim backlog advance` (C-002). `increment-build-loop.js` and build-orchestrator are not moved: they keep serving legacy backlogs until inc-040 retires them. The workflow drains the backlog one increment at a time, and treats each increment as one vertical slice across every repo it touches. It plans each increment just in time, has the plan audited against the requirement, implements, reviews every changed file with the `review` and `code-style` skills' own personas read live, verifies, judges, fixes, proves the result from recorded facts, including an integration proof through the real stack, then has a fresh task judge acceptance, and lands the slice under the programme's delivery policy.
- **tim decides and executes; agents judge.** `tim backlog advance` holds every transition rule and runs every local, reversible step itself: branches, merges of base, rungs, the stack, manifests, commits, fact records and audits. One cheap step agent per gap between judgement spans runs it in a loop. Each push, pull request, merge and Jira write is generated by tim and run as its own visible call, and tim records its effect by looking (C-074 to C-076).
- Every model stage runs through **one contract with two adapters**, Claude or Codex, bound per stage role in `build/run.json`, which the backlog never sees.
- **`tim backlog`** (generalised from `tim parity`'s writer, with `git mv` used only to keep history) is the only thing that writes state. It refuses unverified, recipe-shaped and secret-bearing content, and it will not mark an increment built unless a deterministic audit of that increment's stage records passes.

### 0.2 Which candidate each component comes from, and why

The judges' consensus named the reuse candidate the winner, but their `reuse` dimension broke a tie that R8 says it must not (C-001). Without that row, judge 1 scores quality 80, reuse 77, codex 76. So no candidate is the skeleton. Each component comes from the candidate whose version is better in use, judged only on output quality, requirements and report quality, Codex parity, vertical slices, correctness and operability under the rails.

| Component | What it covers | Taken from | Merit in use |
|---|---|---|---|
| Data model | Atoms plus increments whose members are atoms, `run.json` outside the backlog, a drain with no nested workflows | reuse | No stale acceptance (acceptance is read through members, never copied); R5 (nothing executor-specific in the backlog); operability (no nesting to break) |
| Requirements method | Extract-verify, trace closure, question editor and critic, combine verifier, report sections | quality | Requirements quality and report quality, R1's top priority |
| Control plane | The decision engine `tim backlog advance`, file-first tasks, receipts, op ids, prompt parity, reading audit, review routing as data | codex | Codex parity; a state machine tested on fixtures instead of prose; a lower overhead on every run |
| Lifecycle execution | tim executes every local effect; each outward action is a separate generated call run by the step agent and observed afterwards | the judges' objection to codex, revised by the consolidation | Every outward action stays a call guard-bash sees, local steps become tested code, and a run spends 4 cheap agents an increment instead of 58 (C-075, C-076) |

The grafts below close the remaining gaps. Rows the triage later changed name the change.

| # | Change | From | Judges | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Codex and Claude output travel as files, validated by one accept command that writes a receipt; no LLM courier re-emits JSON | codex | 1, 2, 3 | 8.1, 8.2 |
| 2 | Done gate: an increment is marked built only when a deterministic audit of its stage records passes; a missing receipt is a red | codex | 3 | 4.5, 7.9 |
| 3 | Extract-verify phase, trace-matrix closure, gap auditors for thin slices | quality | 1, 2, 3 | 5.2 |
| 4 | QUESTION_EDITOR, a separate QUESTION_CRITIC, a mechanical question lint, ranking by blast radius | quality | 1, 2, 3 | 5.6 |
| 5 | The writer checks confidence against source role and refuses atoms resting only on constraint, consistency-anchor or current-behaviour sources | quality | 1, 3 | 3.12, 4.3 |
| 6 | Strict v2 schema that refuses recipe and executor keys by name | quality | 3 | 3.14 |
| 7 | Verification guard: `verifiedBy` must be a registered run's task, never a placeholder | architect, after judge 3 | 3 | 3.4, 4.3 |
| 8 | Plan-audit, fix-verify and an independent acceptance stage after the ladder | quality | 1, 2, 3 | 7.4 |
| 9 | One consistency reviewer per increment that reads every touched repo's change and writes a verdict per repo: the review skill's real granularity, corrected against the source (0.4). The persona gains a method for the contract between repos, with a verdict per plan seam, and its findings go through verify, judge and fix (C-023, C-024) | quality, corrected | 1, 2 | 8.5 |
| 10 | Prompt parity, reading audit, three full-stack canaries that between them cover the three routing families, one of them combined, cross-examination, backlog hash unchanged | codex, quality | 1, 2, 3 | 8.10 |
| 11 | Rules resolution adds CLAUDE.md chains, their imports and the memory index; dead-pointer lint; house-rules question for Sam | codex | 1, 3 | 8.6, 9.2 |
| 12 | `--op-id` idempotency on every write | codex | 1, 2 | 4.3 |
| 13 | Read-only Codex sandbox for review, verify, judge, audit and acceptance roles | codex | 1, 2 | 8.4 |
| 14 | tim test: every workflow's GUARD RAILS equals one canonical text | codex | 1, 3 | 4.6 |
| 15 | A red baseline becomes a hygiene increment ahead of the current one, which is held | codex | 1, 2, 3 | 7.4 |
| 16 | Codex-orchestrated third mode generated from the same commands; named presets; `run explain` | codex | 1, 3 | 8.9, 8.11 |

### 0.3 Fatal flaws removed

| Flaw | Judge | How this design removes it |
|---|---|---|
| Quality's own backlog was single-pass, with compound atoms and a placeholder verification record | 1, 3 | The self-backlog (section 11) holds single-requirement atoms combined into increments under stated rules, a ceiling and recorded declined pairs; 11.6 gives the current counts. Every atom is honestly `proposed` with `verifiedBy: null`. The writer refuses placeholders. A different agent verifies every atom against `sam-requirements.md` before inc-001, and inc-010 records that in a registered run before the new path builds any of them (C-053). |
| Codex's Sonar stages rest on the `sonar` CLI, which agents cannot run | 1 | No agent runs `sonar`. SonarCloud results come from the PR checks through tim's CI check waiter; silence is recorded as "no report", never "clean". `sonar-check-pending.sh` is not used: its records come from a hook that never fires for the build's pushes (C-076). |
| Codex moves push and merge into tim, outside `guard-bash`, the push hook and the auto-mode classifier | 2 | tim never pushes, merges a PR or writes to Jira. Each is its own Bash call that tim generates only after its own refusals pass (branch proof, fast-forward, expected head, every PR green), run verbatim by the step agent, so guard-bash sees each one; tim then observes the effect. Every one of these calls is allowlisted (`settings.json:83, 88`), so the allowlist is not the gate (C-042, C-075, C-076). |
| Codex relies on processes that outlive a Bash call | 2 | Nothing outlives a Bash call. The Codex runner stops its whole process tree at the slice deadline and resumes the sessions on the next call. Every tim macro and the Codex runner end their call before 540 seconds with exit 75, killing every descendant process first. |
| The stage machine lives in untested places: prose for a Codex-orchestrated session, workflow JavaScript for Claude | 3, reframed by r8-merit | tim decides and executes; agents judge. `tim backlog advance` holds every transition rule and is tested with vitest on fixture folders. The workflow script is a thin interpreter, and a Codex-orchestrated session runs the same command. Outward commands are generated by tim and run verbatim by the step agent. The judge's objection was to rebuild cost, which carries no weight (R8); the merit-based refusals stand: tim never pushes, merges a PR or writes to Jira itself, never shells out to `tools/` for an integration (a registry rung that is a committed script, such as `tools/backlog/self-test.sh`, is a test command `rung` runs like `npm test`), and nothing outlives a Bash call (C-002). |
| Codex leaks file paths into rows through an exemplar slot | 3 | There is no exemplar or hint slot. Repo how-knowledge is a reading list on the repo's registry entry, never the backlog (C-058). `consistentWith` names a behaviour, never a file. A citation from a source that is not a requirement is labelled in every brief "evidence of current behaviour, not a design to copy", and the plan audit objects when a plan adopts a cited file as a template without a stated decision (C-063). |

The judges also named weaknesses in the winner that were not fatal: the LLM courier (graft 1), the consistency reviewer's granularity (graft 9, where the source settled it), the judge on Opus in Codex mode (graft 17), thinner distillation checks (grafts 3 to 5), self-reported `rulesRead[]` (graft 10) and one large workflow file (graft 21). Each is closed above.

### 0.4 Facts re-checked against the source for this design

- **Permissions.** `Bash(tim:*)`, `tools/**`, `scripts/**` and `npm --prefix ~/git/defra/trade-imports-workspace/*` are allowlisted (`.claude/settings.json:63-66, 85, 88`). `codex` and `gh` are not.
- **The current loop cannot build this programme.** It accepts only `repos/*` paths (`increment-build-loop.js:183-189`), so it cannot target the workspace repo or `tim/`. This programme's first increments are therefore built by hand (section 11.1).
- **House rules.** The loop inlines four house rules for Claude style reviewers (`increment-build-loop.js:1195-1197`). Codex never sees them.
- **What agents receive at start.** Workflow agents receive the CLAUDE.md chain and the user's auto-memory index at start (rules probe table row 1, and observed in this design's own workflow-agent context). Codex receives neither. Left alone, every `codex exec` would inherit `~/.codex` instead: its model, notify, MCP servers, plugins, memories, skills and hooks. So Codex receives only what the pipeline-owned `CODEX_HOME` (`tools/codex/home/`) carries, and nothing from `~/.codex`; each receipt records the Codex version, model, effort and a configuration hash (C-009).
- **The code-style routers.** `bake-rules-bundle.sh` accepts any non-empty key (`:20-27`) and maps each topic to its files in one place (`:35-73`). `file-topics.sh` is a pure function of the path (`:1-22`). `detect-tech.sh` prints JSON (`:1-6, 279`).
- **Rule pointers.** Every `.claude/rules` pointer resolves to an existing file today, including `java/spring-data-mongodb.md`. The globs in `tim/.claude/rules/*.md` are relative to `tim/`.
- **Fixer personas.** The fixer personas contain their own commit step (`REVIEW_ITEM_FIXER.md` Step 5) and commit and mark-outcome steps (`STYLE_IMPLEMENTOR.md` Steps 5 and 6). A workflow that runs them by path must replace those sections (8.5).
- **Sonar.** The per-call `PostToolUse` push hook records pending SonarCloud checks in `.sonar-checks/` (`scripts/sonar/sonar-record-push.sh:18-50`). Only session hooks read them. The reader is allowlisted by path and fails open (`scripts/sonar/sonar-check-pending.sh:22-24, 65`). The hook's `if` is `Bash(git push*)` (`.claude/settings.json:139`), so it does not fire for the loop's `git -C <repo> push …` calls, and the `git push --force` deny rules do not match `git -C` either. Agents also commit without `sonar analyze --staged`, which they cannot run: a deviation from CLAUDE.md rule 3 that is recorded for Sam to accept or refuse (C-042).
- **Consistency review.** The review skill spawns **one** consistency reviewer, which reads every repo's diff together ("You need the full picture across all repos simultaneously") and writes one check per repo (`.claude/skills/review/SKILL.md:147-162`, `references/CONSISTENCY_REVIEWER.md:31-44, 64-111`). The "(one per repo)" at `SKILL.md:66` describes the output, not the number of agents. Judges 1 and 2 read it as one agent per repo. This design follows the source on granularity. The persona's categories (config, dependency bumps, structural patterns, tests, docs, flags) check peer patterns only, and it has no method for the contract between a provider and a consumer, which is what R7 needs. That method goes into the persona itself, so people running the review skill gain it too (C-023).
- **R7 arrived during the design.** `analysis/sam-requirements.md` changed after it was sealed (blob `5f3c88e0` became `0c51d842`). The only change is R7, full-stack slices. The loop already has half of it: a merge order of backend, then tests, then frontend, with every PR green before any merges (`increment-build-loop.js:299-309`). The other half is missing. The plants loop backlog (`workareas/journey-builder/EUDPA-409/backlog.json`, read on 18 September 2026) has 65 increments: by their `repo` field, 47 are frontend-only, 10 tests-only, 2 backend-only, 2 span two repos and 4 have no value.
- **R8 arrived after the design.** Sam added R8, build the right thing: implementation effort, file count, diff size and "least new surface" carry no weight, and a fixed overhead every run pays does count. The design panel's "reuse, smallest new surface" angle and the judges' `reuse` dimension decided a tie, so every decision that rested on reuse or build cost was re-decided on merit (`design/corrections/r8-merit.md`, C-001).
- **tim.** tim has a `tim` npm script (`tim/package.json:18`) and uses zod 4. It has no glob library.
- **Branch-only helpers.** `pr-ensure-draft.sh`, `wait-for-pr-checks.sh` and `npm-in-repo.sh --clone` exist only on `feat/NO_JIRA-frontend-alignment`. `tools/codex/` does not exist. The first two are not ported: tim's `github pr` commands carry their contracts (C-079). `npm-in-repo.sh --clone` is ported.
- **Consolidation facts** (19 September): tim spawns through execa (`exec/exec.js:24, 52`) and runs across repos (`exec/parallel.js:12`); `workspace/branch.js:146-151` creates only a tracking branch; `github-client.js` and `jira-client.js` are read-only; `Bash(tim:*)` and `Bash(git -C ~/…:*)` are allowlisted (`settings.json:83, 88`), so outward tim and git calls are auto-approved and guard-bash (`settings.json:100-109`) is the per-call hook that sees them; the review skill has no verifier persona (`review/SKILL.md:65-67`).
- **Workflow runtime.** Scripts have no clock (`Date.now()` throws), `agent()` returns null when an agent dies, `parallel()` never rejects, `workflow()` nests one level, at most 16 agents run concurrently and a run has at most 1,000 agents (the built-in workflow-authoring reference).

---

## 1. Principles

1. **A backlog item is a requirement, never a recipe.** It says what must be true, why, how a person would observe it and where the requirement came from. The v2 schema refuses file lists, commands, ladders, skill names and executor fields by name, and a lint reports recipe-shaped text and executor vocabulary in every prose field (C-056). A repo's entry in the backlog says only what the repo is and what it consumes; how to build and check it (ladders, reading lists, generated globs) lives on the repo's registry entry (C-058). The plan the workflow writes just in time is the only recipe (R1; S §5.3).
2. **Distillation has two passes, and both are data.** Pass 1 writes one atom per requirement until every source unit is accounted for. Pass 2 combines related atoms into increments under stated rules, records what it left apart, and re-runs after every ruling without moving an id (R1; S §7.8).
3. **Whoever wrote it does not check it, and nothing claims a check it did not get.** Extracts, atoms, combinations, plans, fixes, acceptance and report prose are each checked by a different agent. The writer stamps provenance from task identity and refuses a verification that is not a registered run's task (R1; judge 3).
4. **One writer owns all state.** Only `tim backlog` changes the backlog, run state, run configuration and the phase ledger. Every write is schema-validated, locked, checked for lost updates and idempotent by operation id. No agent composes JSON over state (S §4.6, §8.4).
5. **The backlog does not know how it will be built.** Executor bindings, model tiers, run knobs and prompts live with the implementor. The same `backlog.json`, unmodified, builds under either executor or a mix, and switching part-way is a write to `build/run.json` (R5).
6. **One stage contract, two adapters, one bar.** Each model stage has one brief, one output schema and one accept command for both executors. Both executors run the same fan-out, and their prompts are identical apart from the executor profile. The briefs are written fresh from the contract and hold no executor text; anything only one executor needs lives in its profile (R6; C-007).
7. **Standards are read live, by path, from their owners.** Personas, the skills' own routers and bundles, path rules and the files they point to, CLAUDE.md chains and the memory index are resolved when a task is prepared. They are never copied into a script or a brief (R2, R3).
8. **Facts come from tools, and hook work becomes explicit steps.** Ladder results are recorded exit codes, completeness is a command, the next stage is decided by one tested command (`tim backlog advance`, C-002), which also runs every deterministic step, issued by one cheap step agent per gap and never by a judgement task (C-074, C-075), and an increment is done only when a deterministic audit of its stage records passes. What session hooks did for the main session, such as secrets scanning, SonarCloud results and rule loading, is a stage or recorded state for every executor (R4; S §6.4).
9. **Every increment is a vertical slice of behaviour.** A pass 1 atom is the thinnest end-to-end behaviour a user or system can observe, and it spans every repo that behaviour needs. Repos follow from the slice and never divide the work. The writer refuses layer-split rows, including dependency chains that step from provider to consumer (P11, C-025). One plan, one implementation and one review round cover the whole slice, the consistency reviewer checks the contract between repos, done includes an integration proof through the real stack, and the slice's PRs merge providers first in the order each repo's `consumes` gives (R7; C-023, C-026).
10. **The workflow plans each increment just in time and checks the plan against the requirement.** The planner reads the live code. The plan-auditor derives the requirement from the backlog row, never from the plan's restatement of it (C-060). The acceptance checker judges the final change against the backlog, after the integration proof, and never receives the plan (R1; S Q27; C-021).
11. **The report is a generated decision surface.** What needs Sam comes first, then the calls he may reverse. Every number is derived. Machine-written prose is copy-edited and claim-checked by different agents. No agent edits the page (R1; S §6.3).
12. **Take the best ideas and proven mechanisms, and choose on merit in use.** Options are compared only on output quality, requirements and report quality, Codex parity, vertical slices, correctness and operability under the rails. Build effort, file count, diff size and new surface carry no weight (R8). Collapsing an overhead that every run pays counts. `git mv` is used only to keep history, never as a reason. Headless stages decide and record; a must-answer question blocks what it touches until a person rules (R1, R8; S §8.11; C-001).

## 2. Architecture overview

### 2.1 The diagram

```
 SOURCES (any mix)
 Confluence . Jira issues and comments . Word, PDF, markdown . images and design boards . repos and code
 transcripts, chat, email . existing backlogs and parity corpora . OpenSpec specs . web pages
      |
      |  MAIN SESSION: skill requirements-distiller     dispatcher: tim backlog phase <p> status --json
      |    NEW: interview (references/INTERVIEWER.md, lettered options) -> tim backlog init <p>
      |         tim backlog source add | acquire | seal ; distil/CONTRACT.md from the template, whole
      |    RESUME | ADD-SOURCE | REFRESH | RULE | RECOMBINE | REPORT
      |    tim backlog run open <p> --label <runLabel> --mode distil --at <ISO> --trailer-file <file> ; Workflow({scriptPath, args}) ; tim backlog run close
      |    (per launch; launch 1 ends at the contract, launch 2 runs from the contract to the report; C-080)
      v
 +--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | Workflow .claude/workflows/backlog-distil.js args: {programme, phases, at, runLabel, workspace, scope} |
 | a thin interpreter: a step agent per gap loops advance --distil -> run (a span) | act | handback | stop |
 |                                                                                  (C-002, C-074)       |
 | every model task: tim backlog stage prepare -> [Claude agent | Codex inside advance: tim backlog codex |
 |                   run] -> stage accept                                                       (C-078)   |
 | every gate runs in-process inside advance; a failed gate stops with gate:<name> and the missing list  |
 | spans: characterise -> extract-verify per source ; author -> verify per slice ; gap -> gap-verify ;   |
 |        judges -> chair -> critic ; critic -> questions ; combiner -> verifier ; editor -> claim verifier|
 |                                                                                              (C-077)   |
 |                                                                                                        |
 |  heads .......... tim backlog heads --write                                                            |
 |  characterise ... 1 task per source: SOURCE_CHARACTERISER + source-types/<kind>.md -> units.json;     |
 |                   a crop per visual unit: tim backlog source crop, hashed               (C-039)       |
 |  extract-verify . 1 different task per source: EXTRACT_VERIFIER, a verdict per unit, searchedFor       |
 |                   gate: tim backlog units --strict                                              P1    |
 |  carryover ...... 1 task, only when earlier work exists: CARRYOVER_TRIAGER                             |
 |  reslice ........ only after migrate: RESLICER -> one vertical atom per behaviour, carriedFrom[] ;    |
 |                   a different task verifies it ; the legacy layer rows become superseded   (C-034)   |
 |  slice .......... 1 task: SLICER, slices are areas of behaviour ; gate: slices --strict        P2    |
 |  ==== PASS 1 ==  author -> verify pipelined per slice: REQUIREMENT_AUTHOR, REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER        |
 |                   distil/atoms/<slice>--<slug>.json, notRaised[], premisesDisproved[]                  |
 |                   gates: tim backlog yield --strict (P3), coverage --strict ; GAP_AUDITOR if thin      |
 |  reconcile ...... 1 task: RECONCILER -> conflicts, precedence, assumptions, invariants, questions      |

 +--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

 workareas/shared/<programme>/backlog.json    header + ledgers + requirements[] (atoms) + increments[]

      |
      |<-- RULINGS LOOP ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
      |    Sam rules on the page (copy batch), the answer sheet, or in conversation                        |
      |    tim backlog rule <p> <q> --option B --words "..." --by sam --at <ISO> --note "<why>"  (--tentative)
      |    -> decision appended; option effects applied to atoms in the same write; needs, statuses redone |
      |    -> RULING_APPLIER only for free-text answers ; phases combine..report reset (the reset cascades)|
      |    a question with a default: tim backlog rule --by default applies it (decision `defaulted`)      |
      |    a reversal: tim backlog rule --subject conflict:<id> --choose <side> | combination:<key> --split |
      |    -> backlog-distil.js re-runs combine..report ; ids stable ; started increments frozen ----------+
      |
      |  MAIN SESSION: skill backlog-implementor     dispatcher: tim backlog next <p> --json
      |    "build inc-012 to inc-020 with Codex" -> tim backlog run bind <p> --ids inc-012..inc-020 --preset codex
      |                                              --said "<his words>" --by sam --at <ISO>
      |    tim backlog run open <p> --label <runLabel> --mode build --at <ISO> --trailer-file <file> [--pin]
      |    Workflow({scriptPath: <ABS>/.claude/workflows/backlog-build.js,
      |              args: {programme, cadence, count, at, runLabel, commitTrailer, workspace, mode, firstStep?}})
      |    tim backlog run close <p> --label <runLabel>                                        (C-080)
      v
 +--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | Workflow .claude/workflows/backlog-build.js    a thin interpreter over advance; drains; no nesting     |
 |  LOOP one step agent per gap: tim backlog advance <p> --run <label> --agent <id> --json (C-002, C-074)|
 |       run      -> walk the span graph through the adapters                                (C-077)   |
 |       act      -> the step agent runs each outward command as its own call, observed on the next     |
 |                   advance                                                                  (C-076)   |
 |       handback -> fresh step agent ; stop -> return                                                   |
 |  stage order, every transition rule, holds and resume live in advance's tested stage table; M runs   |
 |  are mechanical (tim, one step agent each), J spans are judgement (agents only):                      |
 |  M0 0 run open ...... launching session: ABS and TILDE, header, run.json, tool pin, Codex schemas     |
 |                       exported ; no agent                                                  (C-080)   |
 |  M1 1 next .......... in-process: one rule; that increment's bindings, re-read at every stage          |
 |     2-6 ticket, branch, baseline (tracked-only fingerprints), sync, ladder facts: tim backlog prepare,  |
 |                       in-process ; the Jira create, when set, is a visible call ; the sync merge is    |
 |                       pushed with land                                                     (C-075)   |
 |  J1 7 PLAN .......... [Claude think | Codex high, read-only] -> build/plans/<id>.md ; seams, invariants |
 |     8 PLAN-AUDIT .... a different task derives the requirement from the row, never from the plan's    |
 |                       restatement of it ; on revise, one more plan and audit in the span  (C-060)   |
 |  M2 7f-8f finalise .. build/plans/<id>.md and state.plan written ; implement prepared                 |
 |     9 added repos ... only when the plan adds a repo: tim backlog prepare --repos for it; surface     |
 |                       amended                                                              (C-031)   |
 |  J2 10 IMPLEMENT .... [Claude doer | Codex medium, write] ; standards from tim backlog standards      |
 |                       (the skills' own routing data, read live, shas recorded)              (C-037)   |
 |  M3 11 IMPLEMENT CHECK tim backlog check runs browser and Docker rungs and command invariants ;      |
 |                       resume implement on red                                                        |
 |     12 MANIFEST ..... diffs from the attempt's pinned heads ; rulesGap[] ; no commit, no ref move ;   |
 |                       the J3 span prepared at once                                                   |
 |  J3 13 REVIEW ....... per content-changed file: style (STYLE_FILE_REVIEWER) + code (FILE_REVIEWER)   |
 |                       one consistency task over every touched repo (CONSISTENCY_REVIEWER: a        |
 |                       verdict per repo and per plan seam, the contract between repos)  (C-023)     |
 |     14 VERIFY ....... per file with findings, started when both its reviews are accepted, plus one    |
 |                       verify-consistency task when consistency has findings ; default refuted        |
 |     15 JUDGE ........ fix-now, defer or reject, for file and consistency findings, when any stands   |
 |     26d DISCOVERED .. a REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER task, only when the judge discovered work              |
 |     16 FIX .......... REVIEW_ITEM_FIXER + STYLE_IMPLEMENTOR (method sections) ; files may span repos ;|
 |                       only when a fix-now ruling exists ; its accept saves the fix diff              |
 |     17 FIX-VERIFY ... a different task, from the saved diff, before the post-fix check ;            |
 |                       consistency again when a fix touches a seam file                     (C-077)   |
 |  M4 11r POST-FIX CHECK tim backlog check --after fix ; on red the fixer resumes and fix-verify re-runs|
 |     18 LADDER ....... tim backlog prove: registry ladders + plan extras ; fact records ; repair, then |
 |                       a repair-review span (style, code, consistency when a seam is touched) (C-021) |
 |     19 SECRETS ...... tim backlog secrets, inside prove                                              |
 |     20 INTEGRATION PROOF tim backlog prove, sharded, sliced with exit 75 ; a record of what ran, on   |
 |                       what, proving what                                                              |

 |                       passes) ; report ; build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md ; state files committed by name ;   |

 |     27 checkpoint ... a checkpoint stops the drain ; otherwise the same step agent carries on into M1 |
 |  model stage:  stage prepare -> task folder (prompt.md, input/, codex.json)                            |
 |                Claude: one agent per task writes output.draft.json and runs stage accept               |

 +--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
      |
      v

      -> report re-rendered: section 1 gains build-time questions, holds and walkthroughs;

```

---

## 3. `backlog.json` schema v2

### 3.1 Files in a programme workarea

A programme lives at `workareas/shared/<programme>/`, tracked (Q2). Only `tim backlog` writes the JSON files.

| File | Holds | Written by | Read by | Tracked |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `backlog.json` | The requirement: header, ledgers, atoms, increments, row status | `tim backlog` only | everyone | yes |
| `distil/CONTRACT.md` | The run's distillation contract (5.3) | the skill at setup, then a person; whole before any author runs | every distil task | yes |
| `distil/phases.json` | Phase ledger, registered distil runs | `tim backlog phase`, `run start` (inside `run open`) | the skill, gates, ingest guard | yes |
| `distil/heads.json`, `distil/seals.json` | Repo heads by key; content seals per source | `tim backlog heads`, `source seal` | drift panel, plan evidence check | yes |
| `distil/sources/<id>/characterisation.md`, `units.json`, `extract-verify.json` | Structure found, addressable units with provenance tokens and `readAt`, per-unit verdicts | characterise, extract-verify (through `stage accept`) | slice, author, verify, coverage, trace | yes (see retention) |
| `distil/sources/<id>/raw/` | Fetched copies | `source acquire`, the skill | characterise | no (gitignored) |
| `distil/sources/<id>/crops/<slug>.png` | A crop per visual unit, its `{crop, box, sha256}` recorded on the unit (C-039) | `tim backlog source crop` | question editor, report | yes |
| `distil/reslice.json` | Candidate behaviour groups for migrated legacy layer rows (C-034) | `migrate` | reslice | yes |
| `distil/slices.json`, `carryover.json`, `critique.json`, `panel/*.json`, `authoring/<slice>.json` | Slicing, carryover verdicts, critic output, panel records, `notRaised[]` and `premisesDisproved[]` | tasks, through `stage accept` | gates, report | yes |
| `distil/atoms/<slice>--<slug>.json` | One authored atom per file (pass 1 input) | author tasks; provenance stamped by `stage accept` | `ingest --atoms` | yes |
| `distil/increments/<slug>.json` | One authored group per file (pass 2 input) | combiner task | `ingest --increments` | yes |
| `build/run.json` | Executor bindings and run knobs | `tim backlog run` | the build workflow | yes: committed after every `run bind` or `run set`, and in each record stage's state commit (7.6) |
| `build/state.json` | Run state per increment: phase, attempts, holds, ticket, branch, heads, plan, commits, PRs, CI, audits, acceptance verdicts; registered build runs | `tim backlog state`, `stage accept`, `status`, the macros | `next`, resume, report | yes |
| `build/journal.jsonl` | Notes `{at, id, stage, by, note}` | `tim backlog state <id> note` | report, handover | yes |
| `build/plans/<id>.md` | The just-in-time plan, the only recipe | plan task, written by `stage accept` | plan-audit, review-style, review-code, consistency, verify, judge and fix, as `input/upstream/plan.md` (C-022); never acceptance (C-021) | yes |
| `build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md` | A standing handover so Codex can take over with no Claude session (C-003) | `tim backlog handover --executor codex --md`, at record and at every hold | Sam, a Codex-orchestrated session | yes |
| `build/reads/<session>.jsonl` | Claude `Read` calls per session, for the reading audit (C-011) | a committed `PostToolUse` Read hook | `stage accept`, `audit-reading` | no |
| `build/.accept-key` | The key for receipts' `acceptMac` (C-012) and fact records' `factMac` (C-082) | `run start` (inside `run open`) | `stage accept`, `stage audit`, fact records | no |
| `build/runs/<label>/<inc>/a<n>/facts/<step>.json` | One fact record per mechanical sub-step: `{step, argv, cwd, exit, log, logSha, startedAt, endedAt, headsBefore, headsAfter, observed, factMac}` (C-082) | tim only | `stage accept`, `stage audit`, resume | no (inside `build/runs/`) |
| `build/runs/`, `distil/runs/` | Task folders: prompts, inputs, drafts, outputs, receipts, Codex logs and sessions | `stage prepare`, tasks, `stage accept` | the workflows, audits, parity proof | no (gitignored) |
| `report/report.md`, `report/answer-sheet.md` | Generated views | `tim backlog report` | Sam | yes |
| `report/index.html`, `report/artifact.html` | Generated views | `tim backlog report` | Sam | no (regenerated) |
| `.backlog.lock`, `build/.state.lock`, `build/.run.lock`, `.backlog-ops.jsonl` | Locks and the operation log | `tim backlog` | `tim backlog` | no |

### 3.2 Why run state is a separate file, not on the row

1. **`git diff backlog.json` is a review surface.** `CLAIM_VERIFIER.md:17` reviews by diff, and parity split `evidence.json` out for exactly this (`citations/evidence.js:199-201`). FA's journal was 217 KB against 53 KB of brief (E §6.8).
2. **R5.** Attempts record which executor built what. That is state, and it must never be in the backlog.
3. **Blast radius.** FA's recorder writes landed on the header (S §4.6). A separate file with its own lock cannot corrupt a requirement.
4. **Readers.** Every reader keys only on `id`, `status` and `dependsOn` (E §5). Those stay on the row. Everything else moves.

### 3.3 Header

| Field | Type | Req | Meaning | Written by | Read by | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `schemaVersion` | `2` | yes | Format version; tim refuses anything else | `init` | every tim command | meta |
| `programme` | slug | yes | Registry key; commit scope; branch slug source | `init` | everyone | meta |
| `profile` | `"requirements-v2"` | yes | Selects schema, id prefixes, kinds and the phase table | `init` | tim | meta |
| `title` | string, 80 characters or fewer | yes | Plain-English name of what is being built | setup | report masthead, PR title | requirement |
| `purpose` | string, two to four sentences | yes | Who it is for, what outcome, why now | setup, reconcile | every stage brief, report | requirement |
| `sources[]` | Source (3.8) | yes | Every source read, with role, tier, version, seal and retention | `source add` | characterise, author, verify, report | requirement (provenance) |
| `precedence[]` | `{factKind, order[sourceId], why}` | yes, may be empty | Which source wins per fact kind: `copy`, `values`, `mandate`, `flow`, `intent`, `scope`, `data-shape`, `integration` | setup, reconcile, `rule` | reconcile, panel, report | requirement |
| `invariants[]` | Invariant | yes | Cross-cutting rules every increment honours, stated once | reconcile, `rule` | plan, plan-audit, every reviewer, judge, acceptance | requirement |
| `direction` | string or null | no | Tie-break rule for choices the sources leave open | reconcile, `rule` | plan, judge | requirement |
| `scope` | `{in[string], out[ScopeOut]}` | yes | In scope, and parked or excluded scope kept so nobody re-raises it | reconcile, `rule` | author, plan, report section 8 | requirement |
| `deviations[]` | `{id, text, reason, decision}` | no | Deliberate departures from a source | `rule` | plan, report | requirement |
| `milestones[]` | `{id, name, goal, checkpoint}` | no | Observable goals; a `walkthrough` checkpoint applies to the milestone's last increment | combine, `rule` | `next`, report | meta |
| `repos` | map of key to Repo | yes | N named repos: what each is and which it consumes. How to build and check them lives in the registry (C-058) | setup | author, verify, P11, merge order, every build stage | meta |
| `delivery` | Delivery | yes | Branch strategy, ticketing, merge policy, state branch | setup, `rule` | lifecycle stages | requirement (ruled policy) |
| `combination` | Combination | yes once pass 2 has run | Rules, ceiling, declined pairs, basis hash | combine | report section 5, re-combine | meta |
| `conflicts[]` | Conflict | yes | Every disagreement between sources | reconcile, panel | report section 7 | requirement |
| `assumptions[]` | Assumption | yes | Defaults the build proceeds on | reconcile, question editor | plan, acceptance, report section 2 | requirement |
| `questions[]` | Question (3.6) | yes | Everything a person must or may decide | reconcile, panel, critic, question editor, judge (through `discover`) | `next`, report section 1 | requirement |
| `decisions[]` | Decision (3.7) | yes | Append-only ruling ledger | `rule` only | everyone | requirement |
| `requirements[]` | Requirement (3.4) | yes | Pass 1 atoms | `ingest --atoms` | combine, `show --brief`, report | requirement |
| `increments[]` | Increment (3.5) | yes | Pass 2 buildable units | `ingest --increments` | implementor | requirement and row status |

Not in the header, on purpose: executor, model tiers, review cap, CI fix attempts, the local E2E knob, where the integration proof comes from (`quality.integrationProofSource`, C-035), commit trailer, workflow args, plans, tickets, branches, commits and PRs. They live in `build/run.json` and `build/state.json`. Nor are repo how-facts: they live in the registry.

**Repo** `{path, role, consumes[]}`, keyed by repo key (C-058):

- `path` is workspace-relative, never absolute. It is `.` for the workspace repo.
- `role` is `product`, `tests`, `workspace` or `tooling`.
- `consumes[]` lists the repo keys this repo calls at run time (C-026). For example `plantsFrontend` consumes `plantsBackend` and `referenceData`, and `tests` consumes every product repo. It describes the system and never assigns work. Setup fills it from the `docker/stack` service dependencies, and a person confirms it. `check` refuses a cycle. Merge order and P11's provider-to-consumer chain check derive from it.

**The repo's registry entry** (`tools/backlog/registry.json`, read by `stage prepare`, never part of the backlog) holds the how-facts that used to sit here (C-058):

- `github`, the remote the lifecycle stages push to.
- `ladder[]`, rungs `{dir, tool, name}`, where `tool` is `npm`, `mvn` or `script`, and `name` is a script name, a goal name or the path of a committed script under `tools/` or `scripts/`, never a command line. A `script` rung names only a committed file, and `tim backlog rung` refuses one that is uncommitted or differs from HEAD (C-067). tim expands a rung into `npm --prefix <repo>/<dir> run <name>`, `mvn -f <repo>/<dir>/pom.xml <name>` or `<workspace>/<name>` with no arguments and runs it in-process with its fact record (C-075, C-082); the forms stay ones a person can run by hand. A long rung may declare `shards`. A rung that cannot run beside another repo's rungs (a shared port, a shared Docker resource) declares `exclusive: true`, and tim runs it alone.
- `knowledge[]`, repo-owned how-to documents the planner reads, such as a frontend repo's recipe docs or `docs/knowledge/journey-frontends.md`. For this programme it lists `workareas/shared/requirements-pipeline/design/DESIGN.md`, the programme's knowledge.
- `generated[]`, globs of generated files the reviewers skip, mechanically, and the programme's own state paths, which fingerprints and manifests exclude.

**Delivery** `{branchStrategy, branch, base, stateBranch, ticketing, merge, draftPrs}`, holding branches, PRs and merges only (C-035):

- `branchStrategy` is `programme`, `per-increment` or `local`.
- `branch` names the programme branch (programme strategy only). It is the same name in every repo (CLAUDE.md rule 2).
- `stateBranch` is the workspace branch that state commits go to. The build refuses to start when the workspace checkout is on another branch, and it never commits to `main`.
- `ticketing` is `null` or `{project, epic, board, inProgress, done}`.
- `merge` is `{policy: never | on-approval | on-green, decision, order}`. `on-green` needs `decision` to name a decision made by a person. By default the merge order is the topological order of `consumes`, providers first, so the tests repo, which consumes every product repo, merges last; `order: [[provider, consumer]]` overrides it only by a ruling (C-026).
- `draftPrs` is a boolean.
- `crossRepoE2e` has left the backlog. Where the integration proof runs, locally or in CI, is a run choice in `build/run.json` (C-035).

**Combination** `{rules[{id, text}], ceiling{acceptance, newSurfaces, e2eJourneys}, declined[{members[key], rule, why}], basis{atomsSha}}`. The default ceiling is 12 acceptance criteria, 1 new user-facing surface and 2 end-to-end journeys. There is no repo ceiling: an increment spans every repo its behaviour needs (R7), so a repo count may never split a slice. For the same reason repos never justify a combination either: `check --strict` refuses a rule keyed on repos, and a group or a declined pair may not rest on a shared repo set, a layer difference or a shared implementation component, only on behaviour (C-027).

### 3.4 Requirement: a pass 1 atom

| Field | Type | Req | Meaning | Written by | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | `req-NNN` | yes | Stable handle, assigned at first ingest, never renumbered (`ingest.js:310-328`) | ingest | meta |
| `key` | `<slice>--<slug>` | yes | Identity: the authored file name. Cross-references are written as keys and resolved to ids in the same pass | author | meta |
| `slice` | slice id | yes | The one owning slice | author | meta |
| `kind` | `capability`, `rule`, `data`, `content`, `integration`, `reference-data`, `non-functional`, `hygiene`, `constraint`, `spike` | yes | Requirement kind, never an implementation route. Every kind is defined by who observes it (C-029, below) | author | requirement |
| `title` | string, 80 characters or fewer | yes | Outcome headline; a solo increment inherits it | author | requirement |
| `statement` | string | yes | One requirement in MUST form. Frozen at first adopted ingest, not at migrate: the oracle every later rewrite is checked against, as parity's `detail` is (`ingest.js:561-582`). A later change is a variant atom, never an edit (C-055) | author, verify | requirement |
| `why` | string | yes | The need or rule it serves | author | requirement |
| `actor` | string | no | Who relies on it | author | requirement |
| `acceptance[]` | Criterion | yes, at least one | Observable at a boundary (3.12) | author, verify | requirement |
| `falsifiedBy` | string | yes | The single observation that would prove the atom wrong (`FINDING-CONTRACT.template.md:149-152`) | author | requirement |
| `sources[]` | Citation | yes, at least one unless every criterion is `gap` | Verbatim or gap: "a requirement with no citation is not a requirement" (`trace-to-requirements.workflow.js:168-179`) | author, verify; `readAt` filled by ingest from the cited unit | requirement (provenance) |
| `confidence` | `observed`, `stated`, `legacy`, `inferred`, `gap` | yes | The weakest criterion confidence, derived | ingest | meta |
| `surface` | `{service, area, repos[repoKey], amendments[]}` | yes | Where, never how. `repos` is every repo the behaviour needs, including the tests repo for a product behaviour; it follows from the behaviour and never divides it (R7). The author's brief lists the header repos with role and `consumes` (C-030). When a plan adds a repo, a recorded surface-change write (`rule --subject surface:<atom> --add-repo <key> --why <text> --by build`) appends `{repoKey, increment, attempt, why}` to `amendments[]` and the repo to `repos` (C-031). The plan's file list never writes the backlog | author; `rule --by build` | requirement |
| `consistentWith[]` | `{ref, relation, divergence}` | no | A behaviour to match, with `relation` one of `same-as`, `variant-of`, `unlike`. `ref` names behaviour, never a file (F K16) | author, reconcile | requirement |
| `constraints[]` | `inv-*` ids | no | Invariants binding this atom beyond the programme-wide ones | reconcile | requirement |
| `outOfScope[]` | string | no | Tempting neighbours this atom does not cover | author | requirement |
| `needs[]` | `q-*` ids | yes, may be empty | Blocking questions that must be ruled first | reconcile, verify, `rule` | requirement |
| `assumptions[]`, `decisions[]`, `conflicts[]` | ids | no | Pointers into the ledgers; the answers live there (`panel/README.md:3-6`) | reconcile, `rule` | requirement |
| `dependsOn[]` | `req-*` ids | yes, may be empty | Genuine requirement-level dependency | author, reconcile | meta |
| `edgeChecks[]` | `{on, observableWithout, why, by}` | yes for every cross-repo `dependsOn` edge | "Can this atom's acceptance be observed at its boundary before `on` lands?" P11 reads it (3.12, C-025). `by` is a task identity `{phase, task, run}`, the same shape as `provenance.authoredBy`; there is no string form, and `check --strict` refuses one. A record written outside a registered run, such as this programme's design-phase records, carries `{phase: "design", task: <author>, run: null}`, and inc-010's registered verify rewrites it with its own identity | `REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER` | meta |
| `relatedTo[]` | `req-*` ids | no | Related, not ordered | author | meta |
| `specRefs[]` | OpenSpec `REQ-` and `SCN-` ids | no | Links to the Behaviour Spec (F P11) | author | requirement |
| `variantOf` | `q-*` or `c-*` id, or null | no | Wording held ready for one option of a question, or for the losing side of a conflict, so a ruling or a reversal can adopt it without an agent editing anything. A variant belongs to no increment until it is adopted | reconcile, panel, question editor | meta |
| `status` | `proposed`, `adopted`, `disputed`, `parked`, `rejected`, `superseded` | yes | Atom lifecycle (3.9) | ingest, `verify-record`, `rule` | state (requirement-level) |
| `supersededBy` | `req-*` or null | no | Set when dedupe or a ruling replaces this atom | dedupe, `rule` | meta |
| `carriedFrom[]` | strings | no | Lineage from carryover or migration. A resliced atom names every legacy row it absorbs, such as one frontend, one backend and one tests row (C-034) | carryover, `migrate`, reslice | meta |
| `provenance` | Provenance | yes | Who authored and who verified it, stamped by tim from task identity | `stage accept`, `verify-record` | meta |

**Citation** `{id, source, ref, quote, readAt, confidence, role}`:

- `ref` is a provenance token from the source's scheme, such as `annex:5/reg:24A(1)(b)`, `EUDPA-123#comment:10442` or `frontend:src/.../schema.js:12-30`.
- `quote` is verbatim. It is `null` only when the source's `retention` is `local` (Q15, S5).
- `readAt` is `{sha, blob, dirty}` for code and `{version, fetchedAt, seal}` otherwise, filled from the cited unit (G3 §4.1).
- `role` is copied from the source.
- A citation whose role is `constraint`, `consistency-anchor` or `current-behaviour` is labelled wherever it reaches a task: `show --brief` and `brief.json` print it as "evidence of current behaviour, not a design to copy". The label is derived from `role`, never stored. The plan audit objects when a plan adopts a cited file as a template without a stated decision (C-063).

**Kinds, by who observes them** (C-029):

- `capability`, `rule` and `content` are behaviour a user or operator observes.
- `data` is a persisted or published shape that a system outside the programme, or an operator, observes at a boundary. A store, collection, endpoint or list that only the programme's own frontend reads is not a `data` atom: the verifier merges it into the behaviour that observes it.
- `reference-data` is values a user sees or a rule applies, observed through that behaviour.
- `integration` is an exchange with a system outside the programme's repos.
- `non-functional`, `constraint`, `hygiene` and `spike` keep their meanings; `hygiene` never adds behaviour (C-032).

**Criterion** `{id, text or given/when/then, witness, confidence, sources[citationId], scenario, resolvedBy}`:

- `witness` is `e2e`, `fit`, `unit`, `contract`, `review` or `observation`. It is the level of proof expected, never a command.
- `scenario` is an OpenSpec `SCN-` id or null.
- `resolvedBy` is the decision that fixed the criterion's wording, or null.

**Provenance** `{authoredBy, verifiedBy, verdict, why, correction, searchedFor}`:

- `authoredBy` and `verifiedBy` are each `{phase, task, run}`. `verifiedBy` can also be `{phase: "ruling", decision}`, or `null` while the atom is `proposed`.
- `verdict` is one of `unverified`, `stands`, `corrected`, `disputed`, `added`, `carried` or `observed`.
- The writer refuses a `verifiedBy` whose `task` equals `authoredBy.task`, or whose `run` is not registered in `distil/phases.json` or `build/state.json` (the judge 3 guard). A placeholder such as "design-panel, pending panel review" can never pass.

### 3.5 Increment: a pass 2 buildable unit

| Field | Type | Req | Meaning | Written by | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | `inc-NNN` | yes | Stable handle | ingest | meta |
| `key` | slug | yes | Identity: `distil/increments/<key>.json`, or `solo--<root atom key>` for an atom nobody grouped, where the root is the member resolved back through `supersededBy`, so a ruling's variant never changes the key (C-054) | combiner, ingest | meta |
| `title` | string, 80 characters or fewer | yes for a combined increment; absent on a solo | Outcome headline. A solo increment stores none: its title is rendered from its member's title at read time, so it cannot drift (C-062) | combiner | requirement |
| `outcome` | string | yes for a combined increment; absent on a solo | What is true when done, for whom, in one to three sentences. A solo increment stores none: its outcome is rendered from its member's statement at read time, so it cannot drift (C-062) | combiner | requirement |
| `why` | string | yes | Why these members ship together, or the atom's why for a solo | combiner | requirement |
| `members[]` | `req-*` ids | yes, at least one | The atoms this increment satisfies. Acceptance is always read through the members, never copied | combiner, ingest resolves | requirement |
| `class` | `feat`, `fix`, `chore`, `refactor`, `test`, `docs` | yes | Change class: the branch prefix and default commit type, derived from member kinds, overridable by `rule`. `test` only when every member is `hygiene` (C-025) | combiner | meta |
| `surface` | `{repos[], areas[]}` | yes | The union of the members' surfaces | ingest (derived) | meta |
| `milestone` | milestone id or null | yes | | combiner | meta |
| `dependsOn[]` | `inc-*` ids | yes, may be empty | Member dependencies lifted to increments, plus explicit ordering. Cycles refused | ingest | meta |
| `edgeChecks[]` | `{on, observableWithout, why, by}` | yes for every cross-repo edge | The atom `edgeChecks` shape, for lifted edges (C-025) | `COMBINE_VERIFIER` | meta |
| `sequence` | `{after[inc], why}` or null | no | An ordering preference that is not a dependency, used only as a tie-break (G5 §8, DR1-U inc-103) | combiner, `rule` | meta |
| `needs[]` | `q-*` ids | yes, may be empty | The union of the members' open `needs`; recomputed on every ingest and ruling | ingest, `rule` | state (derived) |
| `checkpoint` | null, `halt-after`, `walkthrough` | yes | Post-build stop | combiner, `rule` | meta |
| `size` | `{class: S, M or L, basis}` | yes | Requirement-level proxies (criteria, surfaces, repos). Never gates building | combiner, ingest | meta |
| `combination` | null (solo) or `{rules[], why, risk, attributability, reverseEffects[]}` | yes | Why grouped, citing candidate signal ids about behaviour, never repos or components (C-027), and whether a red in one member would be misattributed to another. `reverseEffects` are predeclared, so Sam can split the group with one command (C-047) | combiner | meta |
| `status` | `todo`, `blocked`, `deferred`, `dropped`, `done`, `superseded` | yes | Row status (3.9) | ingest, `rule`, `status` | state (requirement-level) |
| `doneBy` | null, `build`, `ruling` | yes | Separates a built `done` from a decision-only `done` (EUDPA-409 inc-060, C §5.2) | `status`, `rule` | state |
| `statusNote` | string or null | yes | Why the row has its status, such as the decision that deferred it | `status`, `rule` | state |
| `supersededBy[]` | `inc-*` ids | no | Set when a re-combine dissolves this increment before it started | ingest | meta |

### 3.6 Question

This is the only shape for a thing a person must or may decide. It replaces `openQuestions[]`, `openQuestion`, `blockedQuestion`, integer `question` numbers and `decisionRequired` (E §6.11).

```jsonc
{
  "id": "q-notification-list-scope",              // slug: Sam answers by it; never renumbered
  "headline": "Should a user see only their own organisation's notifications?",
  "question": "The dashboard lists notifications. Which notifications may a signed-in user see?",
  "inPlay": "The dashboard list and the notification view, in the plants frontend and backend.",
  "options": [
    { "id": "A", "text": "Only notifications created under the user's current organisation.",
      "consequence": "Colleagues in one organisation see each other's work; nobody sees another organisation's.",
      "sources": ["s-policy-14", "s-conf-31"],
      "counts": { "requirement": 2 },              // derived by role; printed "2 requirement sources (tier 1, tier 2)"
      "effects": [ { "op": "supersede", "target": "req-040", "by": "req-041" },
                   { "op": "supersede", "target": "req-044", "by": "req-045" },
                   { "op": "reject", "target": "req-042" } ] },
    { "id": "B", "text": "Only notifications the user personally created, across organisations.",
      "consequence": "An agent working for two importers sees both; colleagues see nothing of each other's.",
      "sources": ["s-transcript-7"],
      "counts": { "requirement": 1 },
      "effects": [ { "op": "supersede", "target": "req-040", "by": "req-042" },
                   { "op": "supersede", "target": "req-044", "by": "req-046" },
                   { "op": "reject", "target": "req-041" } ] }
  ],
  "recommended": "A",
  "recommendedBy": "panel",                        // panel | reconciler | judge | null
  "ifNobodyAnswers": { "option": null,             // null: must be answered, and it blocks
    "consequence": "Nothing that lists notifications is built. One increment stays blocked.",
    "defaultWhy": null },                          // required when a default differs from "recommended"
  "category": "access-control",                    // scope | policy | security | access-control | data-integrity
                                                   // | legal | design | content | technical | process
  "class": "must-answer",                          // derived: must-answer | defaulted
  "mustAnswer": true,                              // derived: ifNobodyAnswers.option is null
  "audience": "sam",
  "cluster": "access-and-ownership",
  "blastRadius": { "blocks": 1,                    // derived from rows whose needs name the question
                   "restsOnDefault": 0,            // derived from rows citing the default's assumption
                   "reversalCost": "high",
                   "why": "A wrong scope leaks data between organisations." },
  "meanwhile": "Every other dashboard increment stays buildable.",
  "visual": [ { "source": "mural", "unit": "file:dashboard/region:List/item:1",
                "crop": "distil/sources/mural/crops/list.png", "box": "120,340,880,420",
                "sha256": "sha256:3b7e..." } ],     // written by tim backlog source crop (C-039)
  "raisedBy": { "phase": "reconcile", "task": "reconcile", "run": "distil-plants-2026-09-18a" },
  "lint": { "passed": true, "task": "question-critic", "run": "distil-plants-2026-09-18a", "failures": [] },
  "status": "open",                                // open | outstanding | ruled | superseded | withdrawn
  "decision": null                                 // d-NNN once ruled
}
```

- `blastRadius.blocks` is derived by tim from the rows whose `needs` name the question, and `blastRadius.restsOnDefault` from the rows whose `assumptions` cite its default. Both are printed; neither is ever typed (C-052). The derivation is exact:
  - `blocks` is the number of increments whose `needs` name the question.
  - `restsOnDefault` is the number of atoms whose `assumptions` name the assumption that the question's default sets (the assumption whose `question` is this question). Every such atom counts whatever its status, including a `parked` one, because a ruling can bring it back. Increments are not counted: an increment rests on a default only through its members, so counting both would count one thing twice. A must-answer question has no default and `restsOnDefault: 0`.
- **Two behaviours only** (C-044). A question either must be answered, or has a default that is applied. "Blocking with a default" does not exist.
  - `must-answer`: `ifNobodyAnswers.option` is `null`, and the question blocks every row whose `needs` name it until a person rules. A question whose category is security, access-control, data-integrity, legal or policy is always `must-answer`.
  - `defaulted`: any other question. Its default is applied at once by `tim backlog rule --by default`, which writes a decision with status `defaulted` and runs that option's effects. The report lists it in section 2 with "To reverse", and keeps the question in section 1 under "Has a default in force" until someone rules.
- **Every option that changes behaviour carries effects that make the change.** A new behaviour supersedes to a variant atom whose acceptance encodes the option. Taking up, parking or ruling out an atom that already exists uses `adopt`, `park` or `reject`; holding an increment back or ruling it out uses `defer-increment` or `drop-increment`. Each option of a question also sets that question's assumption, if it has one, to its own letter. An option whose only effect is `set-assumption` is allowed only when the current atoms already encode it. An option that changes behaviour with none of these effects is refused. Two options of one question with identical effects are refused, and the default's effects must agree with its consequence text (checked by `QUESTION_CRITIC`).
- **Counts are by role.** An option's `counts` is derived from its sources by role ("2 requirement sources, 1 current behaviour"), and each source is printed with its precedence tier, so a tier-1 requirement source is never added to current-behaviour code (C-050).
- **Visual evidence.** A question in a visual cluster carries a crop per unit, with its box and hash; the question lint fails one that has none (C-039).
- **"Awaiting a ruling"** has exactly one definition: the question's status is `open` or `outstanding` (fixes the three definitions in G5 §2).

### 3.7 Decision: the ledger

```jsonc
{
  "id": "d-017",                                   // append order; never reused
  "question": "q-notification-list-scope",         // or null with "subject": {"kind": "conflict", "id": "c-012"}
  "subject": null,
  "chosen": "A",                                   // option id, or null with "answer" for free text
  "answer": null,
  "words": "Own org only. Obviously.",             // the ruler's words, verbatim
  "note": "Security: a cross-organisation list is a data-isolation failure.",   // mandatory
  "decidedBy": "sam",                              // sam | panel | precedence | default | build | <named person Sam delegated to>
  "decidedAt": "2026-09-18T14:02:00Z",             // passed by the caller; tim never reads the clock for this
  "sealedEvidence": [ { "source": "phnns-policy", "seal": "sha256:9f1c..." } ],
  "appliesTo": ["req-040", "req-044"],             // a group ruling names every id it covers
  "constraints": [],                               // ordering constraints that are not dependencies
  "revisitWhen": null,                             // for example "when the typed record lands"
  "effects": [ { "op": "supersede", "target": "req-040", "by": "req-041", "prior": "adopted" },   // prior: written by rule
               { "op": "supersede", "target": "req-044", "by": "req-045", "prior": "adopted" },
               { "op": "reject", "target": "req-042", "prior": "proposed" } ],                // a superseding ruling restores these first
  "supersedes": null, "supersededBy": null,
  "reopens": false,                                // true only when an effect touches a started or done increment
  "status": "current"                              // current | superseded | tentative | defaulted
}
```

- **Subjects.** A decision names either a `question` or a `subject`, never both. A subject is `{kind, id}`, and the kinds are exactly: `conflict` (a precedence or panel pick, or its reversal with `--choose <side>`), `combination` (a group's `--split`), `surface` (a build's `--add-repo`), and `design` (the adoption of a design document as the programme's requirement, `id` being its path in the workarea; it carries `appliesTo` and no effects). `check --strict` refuses any other kind.
- **Effects** are the machine-applicable part of a ruling: `adopt`, `park`, `reject`, `supersede` (by an existing variant atom), `clear-need`, `set-assumption`, `add-invariant`, `drop-increment`, `defer-increment`, `strip-dependency`, `set-delivery` and `split` (a combination's reverse effect, which pins the named member groups apart for every later re-combine). Each effect is `{op, target}`, plus `by` for `supersede`, `value` for `set-assumption` and `set-delivery`, and `into` for `split`. On a decision, each applied effect also carries `prior`, written by `rule` (below); an option's predeclared effects never do.
- `tim backlog rule` records and applies a ruling in one write, as `spec-add-decision.sh` does (`:2-5`). It recomputes `needs` and statuses, strips dependencies on dropped increments with a note on each dependant (`rule-decision.sh:73-76`), and resets the `combine` phase so the report cannot go stale.
- A free-text answer with no predeclared effects is recorded with `status: current`, and the question is flagged `effects-pending`. The RULING_APPLIER task then derives the effects (5.8).
- A tentative answer ("maybe B?") is recorded with `status: tentative`. The question moves to `outstanding`, and nothing is applied (memory `feedback_question_is_not_a_ruling`).
- A changed ruling is a new entry with `supersedes`, never an edit (`journey-builder/SKILL.md:75-79`).
- **Superseding a decision reverts its effects first.** When `rule` applies an effect it writes the target's state just before, as `prior` on that effect: an atom's or increment's status, or an assumption's value (`null` when unset). A ruling that supersedes a decision, whether a person's ruling over a default or a changed ruling, first restores every target of the superseded decision to its `prior`, in reverse order, and only then applies its own effects. So ruling B over a default of A undoes A's `defer-increment`, `drop-increment`, `park`, `reject`, `supersede` and `set-assumption`, and nothing A deferred stays deferred by accident. There is no separate inverse effect to write or forget. A restore is refused when its target has since moved on for another reason (a later decision touched it, or it is started or done); the ruling then needs `reopens: true` (below) or an explicit effect for that target, and `rule` names the target. `check --strict` refuses an applied effect with no `prior`.
- A default applied by `rule --by default` is a decision with `decidedBy: default` and `status: defaulted`. It runs its effects like any ruling, and a later ruling by a person supersedes it, reverting the default's effects first as above, so a default is always reversible by one ruling (C-044). `words` is the ruler's own words and is always exempt from retention (C-045).
- **A ruling that touches built work re-opens it explicitly** (C-055). `rule` refuses an effect on a member of a started or done increment unless the decision carries `reopens: true`. Then it creates a variant atom and a follow-up solo increment that depends on the done one, and leaves the done increment and its `doneBy` untouched.
- **Reversals are commands.** Conflicts and combinations carry predeclared `reverseEffects`, so a call Sam may reverse is one paste-ready command: `rule --subject conflict:<id> --choose <side>` or `rule --subject combination:<key> --split` (C-047).

### 3.8 Source, conflict, assumption, invariant, scope and deviation

```jsonc
// Source (header.sources[])
{ "id": "phnns-policy", "kind": "docx", "role": "requirement", "tier": 1,
  "title": "Plant health notification policy paper",
  "locator": "distil/sources/phnns-policy/raw/policy.docx", "version": "v1 August 2026",
  "readAt": { "fetchedAt": "2026-09-18T09:10:00Z", "seal": "sha256:9f1c..." },
  "retention": "tracked",                          // tracked | local (quotes kept out of git); keyed on the speaker
  "provenanceScheme": "section:<slug>/para:<n>/item:<k> | annex:<n>/reg:<ref>",
  "characterisation": "distil/sources/phnns-policy/characterisation.md",
  "units": 212, "status": "verified", "absence": null }
// kind: brief | confluence | jira | docx | pdf | markdown | spreadsheet | slides | image-board | design
//       | repo | code | trace-corpus | transcript | chat | email | prototype | live-service | openspec
//       | existing-backlog | parity-corpus | web
//   brief: the requester's own instructions (tracked, confidence stated, role requirement) (C-045)
//   spreadsheet: xlsx or csv, cited by sheet, row and column ; slides: pptx (C-040)
//   live-service: a URL plus a capture, the source of observed behaviour (C-040)
// role: requirement | constraint | consistency-anchor | current-behaviour | decision-record
// status: registered | characterised | verified | stale

// Conflict
{ "id": "c-004", "topic": "Country list for ware potatoes", "factKind": "values",
  "sides": [ { "source": "phnns-policy", "ref": "annex:5/reg:24A(1)(b)", "says": "Four countries." },
             { "source": "confluence-requirements", "ref": "h4:Origin/li:3", "says": "Five countries, adding Cyprus." } ],
  "resolution": { "by": "precedence", "decision": null,
                  "pick": "The policy paper wins on values (precedence 'values')." },
  "reverseEffects": { "confluence-requirements": [ { "op": "supersede", "target": "req-022", "by": "req-023" } ] },
  "reverseNotes": {},                              // why a losing side's list is empty; required for every empty list
  "question": null, "affects": ["req-022"] }         // reverseEffects: per losing side, predeclared (C-047)

// Assumption
{ "id": "as-error-summary-title", "text": "The error summary title is the GOV.UK default.",
  "default": "There is a problem", "confidence": "inferred",
  "reviseIf": "a content designer rules otherwise", "question": "q-error-summary-title", "decision": null }

// Invariant (header.invariants[])
{ "id": "inv-copy-bilingual", "text": "Every user-facing string exists in English and Welsh.",
  "why": "Welsh Language Standards", "sources": ["standards-note"], "decision": "d-002" }

// Scope exclusion (header.scope.out[]) and deviation (header.deviations[])
{ "id": "out-bulk-upload", "text": "Bulk upload of notifications", "why": "Parked by the product owner",
  "decision": "d-003", "sources": ["confluence-user-needs"] }
{ "id": "dv-great-britain", "text": "Great Britain, not England, in every string", "reason": "Territorial scope", "decision": "d-001" }
```

**Retention keys on the speaker, not the source kind** (C-045). The requester's own words are a brief: tracked, and accepted as a requirement source without a ruling. Other people's utterances stay local whatever the container (a transcript, a chat export, an email, a Jira comment); their quote is `null` in git, and a paraphrase is tracked. `decisions[].words` is exempt, as the ruler's own words. Whether third parties' words may ever enter git is a must-answer question, not a default.

**Every conflict carries its reversal** (C-047). `reverseEffects` has one key for every losing side, whatever resolved the conflict (precedence or panel). Where reversing changes what gets built, the list supersedes the affected atom by a variant atom (`variantOf` the conflict) whose acceptance encodes the losing side. Where it cannot, because the losing side is a claim of fact rather than a behaviour, or because it would break one of Sam's standing requirements, the list is empty and `reverseNotes` carries, keyed by the same side, why it is empty and what a reversal would take instead. `check --strict` refuses a conflict with a losing side missing from `reverseEffects`, and an empty list with no note.

**No linked source vanishes** (C-040). A pointer unit on a requirement-role source (a link to a workbook, a SharePoint or OneDrive file, a prototype) must be registered as a source, stated absent with a reason, or raised as a question; P1 fails until it is.

### 3.9 Status enums, and the one buildability rule

**Atom status.**

| Status | Meaning | Set by |
|---|---|---|
| `proposed` | Authored or discovered, not yet verified. Never buildable | ingest (authored or discovered files), migrate (`--from design`) |
| `adopted` | Verified by a different task, or adopted by a ruling; in scope | `stage accept` of a verify task, `verify-record`, `rule` |
| `disputed` | A verifier disputed it; it always carries a question in `needs` | verify, reconcile |
| `parked` | Ruled out of scope for now | `rule`, reconcile with a decision |
| `rejected` | Ruled never | `rule` |
| `superseded` | Absorbed by dedupe, or replaced by a ruling's variant; `supersededBy` names the survivor | dedupe, `rule` |

**Increment status.**

| Status | Meaning | Set by | Buildable |
|---|---|---|---|
| `todo` | Wanted | ingest, `rule` | when the rule below holds |
| `blocked` | Derived: an open must-answer question in `needs`, or a `disputed` member | ingest, `rule` (recomputed) | no |
| `deferred` | Ruled not now; `statusNote` names the decision and `revisitWhen` | `rule` | no |
| `dropped` | Ruled never, or every member parked or rejected; dependants lose the dependency with a note | `rule`, ingest | no |
| `done` | `doneBy: build` means the stage audit passed and the delivery done point was reached. `doneBy: ruling` means a decision resolved it without code | `status`, `rule` | no |
| `superseded` | Dissolved by a re-combine before it started; kept so nothing is deleted | ingest | no |

- **No transition from `done` back to `todo`.** Re-opening built work is always a new follow-up increment created by a decision with `reopens: true` (3.7, C-055).
- **A solo whose member is superseded by a ruling's variant** keeps its status and its id; only the member is swapped, because the key is built from the root atom (C-054).
- **An increment whose integration proof is owed is not `done`.** It sits in `build/state.json` under the hold `proof-owed`, so rule 4 below keeps its dependants unbuildable; independent increments continue (C-035).

**The one buildability rule** (`tim backlog next`). The next increment is the first one in array order for which every one of these holds:

1. its `status` is `todo`;
2. every member is `adopted`;
3. `needs` is empty;
4. every `dependsOn` increment is `done`;
5. `build/state.json` holds no hold for it.

One priority applies first: a `todo` increment named by an existing hold's `releasedBy` (a hygiene increment created by a red baseline) comes ahead of everything. When nothing is buildable, `next` names every reason: all done, the blocking questions, the holds with their release commands, the unverified atoms, and the unmet dependencies. An unknown status cannot occur, because the schema refuses it at write time. So neither the allow-list failure nor the deny-list failure seen today can happen (C §5.1).

**Question status:** `open`, `outstanding` (a tentative answer is recorded), `ruled`, `superseded`, `withdrawn`. **Decision status:** `current`, `superseded`, `tentative`, `defaulted`.

**Done point per delivery strategy:**

| Strategy | Done point |
|---|---|
| `per-increment` | The increment's PRs are merged. Under `merge: never` the increment pauses at `awaiting-merge`, a healthy stop, and becomes `done` when a later run sees its PRs merged (the loop's `resumeAt`, `increment-build-loop.js:938-946`) |
| `programme` | The increment has landed on the programme branch and its CI is green |
| `local` | The increment has landed as a local commit |

### 3.10 `needs`, `checkpoint`, and the end of `gate`

- **`needs` is pre-build.** It holds question ids on atoms. Ingest derives each increment's `needs` from its members and sets `blocked`. `tim backlog rule` clears the need and recomputes the status in the same write. There is no two-field protocol (E §6.4).
- **`checkpoint` is post-build.** `halt-after` stops the drain after the increment lands. `walkthrough` stops it and lists a walkthrough in report section 1. A milestone's `walkthrough` applies to its last increment.
- **`gate` does not exist in v2.** The `parity-v1` profile keeps it for frozen corpora.
- **When a question blocks.** Only `must-answer` questions enter `needs` (C-044). A `defaulted` question never blocks: `tim backlog rule --by default` applies its default as a `defaulted` decision, runs its effects (variant atoms adopted, needs cleared, statuses recomputed) and records the assumption the atoms cite. It stays in report section 1 under "Has a default in force", and section 2 lists the call with its "To reverse" command. This stops the CHED-P pattern of 74 of 108 rows blocked on questions that had safe defaults (F P5).

### 3.11 Ids and keys

- **Identity is the authored file key.** The handle (`req-NNN`, `inc-NNN`) is assigned once and never renumbered (`ingest.js:310-328`). A key can be corrected without moving the handle that rulings, commits and citations attach to (G2 §1.2).
- **Other ids.** Questions use `q-<slug>` because Sam answers by them. Decisions are append-ordered `d-NNN` and conflicts `c-NNN`. Assumptions (`as-`), invariants (`inv-`), scope exclusions (`out-`), deviations (`dv-`), milestones (`m<n>`) and sources are slugs.
- **Cross-references.** Every cross-reference in an authored file (`dependsOn`, `relatedTo`, `members`, `needs`, `constraints`, `assumptions`) is written as a key and resolved in the same pass, so forward references in one batch work (`ingest.js:339-342`). A dangling or self reference is refused, naming the file and the key (`ingest.js:361-388`).
- **Cycles.** A depth-first search over atom `dependsOn`, then over increment `dependsOn`, refuses a cycle and names every key in it.
- **Lifted dependencies.** A combined increment's members' dependencies that point inside the group are dropped. Those that point outside are lifted to the owning increments.

### 3.12 Atoms, combined items, acceptance, provenance and confidence

- Every `adopted` atom is a member of **exactly one** increment. `tim backlog check --strict` proves it with the set algebra of `slices.js:121-145`.
