- An atom nobody grouped gets a solo increment keyed `solo--<root atom key>`: each member is resolved through `supersededBy` to its root, so a variant a ruling adopts never changes the key or the id (C-054). Combined groups match across re-combines by the set of member roots. When a later combine groups it, the solo increment becomes `superseded` if it has not started. If it has started, ingest refuses and names it (`ruled()`, `ingest.js:454-461`).
- A combined increment's acceptance is its members' acceptance, each criterion keeping its sources. Nothing is copied, so a ruling that changes a member is seen at once (judge 1).
- `proposed`, `parked`, `rejected` and `superseded` atoms are members of nothing. The report shows them in section 8 "so nobody re-raises them" (`page.js:368-377`).

**What acceptance may name** (S §5.3): anything a user, operator, downstream system or reviewer can observe at a boundary. That includes rendered copy, options and error text. It includes a route only when an external contract fixes it, a persisted or published document shape only when another system consumes it, and a GDS component only when a design source mandates it.

**What acceptance must not name:** files, functions, classes, annotations, test file names, CSS classes or commands. `tim backlog check` reports any that do. The report is overridden only by a decision or deviation that names an **enumerated contract**: a route, a document shape, or, for a tooling programme, an interface such as CLI verbs and flags, documented exit codes, skill trigger phrases and stop reasons. The lint's allow-list is driven from that enumeration, never switched off; file paths stay refused (C-057).

**Confidence** (F §7) is independent of source kind:

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `observed` | Seen happening in a running system |
| `stated` | Written in a requirement-role source, or a ruling by a person |
| `legacy` | Read from the old system's source: trustworthy for values and copy, not for policy |
| `inferred` | Deduced |
| `gap` | Believed to exist with no evidence. It must become a question or an assumption |

**The writer's role checks** (judges 1 and 3):

- `stated` needs a requirement-role source or a decision.
- `observed` needs a `prototype`, `trace-corpus` or `live-service` source (C-040).
- `legacy` needs a legacy or current-behaviour source.
- An atom whose evidence comes only from `constraint`, `consistency-anchor` or `current-behaviour` sources is refused, unless its kind is `hygiene` or a decision adopts it. The check applies to variant atoms (`variantOf`) as well, whether or not a ruling has swapped them in, so a variant resting only on such sources is listed in the adopting decision's `appliesTo` like any other atom. This is the structural defence against existing code or a sibling service turning into requirements (F K3).

**Vertical slices** (R7). A layer split is the pattern R7 bans: "an increment for the backend, then one for the frontend, then one for the tests". `tim backlog check --strict` refuses it as proof P11, and ingest runs the same checks.

| Check | Refused when | Exception |
|---|---|---|
| Tests-only | An atom or increment's repos are all `tests`-role repos | kind `hygiene`, such as a flaky-test fix nobody's behaviour depends on |
| Layer chain | Any of the three rules below (C-025). They apply to atom edges and to lifted increment edges. A row's product repo set is its `surface.repos` filtered to role `product`; the tests repo is left out, because it sits on every product row and would otherwise make every pair of rows overlap | a decision that names the edge and says why the two are different behaviours |
| No integration proof | An increment touches a `product` repo, but no member criterion has witness `e2e` or `contract`, or the programme's `tests`-role repo is missing from its surface. Or a `feat` or `fix` increment whose repos are all `workspace` or `tooling` has no member criterion witnessed `e2e` that runs the tool's real entry point (C-033) | a `hygiene` increment in a product repo needs a regression proof instead (C-032): the rung that was red is green and, when the stack serves the repo, the existing end-to-end suite is green on the slice's stack. It does not need the tests repo, and it is the only single-repo product increment P11 allows. `docs`, `chore` and `hygiene` tooling increments are exempt from the tooling rule |
| Proof or documentation only | An atom only proves, tests or documents another atom's behaviour | none: the verifier merges it into the atom it proves, and combine's `cr-no-layer-split` rule (5.7) catches any that survive |

**The layer-chain rules** (C-025):

1. **Disjoint product sets.** Refuse any `dependsOn` edge between two rows whose product repo sets are both non-empty and disjoint, whatever their areas. A frontend-and-tests row depending on a backend-and-tests row for the same behaviour is the case in point.
2. **Chains.** Refuse any dependency path of two or more edges that steps from provider to consumer (by `consumes`, 3.3) or ends in a tests-only row.
3. **Every other cross-repo edge needs an `edgeChecks` record** `{on, observableWithout, why, by}`: can the dependant's acceptance be observed at its boundary before the dependency lands? `check --strict` refuses an edge with no record, and refuses one whose dependency is provider-only, cannot be observed by its own actor, and has `observableWithout: false`.
4. The exception stays: a decision that names the edge.
5. Increment class `test` is allowed only when every member is `hygiene`.

For a programme with no product repo, such as this one, "the real stack" is the tool's real entry point. The integration proof is then an `e2e` criterion, met by spawning the CLI or running the workflow or skill end to end against a fixture programme, and the stage audit records that run's exit code and output as the proof (C-033).

### 3.13 Worked examples

**Example 1: an atomic (solo) increment.**

```jsonc
// backlog.json requirements[] entry (authored as distil/atoms/origin--ware-potato-countries.json)
{
  "id": "req-022", "key": "origin--ware-potato-countries", "slice": "origin", "kind": "rule",
  "title": "Ware-potato notifications accept only the four permitted countries",
  "statement": "When a notification carries a ware-potato commodity line, the country of origin MUST be Poland, Portugal, Romania or Spain.",
  "why": "Regulation 24A(1)(b) restricts ware potatoes to four member states.",
  "actor": "importer",
  "acceptance": [
    { "id": "ac-1", "given": "a notification carries a ware-potato commodity line",
      "when": "the importer chooses a country outside Poland, Portugal, Romania and Spain",
      "then": "the country is rejected with an error naming those four countries",
      "witness": "e2e", "confidence": "stated", "sources": ["s1"],
      "scenario": "SCN-PLANTS-OB-ORIGIN-003-A", "resolvedBy": null },
    { "id": "ac-2", "given": "a notification carries no ware-potato line",
      "when": "the importer chooses any country on the service's country list",
      "then": "the country is accepted",
      "witness": "fit", "confidence": "inferred", "sources": ["s2"], "scenario": null, "resolvedBy": null }
  ],
  "falsifiedBy": "A ware-potato notification that saves with a country of origin outside the four.",
  "sources": [
    { "id": "s1", "source": "phnns-policy", "ref": "annex:5/reg:24A(1)(b)",
      "quote": "ware potatoes originating in Poland, Portugal, Romania or Spain",
      "readAt": { "version": "v1 August 2026", "fetchedAt": "2026-09-18T09:10:00Z", "seal": "sha256:9f1c..." },
      "confidence": "stated", "role": "requirement" },
    { "id": "s2", "source": "live-animals", "ref": "frontend:origin country list validation@4f1c9e2",
      "quote": "country list validation",
      "readAt": { "sha": "4f1c9e2...", "blob": "a91b...", "dirty": false },
      "confidence": "inferred", "role": "consistency-anchor" }   // briefs print it as "evidence of current
  ],                                                           // behaviour, not a design to copy" (C-063)
  "confidence": "inferred",
  "surface": { "service": "plants", "area": "origin", "repos": ["plantsFrontend", "plantsBackend", "tests"] },
  "consistentWith": [ { "ref": "live-animals: the country-of-origin question", "relation": "variant-of",
                        "divergence": "The list narrows by commodity." } ],
  "constraints": ["inv-copy-bilingual"],
  "outOfScope": ["Checking the destination address territory (d-001)"],
  "needs": [], "dependsOn": ["req-009"], "specRefs": ["REQ-PLANTS-OB-ORIGIN-003"],
  "edgeChecks": [ { "on": "req-009", "observableWithout": true,
                    "why": "The country list exists before the commodity rule narrows it; the rule is observed on its own page.",
                    "by": { "phase": "verify", "task": "verify:origin", "run": "distil-plants-2026-09-18a" } } ],
  "status": "adopted",
  "provenance": {
    "authoredBy": { "phase": "author", "task": "author:origin", "run": "distil-plants-2026-09-18a" },
    "verifiedBy": { "phase": "verify", "task": "verify:origin", "run": "distil-plants-2026-09-18a" },
    "verdict": "corrected", "why": "The policy is silent on non-potato lines.",
    "correction": { "field": "acceptance[ac-2].confidence", "was": "stated" }, "searchedFor": null }
}

// backlog.json increments[] entry, created by ingest because no combined file claimed the atom.
// No stored title or outcome: readers render them from req-022's title and statement (C-062).
// The key uses the root atom (C-054).
{ "id": "inc-014", "key": "solo--origin--ware-potato-countries",
  "why": "Regulation 24A(1)(b) restricts ware potatoes to four member states.",
  "members": ["req-022"], "class": "feat",
  "surface": { "repos": ["plantsFrontend", "plantsBackend", "tests"], "areas": ["origin"] },
  "milestone": "m1", "dependsOn": ["inc-009"], "needs": [], "checkpoint": null,
  "size": { "class": "S", "basis": "2 criteria, 1 area, 3 repos" },
  "combination": null, "status": "todo", "doneBy": null, "statusNote": null }
```

**Example 2: a combined increment.**

```jsonc
// distil/increments/arrival-details.json (the combiner's file; members written as keys)
{ "title": "Importers give the port and expected date of arrival on one page",
  "outcome": "An importer records where and when the consignment arrives, and cannot give a date in the past or a port the service does not recognise.",
  "why": "Three atoms describe the questions an importer answers about arrival on one page, and one journey through that page shows all three.",
  "members": ["arrival--port-of-entry", "arrival--arrival-date", "arrival--date-not-in-past"],
  "class": "feat", "milestone": "m1", "dependsOn": [], "checkpoint": null,
  "size": { "class": "M", "basis": "7 criteria, 1 area, 3 repos, 1 journey" },
  "combination": {
    "rules": ["cr-same-surface", "cr-same-acceptance-boundary"],       // behaviour signals only (C-027)
    "why": "cr-same-surface: all three are answered on the arrival page. cr-same-acceptance-boundary: one journey through that page observes every criterion.",
    "risk": "low",
    "attributability": "A red in the date rule shows in that member's own criteria; it cannot be misread as a port failure.",
    "reverseEffects": [ { "op": "split", "into": [["arrival--port-of-entry"], ["arrival--arrival-date", "arrival--date-not-in-past"]] } ] } }

// header.combination.declined[] records what the combiner considered and left apart
{ "members": ["arrival--port-of-entry", "transport--vehicle-registration"], "rule": "cr-attributability",
  "why": "Transport has its own reference-data dependency; a red there would stall arrival." }
```

After ingest, the increment is `inc-015` with `members` resolved to `req-031`, `req-032` and `req-033`, its `surface` derived as the union, and its `dependsOn` lifted from the members' dependencies.

**Example 3: an increment born blocked with a question.**

```jsonc
// Two members that wait on the same must-answer question, so cr-no-need-boundary allows them together
{ "id": "req-040", "key": "dashboard--list-scope", "slice": "dashboard", "kind": "rule",
  "title": "The dashboard lists only notifications the user may see",
  "statement": "The dashboard MUST list only the notifications the signed-in user may see, under the scope ruled for q-notification-list-scope.",
  "acceptance": [ { "id": "ac-1", "given": "two users in different organisations each with a notification",
    "when": "either user opens their dashboard", "then": "only notifications within that user's scope are listed",
    "witness": "e2e", "confidence": "gap", "sources": [], "scenario": null, "resolvedBy": null } ],
  "needs": ["q-notification-list-scope"], "status": "adopted", "...": "..." }
{ "id": "req-041", "key": "dashboard--list-scope--org", "variantOf": "q-notification-list-scope",
  "statement": "The dashboard MUST list only notifications created under the signed-in user's current organisation.",
  "status": "proposed", "...": "..." }
{ "id": "req-042", "key": "dashboard--list-scope--own", "variantOf": "q-notification-list-scope",
  "statement": "The dashboard MUST list only notifications the signed-in user created, across organisations.",
  "status": "proposed", "...": "..." }
// req-044 (the notification view's scope) has variants req-045 (organisation) and req-046 (own) held the same way,
// so each option's effects cover both members (3.6, C-061)

// backlog.json increments[] entry
{ "id": "inc-021", "key": "notification-access-scope",
  "title": "Users see only the notifications they may see",
  "outcome": "A signed-in user sees their notifications on the dashboard and in the notification view, and never another organisation's.",
  "why": "Both members wait on the same access-control ruling and share one end-to-end proof.",
  "members": ["req-040", "req-044"], "class": "feat",
  "surface": { "repos": ["plantsFrontend", "plantsBackend", "tests"], "areas": ["dashboard", "notification-view"] },
  "milestone": "m2", "dependsOn": ["inc-017"], "needs": ["q-notification-list-scope"], "checkpoint": "halt-after",
  "size": { "class": "M", "basis": "3 criteria, 2 areas, 3 repos" },
  "combination": { "rules": ["cr-same-acceptance-boundary", "cr-no-need-boundary"],
    "why": "One ruling decides both; one journey proves both.", "risk": "low",
    "attributability": "Each member's criteria name its own page." },
  "status": "blocked", "doneBy": null, "statusNote": "Needs q-notification-list-scope (must answer)." }
```

The question is `must-answer` (category `access-control`, 3.6). The report puts it first, its "If nobody answers" line says nothing that lists notifications is built, and `tim backlog next` never returns inc-021. Every increment that does not touch the list stays buildable, because only inc-021 carries the need (the ched-pp "buildable meanwhile" rule, E §8.5).

When Sam rules A, `tim backlog rule` applies the option's effects in one write:

- req-041 is adopted, verified by the ruling;
- req-040 is superseded by req-041, and req-044 by req-045;
- req-042 is rejected;
- the needs are cleared and the `combine` phase is reset.

The re-combine keeps the key `notification-access-scope`, so the increment keeps id inc-021 with the new members, and it becomes `todo`.

### 3.14 Proof: nothing recipe-shaped, nothing executor-specific

Every field is classed **requirement** (what or why), **meta** (identity, order, class, definition of done) or **state** (lifecycle). A field would fail if it said how to build (a recipe) or who builds (an executor).

| Field family | Class | Recipe? | Executor-specific? | Why not |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `statement`, `why`, `title`, `acceptance`, `falsifiedBy`, `outcome`, `actor` | requirement | no | no | Observable behaviour; the acceptance rule forbids files, functions and commands, and the lint reports them |
| `sources`, `confidence`, `provenance`, `specRefs`, `carriedFrom` | requirement or meta | no | no | Where the requirement came from; `readAt` pins evidence, not implementation. Citations from non-requirement roles are labelled as evidence, never a template (C-063) |
| `surface`, `edgeChecks`, header `repos` (path, role, consumes) | meta | no | no | Where, and what calls what at run time; keys into a repo table every executor reads |
| Repo how-facts: `github`, `ladder`, `knowledge`, `generated` | not in the backlog | would be | no | They describe how to build and check a repo, not a requirement, so they live on the registry entry (C-058). An increment cannot add rungs; only the plan can (FA:973-1001) |
| `consistentWith` | requirement | no | no | A relation to existing behaviour with the divergence stated; no file to copy |
| `constraints`, `invariants`, `direction`, `scope`, `deviations`, `precedence` | requirement | no | no | Programme-level rules, stated once |
| `questions`, `decisions`, `conflicts`, `assumptions` | requirement | no | no | Ledgers; `effects` act on requirements, never on code |
| `kind`, `class`, `size`, `milestone`, `checkpoint`, `dependsOn`, `sequence`, `members`, `combination`, `variantOf` | meta | no | no | Classification and order |
| `delivery` | requirement (ruled policy) | no | no | Branches, PRs and merges only: the policy Sam rules and every build honours. Where the integration proof runs is a run choice, not delivery (C-035) |
| `status`, `doneBy`, `statusNote`, `needs`, `supersededBy` | state | no | no | Requirement-level lifecycle only |

**Absent by design, and refused by name** by the strict v2 schema (judge 3, graft 6):

- **Recipe fields:** `filesToTouch`, `verification`, `recipe`, `implementorSkill`, `obligations`, `flowChanges`, `schemaFields`, `copyKeys`, `specs`, `exemplars`, `hints`, `reference`, `type` (a `frontend-change` mode), `band`, `sizeGuess`, `targetTree`, `plan`.
- **Executor fields:** `executor`, `model`, `models`, `tier`, `brief`, `outputSchema`, `sandbox`, `reviewCap`, `ciFixAttempts`.
- **Run-state fields:** `ticket`, `branch`, `commit`, `commits`, `prs`, `notes`, `openQuestions`, `gate`, `failure_reason`, `crossRepoE2e`, `integrationProofSource`.
- **Repo how-fields on a header repo:** `github`, `ladder`, `knowledge`, `generated` (their home is the registry entry, C-058).
- **Layer field:** `repo`, the loop's `frontend | backend | tests | both` (`increment-build-loop.js:282-283`), which is how layer-split rows were written. `surface.repos`, derived from the behaviour, replaces it (R7).

Each refusal message names the field's home, for example: "`filesToTouch` is a recipe field. The plan owns files: `build/plans/<id>.md`."

**The recipe and executor lint covers every prose field** (C-056), not only `statement` and acceptance: assumptions, deviations, invariants, `direction`, `outcome`, `why`, `combination.why`, `statusNote` and question text. Beside the recipe patterns, it refuses executor and build-mode vocabulary: Claude, Codex, preset, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, sandbox, main session and "by hand". Such detail belongs in `presets.json` notes, `build/run.json`, the journal or the handover. The only override is an enumerated contract named by a decision or deviation (3.12, C-057); it never switches the lint off.

**Strictness.** The v2 schema is strict: an unknown key is refused, not passed through. A new neutral field is added to the schema, with a class. A tim test asserts that every v2 field appears in `tim/src/backlog/profiles/requirements-v2.classes.js` with a class, so a field cannot enter without being classed (codex). Parity's `parity-v1` profile keeps its additive-tolerant schema (`schema.js:11-15`) for the frozen corpora.

**R5's test** is an acceptance criterion in this programme (req-024). Binding a range of increments to Codex and back leaves the backlog's hash unchanged, and the parity proof checks the hash before and after every one of its nine builds: the three canaries under `claude`, under `codex` and in a second `claude` run (8.10).

---

## 4. The writer and tooling

### 4.1 `tim backlog`: generalising `tim parity`

The base is `tim parity ingest` with `set.js`, `io.js` and `schema.js`, the writer that produced the 133-item DR1 backlog (G2). It is chosen on what it does in use, not on how little it changes (R8; C-001, C-043):

- **Ids survive.** Ingest is proven id-stable: re-ingesting with an item inserted ahead leaves every existing id unchanged, which is what lets Sam answer by id across renders.
- **Rulings are safe.** A ruled or started item cannot be struck, regrouped or silently rewritten, so a re-distillation never undoes a person's decision.
- **One core for every write.** Locks, operation ids and lost-update refusal live in one tested place, shared by parity corpora and requirement backlogs, so neither can drift into a weaker copy.

Those are the properties a requirement backlog needs, and the core is judged on serving one: its first increment must prove them on a fixture `requirements-v2` programme found through the registry, not only that parity still works (C-043).

**Moved, with their tests, from `tim/src/parity/` to `tim/src/backlog/`** (`git mv` is used only so `git log --follow` keeps their history):

- **Core:** `ingest.js` (`assignIds`, `resolveRelatedTo`, `born`, `refreshed`, `ruled`, the replace, strike and freeze refusals, the verify gate), `set.js`, `io.js` and `schema.js`.
- **Checks:** `coverage.js`, `slices.js`, `yield.js`, `duplicates.js`, `heads.js`, `meta.js`, `seals.js` and `check-evidence.js`.
- **Citations:** `citations/{evidence,anchor-check,github-url,carry-forward,resolve,snippet,parse}.js`.
- **Profile loading:** `corpus-profile.js`, renamed `profile.js`.
- **Shared render pieces:** `render/theme.js`, `render/prose.js` and `render/artifact.js`, plus the citation and snippet helpers in `render/sections.js`, move to `tim/src/backlog/render/shared/`.

**Stay in `tim/src/parity/`:** `capture/`, `manifest.js`, `dom.js`, `join.js`, `insertion.js`, `page-model-schema.js`, `split-sentinels.js`, `normalise.js`, `repoint.js`, `anchors.js`, `load.js`, `check.js`, `counts.js`, `resolution.js`, `sentences.js`, the two-sided `render/{page,card,run,sections}.js` (importing the shared pieces) and `report.contract.test.js`.

**Parity keeps working, unchanged.** `tim parity <verb>` stays registered, and each verb calls the moved core with the `parity-v1` profile. `report.contract.test.js` keeps parsing the tracked EUDPA-328 backlog through the same `parseBacklog` (G5 §6). Parity's non-regression is one of the first writer increment's criteria among several: every existing tim test passes with no fixture changed, and the EUDPA-328 page renders the same apart from its stamp. The others are the requirement-backlog proofs above: given a fixture `requirements-v2` programme, when atoms are ingested twice with one inserted ahead, every existing id is unchanged and every refusal names its field (C-043).

**Profiles as data** (`tim/src/backlog/profiles/`):

| Profile key | `parity-v1` (existing behaviour) | `requirements-v2` (new) |
|---|---|---|
| Schema | today's `schema.js:112-143`, additive-tolerant | section 3, strict, refused keys named |
| Item folders | `findings/` | `distil/atoms/`, `distil/increments/` |
| Id prefixes | `inc-` | `req-`, `inc-`, `q-` (slug), `d-`, `c-`, `as-`, `inv-`, `out-`, `dv-` |
| Kinds | `FINDING_TYPES` (`ingest.js:20-28`) | 3.4 `kind`, 3.5 `class` |
| Prose slots | `frontend`, `prototype`, `difference`, `falsifiedBy` and the `set.js` extras | `title`, `statement` (frozen), `why`, `falsifiedBy`; for a combined increment `title`, `outcome`, `why` and `combination.why`. A solo increment's title and outcome are not slots: they are rendered from its member at read time, so no stored copy can drift (C-062) |
| Unit loader | capture manifests (screens) | `distil/sources/*/units.json` |
| Verification required at first ingest | per corpus (`requireVerification`) | always, with the registered-run guard |
| Phase table | stays in `tools/parity/phase.sh:29-44` | 5.2, held by `tim backlog phase` |
| Born status | `todo` | derived (3.9) |
| Registry | `tools/backlog/registry.json` entries with `profile: parity-v1` | entries with `profile: requirements-v2` |

`tools/parity/corpora.json` becomes `tools/backlog/registry.json` (`git mv`, for history). Each entry gains a `profile`, a `workarea` and, per repo key, the how-facts the backlog no longer carries: `github`, `ladder` (rungs, with optional `shards` for a long rung, C-019; a `script` rung names only a committed file, C-067), `knowledge` and `generated` (C-058). `stage prepare` reads them from here. For a self-hosting programme, `generated` covers `workareas/shared/<p>/**`, so the programme's own state never dirties the workspace repo (C-065). Readers that built paths from a run id (`corpus-profile.js:88-90`, `scaffold-corpus.sh:98`) read the registry instead. For new programmes the `EUDPA-*` run-id rule goes. `start-comparison.sh:46-49` says the rule exists only because of the bash readers, which keep working for the four frozen corpora.

### 4.2 Command inventory

Every command takes a programme key `<p>` (a registry key) or `--workarea <path>`. With `--json` it prints a schema-versioned envelope, as CLAUDE.md requires. Every write accepts `--op-id` and `--expect-sha`.

**Programme, registry and migration**

| Command | Does | Reuses |
|---|---|---|
| `init <p> --title --purpose-file [--workarea]` | Creates the workarea, the header skeleton, `distil/CONTRACT.md` from the template and the registry entry | `scaffold-corpus.sh` behaviour, now in tim |
| `registry list \| show <p> \| add <p>` | The programme registry | `corpora.json`, `corpus-profile.js` |
| `migrate <path> --to <p> --from loop-v1\|parity\|parity-union\|trace\|fa-stages\|design [--dry-run]` | Translates a v1 backlog into v2 (section 10), or adopts a hand-written v2 file after validation (`design`). Built rows stay done, as history. Unbuilt legacy rows become `proposed` legacy atoms, and `distil/reslice.json` lists candidate behaviour groups (shared areas and titles, dependency edges across different repo tags, tests-for relations) for the `reslice` phase. Legacy recipe fields (files, steps, ladders) go to an existing-backlog source, `distil/sources/<legacy-id>/units.json` with role `current-behaviour`: they can be cited, and never enter `brief.json` or `standards.json`. Ingest refuses any migrated row still layer-split (C-034) | new |

**The decision engine** (C-002)

| Command | Does | Reuses |
|---|---|---|
| `advance <p> --run <label> --agent <id> [--inc <id>] [--budget-seconds 540] [--json]` | The one place every build transition rule lives, tested with vitest on fixture folders. Each call accepts pending drafts (`stage accept`, then `--finalise`), checks the expected task set against `stage.json`, re-derives the resume point from persisted state and fact records, applies the stage table's transition rule, and **executes** every mechanical sub-step in stage-table order through the macros below (next, bindings, branches, sync, rungs, manifest, secrets, the stack, local commits, stage audit, status, report, counts and the state commit), writing a fact record for each (C-075, C-082). It continues until it reaches exactly one of: `{status: run, span, tasks[], count, stageSha, then}` (a judgement span to walk, with the compact task graph, its count and the `stage.json` sha256, C-070, C-077); `{status: act, commands[]}` (outward commands only, each to be run verbatim as its own Bash call, C-076); `{status: handback}` (this `--agent` reached `quality.stepBudget` calls, counted per agent id in run state, C-074); `{status: stop, stopReason}`; or it exits 75 before `--budget-seconds` so the same command is called again. It observes every outward effect on the next call (remote ref, PR, check, merge, ticket), so no exit code is ever relayed into state. `--record` and the `wait` step are removed | the loop's resume rule (`increment-build-loop.js:938-946`), ported and tested; `exec/exec.js`, `exec/parallel.js` |
| `advance <p> --distil --run <label> --agent <id> [--json]` | The same engine over the distil phase table. It runs in-process: heads, `units --strict`, `slices --strict`, yield, coverage, `ingest --atoms`, `trace --strict`, question lint, `candidates --combine`, `ingest --increments`, `check --strict` and report, and any Codex-bound phase through `tim backlog codex run` (C-078). Model phases come back as spans; a stage whose remaining work to a stop is light carries `then: close`. A failed gate returns `{status: stop, stopReason: gate:<name>}` with the command's missing list | `phase.sh` ledger rules |

An `act` command is always an outward action: a native refspec push generated after tim's branch proof and fast-forward check, `tim github pr ensure-draft`, `tim github pr merge --expect-head`, or `tim jira create | transition`. The step agent runs each verbatim as its own call, and the next `advance` observes its effect. Every local step runs inside tim (C-075, C-076). Verify-skip, fix rounds, repair budgets, `ciFixAttempts`, the baseline-red hygiene rule, `systemicHaltAfter`, holds and the resume point are all rules in the stage table read by `advance` (4.5), not prose in a workflow script. A Workflow run and a Codex-orchestrated session call the same command, so they cannot follow different rules.

**Mechanical macros and outward commands** (C-074 to C-079)

Every macro follows `tim/CLAUDE.md`: a sibling `*.test.js`, input and output tests, git fixtures for git, undici MockAgent for HTTP, no shell-out to `gh`, `jq`, `curl` or `tools/*.sh`, GDS plain English and the `--json` envelope. Every one takes `--op-id`, is idempotent at sub-step granularity (it skips a sub-step whose fact record exists at the same heads and tree fingerprint), writes a fact record per sub-step (C-082), and stops itself with exit 75 before 540 seconds, killing every descendant process first. Each is callable on its own by a person or a Codex-orchestrated session; `advance` calls the same functions in-process. This is Sam's "mega prepare": one tested call per run of mechanical steps, issued by a cheap step agent that makes no decision (C-074).

| Command | Stage ids | Does | Reuses |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prepare <p> --run <l> [--inc <id>] [--repos <key>...] --json` | 1-6, 7p; 9 with `--repos` | Next and bindings; the Jira create returned as `act` when `ticketing` is set; branch checkout, track or cut under one name in every surface repo; `heads --for --strict`; parallel fetch; `merge-tree --write-tree` probe; local merge of base, or abort and hold `sync-blocked` with no partial merge left (the merge is pushed with land, never on its own); baseline rungs, in parallel across repos except rungs the registry marks `exclusive`; hygiene `discover` and hold `baseline-red` on a red rung; `stage prepare --span plan` | `workspace/branch.js` (`inspectAll`, `fetchAll`, `checkoutArgs` for the tracking case), `exec/exec.js`, `exec/parallel.js` `runAcross`, `backlog/heads.js`, new `backlog/git/{branch,merge,commit,stash}.js` |
| `rung <p> --run <l> --inc <id> --repo <key> --rung <name> [--shard <i>/<n>] [--phase baseline\|check\|ladder\|proof] --json` | one rung | Runs one npm, mvn or committed-script rung in the foreground with a timeout; writes its log and fact record; `skipped-undefined`; `could-not-run:uncommitted` (C-067); `could-not-run:timeout` becomes hold `environment` with a release command. Replaces `run-rung.sh` | `workspace/test.js` npm and Maven detection, `exec.js` |
| `check <p> --run <l> --inc <id> --after implement\|fix\|repair --json` | 11, 12, 13p (11r after a fix) | Browser, Docker and command-invariant rungs of the changed repos; the manifest from the pinned heads with `rulesGap[]`, protected-path refusal and the proof that no HEAD or remote ref moved; prepares the next span. On red it returns the implement, fix or repair task to resume as `run` | `rung`, `exec/stack.js`, `stage manifest` |
| `prove <p> --run <l> --inc <id> [--budget-seconds 540] --json` | 18-20, 21p | Registry rungs, then plan extras; `secrets --inc`; `tim docker dev --include <touched>`; one shard per call sized from recorded durations; one retry of failed shards; `tim docker down` always; `state.integrationProof` from the JSON reporter; prepares acceptance, never with the plan | `rung`, `exec/stack.js`, `commands/docker` (gains `--include`), `backlog/secrets.js` |
| `land <p> --run <l> --inc <id> --json` | 22 | Commits each touched repo by name with `-F`, the trailer from `run.json` and `Backlog-Increment: <p>/<inc>/a<n>`, in merge order, so a re-run finds the commit and never commits twice; wip commits and named-path stashes for red work; branch proof (on `<b>`, not `main` or `master`, refspec names it) and fast-forward check against the observed remote ref; returns one push per repo as `act`, or none under `local` delivery | `backlog/git/commit.js`, `graph.js` merge order |
| `ci-wait <p> --run <l> --inc <id> [--budget-seconds 540] --json` | 24 | Every PR's check runs and statuses for its head through octokit; SonarCloud from the checks, or "no report"; exit 2 unresolved, 4 none, never green; a red check returns the CI fixer as `run` | `github-client` (gains `listCheckRunsForRef`, `getCombinedStatus`), `gha-client` polling |
| `record <p> --run <l> --inc <id> --json` | 26, 27 | Adopts verified discovered atoms, `status done --done-by build` (the audit), report, handover, the state commit by name; returns the state push as `act` when `record` is `commit-push` | the writer core, `backlog/git/commit.js` |
| `codex run <p> <stageDir> [--budget-seconds 540] [--check] --json` | any Codex stage | `run-stage.sh`'s contract and exit codes (8.2) in tested JavaScript: `CODEX_HOME`, the stage lock, concurrency by sandbox, starts and resumes, accept after every draft with three correction rounds, the descendant-tree kill (every descendant from `ps -A -o pid=,ppid=`, so a grandchild in its own process group dies too, C-015), five slices, `runner.json`. Called in-process by `advance` (C-078) | `exec.js`; `test-support/fake-codex.js` replaces `fake-codex.sh` |
| `pin --commit <sha> [--json]` | 0 | Clones the workspace at the commit into `workareas/clones/pipeline-pin/`, installs tim through the canonical real path (`pwd -P`, so the lockfile trap cannot corrupt it), reuses an intact pin at the same commit, prints the pinned prefix. Replaces `pin-tools.sh` (C-079) | `exec.js` |

The registry's rung entries gain `exclusive: true` for a rung that must not run beside another repo's rungs (a shared port, a shared Docker resource); `prepare`, `check` and `prove` run every other rung in parallel across repos when `quality.rungParallel` is set (7.7).

**Outward commands.** Each is issued only as its own visible Bash call, generated by a macro after its refusals pass, run verbatim by the step agent, and observed by the next `advance`. They are allowlisted (`settings.json:83, 88`), so the allowlist is not the gate: tim's refusals, guard-bash on the call and observation afterwards are (C-076).

| Command | Does |
|---|---|
| `git -C <tilde repo> push -u origin refs/heads/<b>:refs/heads/<b>` | Native git, generated by `land` or `record` only after the branch proof and fast-forward check. Never `--force`, never a `+` refspec. octokit cannot push, and inside tim a push would be invisible to guard-bash |
| `tim github pr ensure-draft --repo <slug> --head <b> --base <b> --title-file <f> --body-file <f> --json` | Creates or reuses a draft PR, one call per repo, only where tim observes no open PR; exit 3 when only a merged or closed PR exists; never marks ready. `github-client` gains `pulls.list`, `pulls.create`, `pulls.update` |
| `tim github pr checks --repo <slug> --head <sha> [--wait] [--budget-seconds 540] --json` | Read-only check waiter with the exit-2-and-4 contract (req-001). `ci-wait` calls the same function in-process, so it is never an `act` in the loop |
| `tim github pr merge --repo <slug> --number <n> --expect-head <sha> [--method merge] --json` | Merges one PR only if its head equals `--expect-head`, it is green, and it is approved where required; emitted per repo in `consumes` order, only when `delivery.merge` carries a person's decision; a refused merge stops the rest with `pr-left-open`. `github-client` gains `pulls.merge`, `pulls.listReviews` |
| `tim jira create --type <t> --summary-file <f> --description-file <f> [--parent <key>] --json`; `tim jira transition <key> --to <status> --json` | Jira writes, when `ticketing` is set. `jira-client` gains POST for issues and transitions |

The GitHub and Jira commands live under the existing `tim github` and `tim jira` groups, not `tim backlog`, so inc-001 (the PR and check commands) gains no dependency on the writer.

**Distillation control plane**

| Command | Does | Reuses |
|---|---|---|
| `phase <p> status \| start <ph> \| done <ph> --note --run <label> \| gate <ph> \| reset <ph> --note [--scope source:<id>]` | The phase ledger. `done` needs its prerequisites and a note, and records an artefact fingerprint. `reset` cascades to every dependant phase. `gate` exits 1 naming what is missing | `phase.sh:29-44, 145-173`, fixing G1 §2 item 4 (no cascade, no fingerprint, bash, untracked) |
| `heads <p> [--write] [--strict] [--for <inc>]` | Records `{sha, branch, dirty}` per repo key, never an absolute path. `--strict` exits 1 on `moved` and on dirty-at-start. Dirty means tracked files only (`--untracked-files=no`), scoped to the increment's plan and manifest paths, with the registry's `generated` globs excluded, so another session's untracked files or the programme's own state never fail a build (C-065) | `heads.js`, fixing G3 §1.4 items 1 to 4 |
| `source <p> add --file \| acquire <id> \| seal [--reseal] \| check` | Registers a source; fetches Confluence and Jira through tim's clients and copies files; converts Word (docx), spreadsheet (xlsx, csv) and slide (pptx) files to `raw/<id>.txt` with paragraph, row, cell and slide markers, library-first with `fflate`, tested on fixtures, so no agent needs `unzip` or a sed chain (C-040); seals content with `{kind, locator, contentHash}`; reports drift kinds | `seals.js` algorithm generalised (G3 §3.4); `confluence-client`, `jira-client` |
| `source <p> crop <sourceId> <unitToken> --box x,y,w,h --name <slug>` | Cuts a crop from a visual source with `sharp`, writes `distil/sources/<id>/crops/<slug>.png` and records `{crop, box, sha256}` on the unit. Characterise calls it for every visual unit, so a question about something visual always carries the picture (C-039) | new |
| `units <p> [--strict]` | P1: every source has units or a stated absence, and an extract-verify record for every unit. It also fails while any pointer unit on a requirement-role source (a linked workbook, a SharePoint or OneDrive file, a prototype) is not registered as a source, stated absent with a reason, or raised as a question (C-040) | `coverage.js` set algebra with the units loader |
| `slices <p> [--strict]` | P2: every unit in exactly one slice; exactly one cross-cutting slice; every slice has a `behaviour` sentence. It flags a slice whose units all come from one code repo, or whose id or brief names a repo key or a layer word (backend, frontend, tests, API, database and the like); a flagged slice fails unless its record carries a written reason (C-028) | `slices.js:121-263` |
| `yield <p> [--strict]` | P3: slices under 0.4 of the median atoms per owned unit; homeless, strayed and unverified atoms; author equal to verifier. The cross-cutting slice is exempt | `yield.js:23, 69-161` plus the identity check |
| `coverage <p> [--strict]` | Every unit is cited by an atom or listed by its slice's author as not a requirement, with a reason. It fails on any "not a requirement" dismissal that no verify task has ruled on, so the author alone can never drop a unit (C-049) | `coverage.js:75-102` |
| `trace <p> [--strict]` | P4: every unit ends in exactly one of five buckets: cited, ruled not a requirement (by an author and a verifier who agreed, C-049), a side of a conflict, excluded by scope, or a duplicate | new, on the same set algebra |
| `duplicates <p> [--cross-slice-only]` | Candidate duplicate pairs over **all pairs by default** (DR1C's within-slice miss, G1 §2 item 5), with structured signals: shared unit, shared decision, shared `consistentWith`. Sharing repos is not a signal: under R7 almost every product atom touches the same repos (C-027) | `duplicates.js:168-248` |
| `candidates <p> --combine` | Pass 2 candidates from behaviour-level signals only (5.7), each with a signal id the combiner must cite, scored and explained, exclusions named. It emits no repo-set signal (C-027) | `duplicates.js` pair machinery, one level up |

**Content and ledgers**

| Command | Does | Reuses |
|---|---|---|
| `ingest <p> --atoms \| --increments [--dry-run] [--replace]` | Assigns stable ids by key; resolves every reference; refuses dangling, self and cyclic references, unverified atoms and bad provenance; freezes `statement` at the first adopted ingest (C-055); derives confidence, surfaces, `needs`, statuses, lifted dependencies, option `counts` by role (C-050) and `blastRadius.blocks` and `restsOnDefault` (C-052); creates solo increments keyed `solo--<root atom key>` (C-054); refuses to regroup or drop a started or ruled item. It knows every v2 field: `edgeChecks`, `surface.amendments`, `carriedFrom[]`, `defaultWhy`, decision `reopens`, `reverseEffects` and the effect op `split` with `into` | `ingest.js`, extended to `dependsOn`, `members`, `needs` (G2 §3 item 6) |
| `check <p> [--strict]` | Schema; references; cycles, including a cycle in the repos' `consumes` (C-026); derived fields; one increment per adopted atom (P9); role and confidence checks, including that `observed` cites a `prototype`, `trace-corpus` or `live-service` source (C-040); credential lint; the same criterion text in three or more atoms, which must be an invariant (P6); `on-green` merge without a person's decision; `run.json` presets exist. **Recipe and executor lint** over every prose field (statement, acceptance, `why`, outcome, `combination.why`, assumptions, deviations, invariants, direction, `statusNote` and question text): it refuses recipe shapes (file paths, commands, steps) outside an enumerated contract override, and executor vocabulary (Claude, Codex, preset, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, sandbox, main session, by hand) everywhere (C-056, C-057). **Header refusals:** repo how-fields (`github`, `ladder`, `knowledge`, `generated`) and `delivery.crossRepoE2e`, named (C-035, C-058); a combination rule keyed on repos (C-027). **P11, the five layer-chain rules of 3.12** (C-025): (1) a `dependsOn` edge, on atoms or lifted onto increments, between two rows whose product repo sets are both non-empty and disjoint; (2) a dependency path of two or more edges that steps from provider to consumer by `consumes`, or ends in a tests-only row; (3) any other cross-repo edge with no `edgeChecks` record, or whose dependency is provider-only, cannot be observed by its own actor and has `observableWithout: false`; (4) the one exception, a decision that names the edge; (5) class `test` on a row with a non-`hygiene` member. It also refuses a missing integration-proof criterion (3.12), including a tooling `feat` or `fix` with no `e2e` criterion that runs the tool's real entry point (C-033), and a single-repo product row unless it is `hygiene` with a regression criterion (C-032) | `schema.js`, parity `check.js` idiom |
| `show <p> [<id>] [--header] [--brief] [--state] [--out <file>]` | `--brief` is **the** input every build task receives: the increment, each member with its acceptance, sources and quotes, the invariants, decisions and assumptions they reference (expanded), the header purpose, scope, direction and repos (with role and `consumes`), and each dependency's title and status. Citations from non-requirement roles (code, current behaviour) are labelled "evidence of current behaviour, not a design to copy" (C-063). Legacy recipe fields from a migrated backlog never appear (C-034) | new view over existing data |
| `set <p> <id> <slot> --file` | One slot on one atom or increment; profile whitelist; `statement` refused after the first adopted ingest; a solo increment has no title or outcome slot (C-062) | `set.js:47-77` |
| `question <p> add \| set \| lint` ; `conflict`, `assumption`, `invariant <p> add` | Ledger records from a file; `question lint` is P8's mechanical half | the `writeIncrement` idiom |
| `rule <p> (<q> \| --subject <kind>:<id>) (--option X \| --choose <side> \| --split \| --add-repo <key> \| --answer-file f \| --effects-file f) --words --by --at --note [--why <text>] [--tentative] [--reopens] [--supersedes d-N] [--applies-to ids]` | Appends a decision and applies its effects in one write (3.7). `--by` is `sam`, a named delegate, `panel`, `precedence`, `default` or `build`. **Defaults:** `--by default` applies a defaulted question's default at once, writing a decision with status `defaulted` that runs its effects; a later ruling by a person supersedes it (C-044). **Supersession reverts first:** every applied effect records the target's `prior` state, and a ruling that supersedes a decision (a person's ruling over a default, or `--supersedes d-N`) restores each of that decision's targets to its `prior`, in reverse order, before applying its own effects, so an increment a default deferred or dropped comes back when the other option is ruled. A target that has since moved on is named, and the ruling must then carry `--reopens` or an explicit effect for it (3.7). **Reversals:** `--subject conflict:<id> --choose <side>` applies that side's predeclared `reverseEffects`, and `--subject combination:<key> --split` applies the group's `split` effect, so every call in report section 2 is one paste-ready command (C-047). **Surface amendments:** `--subject surface:<atom> --add-repo <key> --why <text> --by build` appends `{repoKey, increment, attempt, why}` to the atom's `surface.amendments[]` and the repo to `surface.repos` when a plan adds a repo (C-031). `--by build` takes `--at` from the workflow args and `--words`/`--note` from the plan's `reposAdded[].why`, so the form above (3.4, 4.2, 7.4) is complete as written. **Design adoption:** `--subject design:<path> --answer-file f --applies-to ids` records a decision that adopts a design document as the programme's requirement, with no effects; it is how a programme that starts from a design rather than a distillation adopts that design and the inferred atoms it lists (3.7). **Built work:** it refuses an effect on a member of a started or done increment unless the decision carries `--reopens`, which creates a variant atom and a follow-up solo increment depending on the done one and leaves `doneBy` untouched (C-055) | `spec-add-decision.sh` semantics; `rule-decision.sh`'s reason rule |
| `verify-record <p> --file <verdicts.json>` | Records verdicts from a registered verifier task onto atoms: stands, corrected (with the original kept), disputed (a question is required), added; and on every "not a requirement" dismissal: agree, or dispute with an added atom (C-049). It also writes each verifier's `edgeChecks` records (C-025) | new; used by inc-010 and by `stage accept` |
| `discover <p> --from <inc> --file <item.json>` | Adds work a build found and the judge adopted, as a `proposed` atom with a solo increment that the next combine may regroup. It is adopted only after a separate `REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER` task and the recipe lint pass, like any other atom. The file and line that prompted it go to `build/state.json` and the journal, never into the atom's sources (C-059) | replaces three channels (C §2.6) |
| `status <p> <id> <status> --note [--decision d-N] [--done-by build\|ruling]` | Row status transitions, validated. `done --done-by build` runs `stage audit` and refuses unless it passes | replaces `backlog-set-status.sh` |
| `counts <p>` | N of TOTAL (P%), with deferred and dropped named separately; awaiting-a-ruling by the one definition; defaults in force counted apart from open must-answer questions (C-044) | replaces `decision-counts.sh`, `backlog-counts.sh` for v2 |

**Build state and run configuration**

| Command | Does | Reuses |
|---|---|---|
| `next <p> [--explain <id>]` | The one buildability rule (3.9). Returns `{id, bindings}` for that increment, resolved now, or `{none, reasons}`. `advance` runs it in-process; on its own it is for people and handovers | replaces three rules (C §5.1) |
| `state <p> <id> set <field> --json v \| note --file \| hold <reason> --note [--released-by <inc>] \| release \| retry --from <stage>` | Run-state writes with a field whitelist. A hold withholds the increment from `next` until released | replaces agent `jq` in both workflows |
| `run <p> init \| show \| bind (--ids a..b \| --from <id>\|next\|after-current [--to <id>]) (--preset <name> \| --role r=claude\|codex) --said <sentence> --by --at \| set <knob> <v> \| explain <inc> \| open --label <l> --mode build\|distil --at <ISO> --trailer-file <f> [--pin] \| close --label <l> \| start --label --mode build\|distil --at --args-file \| end --label --stop <reason>` | Executor bindings and run knobs in `build/run.json`; run registration (the ingest guard's source of truth). A binding applies from the next stage prepared, even mid-increment: a running task finishes on its executor, and prepared but unstarted tasks are re-prepared under the new binding (C-003). **`run open`** is the launch: preflight (state branch, tim root, the pinned tim answers `--version --json`), the pin (`pin`), `run start`, stage schemas exported, then `advance` while the next sub-steps are `light`; it writes the args file itself and prints the Workflow args, with `firstStep` when it reached a `run` (C-080). **`run close`** runs `advance` while the remaining sub-steps are `light` and end in a stop, then `run end`, `counts` and the handover print. `run start` and `run end` stay as the steps `open` and `close` call, and remain callable on their own for people and recovery. `run start` creates the gitignored `build/.accept-key` (C-012) and `distil/phases.json` on a distil run | new file, same writer |

**Stages, context and proofs**

| Command | Does |
|---|---|
| `stage <p> prepare --run <label> --inc <id> --attempt <n> --stage <s> [--span <name>]` | Builds the stage's task folders (4.5, 8.1); with `--span`, prepares every task of a span with upstream references, `after`, `when`, `mustIf` and `then` (C-077); refuses when an upstream stage of the attempt is incomplete; returns the task list, each task's executor and whether the stage is skipped, then prints a compact task list, the count and the sha256 of `stage.json` so a relayed list can be checked before fan-out (C-070). `advance` calls it in-process |
| `stage <p> accept <taskDir>` ; `stage <p> accept --stage <stageDir> --finalise` | Per task: validates the draft against the stage schema, cross-checks it against tool facts and the reading record, and writes only `output.json` and `receipt.json` in that task's folder, or a correction request (4.5). `--finalise`, once per stage after the fan-out, applies every shared side effect (C-005) |
| `stage <p> audit --inc <id> [--attempt <n>]` | Re-derives the attempt from disk and lists every missing or failed record (4.5) |
| `stage <p> manifest --inc <id>` | The change manifest per touched repo, diffed from the attempt's pinned baseline heads (C-068), with mechanical exclusions and `rulesGap[]` (C-037); refuses changes to protected paths (C-010); proves no commit and no remote-ref move by any task |
| `stage schemas --out <dir>` ; `stage table` | Codex-strict JSON Schemas exported from the zod source; the stage table |
| `standards --files <repoKey:path>... [--repo-level <repoKey>...] [--lint]` | Renamed from `rules` (C-037). The single resolver over both skills' routing data (`.claude/skills/code-style/assets/routing.json`, `.claude/skills/review/assets/routing.json`), the `.claude/rules` globs and the best-practice files each rule points to, the CLAUDE.md chain with `@` imports, and the memory index (8.6). Per file it returns the rules, topics and best-practice files; per repo, the repo-level routing; per task role, the skill's `SKILL.md` and persona files (8.5). `stage prepare` calls it in-process for every task it prepares and writes `standards.json`: every file by its live workspace path, never a copy or a baked bundle, each with the blob sha it had at that moment. The shas are an audit record, so both executors can be shown to have read the same standards; they never pin a file, and nothing refuses to run because a standard changed (8.5). `--lint` exits 1 on a pointer to a missing file |
| `evidence <p> --inc <id>` | Per citation of the increment's members: blob equal, out of range, missing from file or dead, against current heads (G3 §4 item 5) |
| `secrets <p> --inc <id> [--staged]` | A credential-shape scan of the increment's diff per touched repo, with the same detector as the credential lint; exits 1 naming file and line |
| `report <p> [--artifact] [--answer-sheet] [--decisions] [--merge-plan] [--pr-body <repoKey>] [--lint]` | Renders section 6's report, the answer sheet, a PR body and, from `questions[]`, the decisions page, so no hand-written copy can disagree with the JSON (C-052). `--merge-plan` prints an increment's or programme's PRs in merge order from `consumes`, providers first, with the rule "all green, then merge in this order, no partial merge" (C-026). `--lint` runs the prose checks of 6.6 over the report and the answer sheet |
| `audit-reading <p> --run <label> [--task <dir>]` | Decides, per task, whether every "must" file was read. A file counts as read only when reads cover its full line range, singly or cumulatively. The same rule applies to Claude `Read` calls with offset and limit (from `build/reads/<session>.jsonl`) and to Codex command output (from `events.jsonl`): `sed -n` ranges, `cat`, `head` and `tail`, and a loop over files; `rg -l` and `ls` read nothing. Fixture tests cover each. `stage accept` calls it per task (4.5); on its own it summarises a run (C-011) |
| `compare <p> --left <label> --right <label> [--spread <label>]` | The parity proof report (8.10). `--spread` names the second Claude run, whose Claude-to-Claude spread sets the option D bar (C-018, d-007). It fails a measure when the two sides' Codex version, model, effort or config hash differ (C-009). When their recorded standards shas differ, it names the changed files and marks the comparison not comparable: the fix is to re-run the proof under the current standards, never to hold a standard back (C-037, 8.5) |
| `handover <p> --executor claude\|codex [--md] [--out <file>]` | The resumption prompt, or the Codex-orchestrated variant (8.11). The record stage and every hold write `build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md` with it, so Codex can take over with no Claude session (C-003) |

### 4.3 Validation, locks, lost updates, idempotency and clocks

- **Schema.** zod, strict for v2 (3.14) and additive-tolerant for `parity-v1`. Every write parses the file before and after.
- **Lock.** Each canonical file has its own lock (`.backlog.lock`, `build/.state.lock`, `build/.run.lock`, `distil/.phases.lock`). The lock is created exclusively and holds `{pid, host, command, opId, at}`. A stale lock whose process has gone is broken with a note. A live lock makes the command retry three times with back-off inside tim, which is a committed tool polling, not an agent sleeping. It then exits 4 (`LOCKED`), which always means "run the same command again unchanged", never "correct your draft". This is journey-builder's lock (`backlog-plan-increment.sh:60-71`) in tim form. Contention stays low by design: a per-task `stage accept` writes only inside its own task folder and takes no shared lock, so up to 16 concurrent accepts never compete; every shared write happens once, in `stage accept --finalise` (C-005).
- **Lost updates.** Every write returns `{sha256}`, as `io.js:44-55` already does, and accepts `--expect-sha`. A mismatch exits 3 (`LOST_UPDATE`) and changes nothing. Only one step agent is in flight at a time, so mechanical writes are serial.
- **Idempotency.** Every write accepts `--op-id <runLabel>:<inc>:<attempt>:<stage>:<task>:<verb>`. `.backlog-ops.jsonl` records applied operations with their results, and a replay is a no-op that prints the original result (codex; judges 1 and 2).
- **Temp files** get a unique name per process, `.<name>.<pid>.<random>.tmp` (fixes the shared name at `io.js:48`).
- **Before and after.** Every write prints the changed paths with their old and new values.
- **Clocks.** Ledger writes (decisions, rulings, questions) never read the clock. The caller passes `--at`, so a replayed session gives a byte-identical ledger (`journey-builder/SKILL.md:72-73`). Workflows receive `at` in their args, because scripts cannot read the clock. Run-state and journal writes are stamped by tim, because they record when something happened on this machine, and operation ids keep them idempotent.
- **Refusals** that protect work:
  - A ruled or started item cannot be struck or regrouped (`ingest.js:526-559`).
  - `statement` is frozen from the first ingest that adopts the atom (`ingest.js:561-582`; C-055).
  - `rule` refuses an effect on a member of a started or done increment unless the decision carries `reopens: true`. Then it creates a variant atom and a follow-up solo increment that depends on the done one, and leaves `doneBy` untouched: built work is re-opened explicitly, never silently moved back to `todo` (C-055).
  - A verification must come from a registered run's task that is not the author. **Registered-run guard, bootstrap case** (C-053): a bootstrap verification record under `distil/bootstrap-verify/` (made by hand-launched agents before task folders exist) is accepted only as upstream input to a registered verification run, never as a verification on its own.
  - Credential-shaped text is refused wherever it appears.
- **Exit codes:** 0 ok, 1 refused by validation or a check, 2 usage, 3 lost update, 4 locked, 5 audit failed, 75 slice boundary (call the same command again). Every message is GDS plain English (`tim/CLAUDE.md:20`).

### 4.4 Ids, keys, cycles and lifting

`assignIds` (`ingest.js:310-328`) keys every handle on the authored file's key. `resolveRelatedTo` (`ingest.js:361-388`) becomes a general resolver over `dependsOn`, `relatedTo`, `members`, `needs`, `constraints`, `assumptions` and `variantOf`. It fails on dangling and self references in the same pass that hands out ids. A new `graph.js` runs Tarjan's algorithm over atom and increment `dependsOn`, and over the repos' `consumes`, and names the cycle as a path of keys and titles. Lifting drops a member's dependency that points inside its own group and maps one that points outside to the owning increment. Carryover and re-combine match previous items by stable content, as `carry-forward.js:14-36` does, never by position.

**Ids survive rulings** (C-054). Every member is first resolved through `supersededBy` to its root atom. A solo increment is keyed `solo--<root atom key>`, and a combined group is matched by the set of its members' roots, so a variant that supersedes a member changes neither key nor id: the increment keeps its id and status, with the member swapped. Ingest carries keys forward whatever the combiner writes. A test rules a variant onto a solo member and onto a combined member and asserts both increment ids are unchanged.

### 4.5 The stage machinery

This is the file-first contract and the stage machine, both from the codex candidate. **tim decides every transition and executes every local, reversible effect; outward effects are generated, run as visible calls and observed** (C-002, C-075). The stage machine was first declined only for its rebuild cost, which carries no weight (R8). In use, a second copy of the transition rules in workflow prose, or in a Codex-orchestrated session's prompt, is lossy, and resume logic in untestable workflow JavaScript is where the loop has failed before. So the rules live once, in data plus `tim backlog advance` (4.2), tested with vitest on fixture folders. The workflow scripts are thin interpreters of its steps. Outward commands (push, PR, merge, Jira) are generated by tim and run verbatim by the step agent, one Bash call each, so guard-bash sees every one (C-042, C-076).

- **The stage table** (`tim/src/backlog/stages/table.js`) is data for every build and distil stage: `{id, kind: mechanical | outward | model, cost: light | heavy, span, role, fanOut, requires[], inputs[], brief, schema, bindable, sandbox, after[], when, transitions}`. `cost` is `heavy` for any sub-step that runs a rung, the stack, Codex, a network call or a git write in a product repo; `run open` and `run close` run only `light` sub-steps (C-080). `span` names the judgement span a model stage runs in (J1 to J4, DJ1 to DJ11), and `after[]` and `when` are the span walker's start rules. Every conditional stage's `when` is data the audit re-derives (C-081). `fanOut` is one of `one`, `per-file`, `per-file-with-findings`, `per-source` or `per-slice`. `inputs[]` is the stage's input matrix (8.1): what `stage prepare` copies into each task, and what the audit checks was copied. `transitions` holds the stage's rules: its skip condition (verify skipped when no findings), its round and repair budgets, `ciFixAttempts`, the baseline-red hygiene rule, `systemicHaltAfter`, the hold each failure maps to (a rung timeout becomes hold `environment` naming the rung, with a release command, C-019) and where a resume re-enters. The build stages follow the canonical order 0 to 27 in 7.4. No build stage fans out per repo: the plan, the implementation, the consistency review and the acceptance check each see the whole slice (R7). There is no `STAGE_ORDER` constant in any script: `advance` is the only reader of the order.
- **`stage prepare`** does eight things:
  1. It resolves the bindings in force (`build/run.json` with `.claude/workflows/executors/presets.json`) per task, at every prepare, and records each task's executor in `stage.json`. A binding changed mid-increment therefore applies from the next stage (C-003).
  2. It refuses when an upstream stage of the same attempt is incomplete, and returns the upstream stage's route summary: counts, files with findings, whether a fix is due.
  3. It creates one task folder per task (8.1), and writes `input/brief.json` from `show --brief` and `input/standards.json` from `tim backlog standards` (in-process), the persona map, the registry's knowledge list and the memory index. For every review, verify, judge, fix and fix-verify task, the "must" list names the owning skill's `SKILL.md` and the persona by their live paths, beside the routed best-practice files (8.5). Every entry is a live workspace path with the blob sha it had at prepare time, for audit only: nothing is copied or baked, and a skill or standard edited after prepare reaches the task when it reads the file. A writing task's standards are the union of the routing for its planned files and the repo-level routing for every repo in the surface, so a Codex writer gets the rules for a file it creates as well as those it planned (C-037). It copies only the inputs the stage's matrix names: in particular it never copies the plan or a plan output into an acceptance task (C-021, C-022).
  4. It writes stage extras: per-file diffs from the manifest, `input/slice.json` (the plan's seams and every changed file in every repo) for per-file review tasks, `evidence-check.json` for the plan, and ladder logs for repair.
  5. It writes `prompt.md`, a `codex.json` for each Codex task, and a `stage.json` manifest listing every task, its executor and, for review stages, the manifest base heads (C-068).
  6. It prints a compact task list, the task count and the sha256 of `stage.json`. The script checks what the step agent relays against them before it fans out (C-070).
  7. It is idempotent: a task with an accepted receipt is marked done and skipped.
  8. With `--span`, it prepares every task of the span at once: a downstream task's `input/upstream/` holds references by path to its upstream outputs, and its `standards.json` marks entries `mustIf` a ruled file, so the reading audit requires only those that apply (C-077). Each task carries its `after[]` and `when`, and the span carries `then` (`close` when the remaining work to a stop is light).
- **`stage accept <taskDir>`** (per task) writes only inside that task's folder (C-005):
  1. It validates `output.draft.json` against the stage's zod schema.
  2. It cross-checks the draft against tool facts: ladder results against tim's fact records (C-082), changed files against the manifest, and a summary that calls a failure "pre-existing" without a reproduction on the base branch.
  3. It checks the reading record with `audit-reading`'s rule (4.2): every "must" file in the task's `standards.json`, including the memory index and, for the roles 8.5 names, the skill's `SKILL.md` and persona, read over its full line range. Missing files are problems, like a schema error (C-011). It re-reads each must file's blob sha and records any that changed since prepare in the receipt's `standardsChanged[]`, as an audit note: a changed standard is never a problem, because the task read the current file.
  4. It refuses, as hold `stage-failed` rather than a correction, any change the task made under `backlog*.json`, `decisions.json`, `build/**` outside its own task folder, `.claude/**`, `docker/stack/.staged/**` or any `.git/hooks/**`, for either executor (C-010).
  5. For a read-only stage it compares each touched repo's tree fingerprint with the one `stage prepare` recorded, **before** it writes anything. The fingerprint is HEAD plus `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no`, scoped to the increment's plan and manifest paths, with the registry's `generated` globs (including `workareas/shared/<p>/**`) excluded. A difference fails the stage: a read-only task changed a repo. Another session's untracked files, and the programme's own state writes, do not (C-065).
  6. It stamps provenance for distil outputs from the task's identity.
  7. On success it writes `output.json` and `receipt.json` (8.1). The receipt carries the executor, `attemptsToAccept`, `completed` for checking stages (C-006), `outputSha256` and `acceptMac`, an HMAC over the task id and `outputSha256` keyed by the gitignored `build/.accept-key` that `run start` creates, so a hand-written receipt fails the audit (C-012). Codex receipts also record `codexVersion`, `model`, `effort` and the config hash (C-009). The receipt carries `counts` (`findings`, `standing`, `fixNow`, `added`, `discovered`, `newAtoms`), which the span walker's `when` rules read, and `upstreamShas`, the `outputSha256` of every upstream output the task read (C-077). A fix task's accept saves its diff from the pre-fix fingerprint for fix-verify. The MAC holds only while no agent can read the key or write a receipt. Accept's refusal in step 4 catches a task that changes `build/**` outside its folder after the fact; the per-call guards must stop the read or write as it happens. **Owed to Sam before inc-014:** extend guard-edits and the secrets-read check to cover `build/runs/**/receipt.json`, `build/runs/**/output.json` and `build/.accept-key`, because agents cannot edit the workspace settings or hooks. This is owed item 4 in 9.2.
  8. On rejection it exits 1 with the problems, moves the draft to `rejected-<n>.json`, writes `input/extras/accept-problems-<n>.json` and marks the task `needs-correction` in `runner.json`. **Both executors get the same three correction rounds** (C-004): a Claude task corrects its draft and runs accept again; `tim backlog codex run` resumes the Codex task's own session with "tim rejected your output: <problems verbatim>. Correct it and write your final result." A semantic check that `--output-schema` cannot express is therefore corrected on Codex exactly as on Claude.
- **`stage accept --stage <stageDir> --finalise`** runs once after the fan-out (from `advance`, in-process) and applies every shared side effect under the shared lock: it writes `build/plans/<id>.md` from the plan's `planMarkdown` and persists `state.plan` (including `reposAdded[]`, C-031), moves atom, unit and increment files distil tasks wrote inside their own folders into place, applies verify verdicts to atom files, and records counts (C-005).
- **`stage audit`** re-derives the attempt from disk:
  - **Receipts.** Every prepared task has an accepted receipt whose executor equals the executor `stage.json` recorded for it (C-003), whose `acceptMac` verifies and whose `outputSha256` matches `output.json` (C-012). Every review-style, review-code, consistency, verify, fix-verify and acceptance receipt says `completed: true` (C-006).
  - **Inputs.** Each task received exactly its stage's input matrix. No acceptance task holds the plan or a plan output (C-021).
  - **Reading.** Every review, implement and fix receipt carries a clean reading record (C-011).
  - **Standards.** The manifest's `rulesGap[]`, the changed files whose rules were missing from the writing task's standards list, is empty, for either executor (C-037).
  - **Review.** The review tasks match the manifest: a style and a code task per content-changed file, and one consistency task. The consistency output has a verdict for every touched repo and a seam verdict other than `not-checked` for every seam in the plan (C-023).
  - **Findings.** There is a verify task per file with findings, plus one verify-consistency task when the consistency output has findings, or a recorded fail-open. Every verified finding, consistency findings included, has a judge ruling (C-024). A fix and a fix-verify exist when the judge ruled fixes, and every fix is applied and confirmed.
  - **Repairs.** Every repair diff (ladder-repair, e2e-repair, ci-fix) has a repair-review receipt (C-021).
  - **Invariants.** Every plan invariant has a result: a rung fact record for a `command` invariant, or an acceptance verdict for a `judgement` invariant. A mechanical invariant is never a model's claim (C-008).
  - **Ladder and secrets.** Every ladder rung is `pass` or `skipped-undefined`, and the secrets scan is clean.
  - **Acceptance.** Every acceptance criterion is `met`, and every `e2e` or `contract` member criterion is mapped by the acceptance task to a passed spec in the integration-proof record (C-020, C-021).
  - **Facts.** Every mechanical sub-step the stage table requires has a fact record whose `factMac` verifies (C-082).
  - **Observation.** Every outward action has an `observed` record: remote ref, PR, check, merge or ticket (C-076).
  - **Spans and skips.** Every span task's `upstreamShas` equal its upstreams' accepted outputs, and every skipped conditional task's `when` is false on disk (C-077, C-081).
  - **Integration proof** (R7, C-020). An increment that touches a `product` repo has a green `state.integrationProof` record from the tests repo's end-to-end or contract suite, run against the real stack, locally or in CI. In it: every touched repo's service built from its head for the attempt, or an image resolved to its branch tag and never a `:latest` fallback; every untouched service from `:latest` or a clean checkout of its base; and at least one new or changed spec, unless the plan's evidence check names an existing spec that already proves the criterion. A `hygiene` increment in one product repo needs a regression proof instead: the rung that was red is green and, when the stack serves the repo, the existing suite is green (C-032). A tooling `feat` or `fix` needs the recorded exit code and output of its `e2e` run through the tool's real entry point against a fixture programme (C-033).
  - **Repos and branches.** Every repo in the manifest has the same `state.branches` name, equal to the delivery or increment branch, plus a baseline record and ladder results, including any repo the plan added (C-031). Commits exist for every touched repo, and PR and CI state match the delivery strategy.

  It returns `{ok, problems[]}`. `status done --done-by build` refuses unless `ok`. A dropped reviewer, a skipped verification, an ignored fixer or an agent's "green" therefore cannot reach `done`, even if a step agent misrelays a count.
- **`stage schemas`** exports each stage schema as Codex-strict JSON Schema: every property in `required`, optional values nullable, `additionalProperties: false`. A contract test asserts the export is Codex-strict (S §7.3). There is one copy of every schema: the zod source in tim. Claude agents validate through the same accept command, so no script needs the full schema. **zod and the export accept the same drafts** (C-016): stage schemas use `.nullish()`, never `.optional()`, and a contract test per stage schema asserts that a document valid under the export with every nullable field `null` passes zod, and the reverse after normalisation. `.refine` is allowed only when the refinement is also stated in the neutral stage brief; a test lists every refinement and checks the brief names it. Without this, every Codex draft could fail where Claude's pass.
- **`stage manifest`** lists, per touched repo, the files changed since the attempt's pinned baseline heads (`state.heads` from stage 4, recorded in `stage.json` so the audit re-derives the same set), plus the index and the working tree (C-068). Under programme delivery a diff from `main` would carry every earlier increment's files, and review cost would grow every increment. It reviews wip-committed work on resume, which fixes the staged-only gap (B §3.5). It lists its mechanical exclusions: pure renames (`git diff -M100%`), lockfiles and the registry's `generated` globs for the repo, which for the workspace repo include `workareas/shared/<p>/**` (C-058, C-065). It records `rulesGap[]` (C-037) and refuses changes to the protected paths above (C-010). It proves each repo's HEAD equals the pinned HEAD and that no remote ref moved during the stage, so no task of either executor committed or pushed unnoticed (judge 3). A changed repo that is not in the plan holds the increment `stage-failed`, naming the repo (C-031).

tim runs git through `src/exec`. It writes only to local repos: branches, merges of base, commits, wip commits, named-path stashes and the state commit, each tested on git fixtures. It never pushes, opens or merges a PR, or writes to Jira itself; it generates those as visible calls (C-075). It never runs `gh`.

### 4.6 Tools outside tim, and the canonical rails

| Tool | Status | Purpose and contract |
|---|---|---|
| `tools/codex/home/` (`config.toml`, `hooks.json`) | new | The pipeline-owned Codex home (C-009). `config.toml` names the model explicitly and carries no MCP servers, plugins, notify, memories or skills, so no `codex exec` inherits `~/.codex` and parity is not skewed by hidden context. `hooks.json` runs `tools/codex/guard.sh` on PreToolUse (C-010). `auth.json` is symlinked in at run time by `tim backlog codex run`, never committed. The Codex runner itself is tim (4.2, C-078): `run-stage.sh` is never written |
| `tools/codex/guard.sh` | new | Adapts Codex's PreToolUse hook input to `guard-bash.sh`, `guard-edits.sh` and the secrets-read check, so Codex tasks meet the same per-call guards as Claude agents. Whether Codex honours a deny from `exec` is probed in inc-015; 8.7 records the result either way (C-010) |
| `tools/backlog/record-read.sh` | new | The committed `PostToolUse` hook on `Read`. It appends `{session_id, transcript_path, file_path, offset, limit}` to `build/reads/<session_id>.jsonl` for the programme being built, and does nothing otherwise. The reading audit uses it for Claude tasks (C-011). Wiring it into `.claude/settings.json` is a change guard-edits forbids agents to make, so it is listed in "Owed to you" |
| `tools/backlog/self-test.sh` | new | Runs `--self-test` for `guard.sh` and checks that `guard.sh` and `record-read.sh` are mode 100755 at HEAD (C-066). For a self-hosted build it runs from the pinned copy (C-064, C-067). Exits 1 naming any that fail. Every other lifecycle tool is tim, tested by vitest (C-079) |
| `tools/npm/npm-in-repo.sh --clone` | ported | Installs in clones without corrupting lockfiles, for people. `tim backlog pin` follows the same canonical-path rule itself and prints the pinned prefix, `npm --prefix ~/git/defra/trade-imports-workspace/workareas/clones/pipeline-pin/tim run --silent tim --`, the allowlisted `npm --prefix` form; a test asserts its stdout parses as JSON |
| `tools/style/file-topics.sh`, `tools/style/bake-rules-bundle.sh`, `tools/review/detect-tech.sh` | evolved | Their path-to-topic map (`file-topics.sh:34-58`), topic-to-files lists (`bake-rules-bundle.sh:37-73`) and detectors with best-practice lists (`detect-tech.sh:53-265`) move into `.claude/skills/code-style/assets/routing.json` and `.claude/skills/review/assets/routing.json` as data (`fileContains`, `anyFileContains`, `fileExists`). The scripts become thin jq readers of those files, with golden tests: old output equals new output on every fixture path, so a person running either skill sees no change. Each skill still owns its routing, read live (R2; C-037) |
| `tools/jira/*`, `tools/confluence/page.sh`, `tools/refine/prepare-refinement.sh`, `tools/github-actions/get-failure.sh` | unchanged | Called live. `scripts/sonar/sonar-check-pending.sh` leaves the pipeline: its records come from a hook that never fires for the build's `git -C` pushes (C-076) |
| `tools/parity/*.sh` | frozen for `parity-v1` | Keep working for the four frozen corpora; `scaffold-corpus.sh` reads the registry at its new path |

**Canonical rails.** `docs/agent-rails/GUARD-RAILS.md` holds four texts:

- **GUARD RAILS:** the `FA:102-117` superset. It adds: run tim as `tim ...`, or with the pinned prefix the prompt gives; never write JSON state, use the named tim command; exit 75 from a runner means call it again; stash only by path when another session owns files.
- **PUSH RULE:** the refspec push and branch proof (`FA:125-132`), with the proof and a fast-forward check run by tim before any push is generated: `land` for the increment, `record` for the state branch, and `advance` for the wip commit pushed before a red hold under `programme` and `per-increment` (7.6); the skill's binding-commit push (7.6) uses the same refspec form and is its own call in the launching session; no push is ever generated with `--force` or a `+` refspec. Force-push is refused by this rule and by the refspec form: the deny list's `git push --force` and `-f` patterns do not match `git -C <repo> push …`, so they cannot be relied on (C-042).
- **COMMIT RULE:** commit with `-F <file>`, ending with the trailer the prompt gives (`FA:134-138`, with the trailer from args). tim makes every commit inside a run (`land`, `record`, and the wip commits `advance` makes before a red hold); no agent in a run commits. The one commit outside a run is the skill's binding commit in the launching session (7.6). It states the deviation plainly: the pipeline commits without `sonar analyze --staged`, because `sonar` is not runnable by agents or from tim, and the pipeline's SonarCloud evidence comes from PR checks (stage 24). CLAUDE.md rule 3 asks for the local scan; this deviation is listed in "Owed to you" for Sam to accept or refuse (C-042).
