- **STEP AGENT:** the prompt in `consolidation/decision.md` 3.2: loop `tim backlog advance … --agent <id> --json`; on exit 75 run the same command again; on `act` run each command exactly as given, one Bash call each, stopping at the first non-zero exit; on `run`, `handback` or `stop` return the `step` object as printed, with every exit code and the `wrote` paths tim printed. Never run any other command, change a command, or read or edit a file. It is issued to Haiku at low effort and makes no decision (C-074).

Each workflow inlines the four texts as constants between marker comments, because scripts cannot import (G4 §2.2). The test `tim/src/backlog/workflow-contract.test.js` parses every `.claude/workflows/*.js` with acorn, using `sourceType: 'module'` and `allowReturnOutsideFunction: true` (a workflow script is a module that returns at top level; a fixture uses both, C-069), and asserts six things:

1. The four rails constants, the step agent's prompt included, equal the doc byte for byte.
2. Every brief, profile and persona a script names exists.
3. Every script parses.
4. No neutral stage brief (`.claude/workflows/stages/*.md`) mentions sandbox, Codex, Claude, GUARD RAILS or the Write tool: executor-only text belongs in `executors/claude.md` or `executors/codex.md` (C-007).
5. The skill contract (8.5): each of the `review` and `code-style` skills' `SKILL.md`, read live, still lists every worker the persona map runs, at the granularity the stage table fans out, and still has its "When a workflow runs this skill" section. No script, brief or profile contains text copied from either skill.
6. The span walker helper, byte-identical between marker comments in both workflow scripts, extracted with acorn and run on fixture graphs with a fake `agent()`: a task starts only when every `after` task is accepted and its `when` holds; a dead or refused upstream ends the walk for its dependants; a misrelayed count or `stage.json` sha stops the walk (C-070, C-077).

There is no stage-order item: the order lives only in the stage table, read by `advance` (C-002). Drift then fails tim CI instead of reaching a run (codex; judges 1 and 3).

**New scripts and the execute bit** (C-066). `git add --chmod=+x` sets only the index, the Write tool creates files with mode 644, and `chmod` is denied. So every new script goes through this sequence before any agent runs it: `git add <path>`, `git update-index --chmod=+x <path>`, commit, `git checkout -- <path>` (which rewrites the working-tree file with the committed mode), then check that the commit output says "create mode 100755" (`guard-bash.sh:106-129`). The tool self-test fails if `tools/codex/guard.sh` or `tools/backlog/record-read.sh` is not 100755 at HEAD. They are the only two bash scripts the pipeline adds (C-079).

### 4.7 tim rails compliance

All tim work follows `tim/CLAUDE.md`:

- vitest, tested on input and output: spawn the CLI and assert stdout, or call the function and assert its return;
- git fixtures through `src/test-support/git-fixtures.js` (`createBareRepo`, `pushCommit`, `createFatClone`), including write operations: branch cut tracking a remote, clean and conflicting merges of base, commits found again by trailer, named-path stash, and the refusal to generate a push from `main` or a non-fast-forward;
- Confluence and Jira acquisition through the existing clients, tested with undici MockAgent;
- octokit writes (`pulls.create`, `pulls.update`, `pulls.merge`) and `jira-client` POSTs tested with undici MockAgent (`test-support/http-mock.js`);
- a fake Codex binary under `test-support/` (`fake-codex.js`) for `tim backlog codex run`;
- no shell-out to `gh`, `jq`, `curl` or `../tools/*.sh`;
- a sibling `*.test.js` for every new module;
- GDS plain English in every message.

New dependencies, all library-first:

- `picomatch` for rule globs and the routing data's path patterns;
- `sharp` for `source crop` (C-039);
- `fflate` to unzip docx, xlsx and pptx for `source acquire` (C-040);
- `acorn`, as a development dependency, for the workflow contract test.

None for the consolidation (C-074 to C-082): processes run through the existing execa wrapper (`exec/exec.js`), and the Codex runner's descendant walk parses `ps -A -o pid=,ppid=` through it.

### 4.8 The file ledger

Every file this design adds or reshapes, with what it does better in use. New surface, file count and build effort are not reasons either way (R8; C-001).

| File | What it does better in use |
|---|---|
| `tim/src/backlog/**` (the parity core moved with its history) | One tested core serves both parity corpora and requirement backlogs: ids stay stable across re-ingests, rulings cannot be undone by a re-distillation, and every write shares one lock, op-id and lost-update discipline (4.1, C-043) |
| `tim/src/backlog/profiles/requirements-v2.js`, `requirements-v2.classes.js` | Kinds, slots and id rules become data a test can read, so a requirement backlog is validated strictly while parity stays additive-tolerant |
| `tim/src/backlog/advance/**` | Every build and distil transition rule, hold, budget and resume point is decided by one command tested on fixture folders, and a Workflow run and a Codex-orchestrated session cannot follow different rules. It also executes every local mechanical step, so a run spends one cheap agent per gap between judgement spans rather than one per action (C-002, C-074, C-075) |
| `tim/src/backlog/macros/**` (`prepare`, `rung`, `check`, `prove`, `land`, `ci-wait`, `record`, `run open`, `run close`, `pin`) | Every deterministic step (branches, sync, rungs, the stack, manifests, commits, the state commit) is one tested command that people, the step agent and a Codex-orchestrated session all call the same way; a typical increment spends 4 cheap step agents instead of 49 watchers and 9 relays, and the number does not grow with repos, rungs, shards or PRs (C-074, C-075, C-080) |
| `tim/src/backlog/git/**` | Branch cut, merge of base, increment and wip commits, named-path stashes and the state commit are tested on fixtures (conflict, dirty tree, detached HEAD, commit found again by trailer), which guards them better than a Haiku typing the command; a conflicting merge leaves no partial merge (C-075) |
| `tim/src/backlog/codex/runner.js` | Slicing, resume, correction rounds, the stage lock and the descendant-tree kill become tested JavaScript run in-process by `advance`, so a Codex phase costs no agent (including today's `pkill ... ; grep` compound that breaks its own rails, `increment-build-loop.js:762`) (C-004, C-014, C-015, C-078) |
| `tim/src/backlog/facts.js` | Every mechanical result is a MACed fact record written by tim, so no agent's relayed exit code is ever trusted, and resume starts at the first sub-step without one (C-082) |
| The span walker (both workflow scripts, between marker comments) | Judgement stages with nothing mechanical between them start as soon as their inputs are accepted, with no agent spent between them; `advance` re-derives the span on its next call and the audit re-derives every skip (C-077, C-081) |
| `tim github pr ensure-draft \| checks \| merge`, `tim jira create \| transition` | Each outward action is a library-first, tested command run as its own visible call, per repo, with its refusals (exit 3 on a merged or closed PR, `--expect-head` on merge, every PR green) in code rather than in a bash helper; the effect is then observed, never relayed (C-076, C-079) |
| `tim/src/backlog/standards.js` | One resolver over both skills' routing data, rule globs, pointers, the CLAUDE.md chain and the memory index, with each source's blob sha recorded: both executors provably read the same standards, and a Codex writer gets rules for files it creates, not only those it planned (C-037) |
| `tim/src/backlog/rule.js` | Defaults, reversals, surface amendments and re-opening built work are each one validated command, so every call in report section 2 is paste-ready and no ruling can silently strike built work (C-044, C-047, C-031, C-055) |
| `tim/src/backlog/stages/**` | Each stage's contract, input matrix, schema and transitions are tested data both executors meet through the same accept, with three correction rounds each and receipts no agent can forge (C-004, C-012, C-016) |
| `tim/src/backlog/reading.js` | The reading audit is deterministic, the same rule for Claude `Read` ranges and Codex command output, and is enforced at accept rather than discovered after the increment (C-011) |
| `tim/src/backlog/sources/{acquire,crop}.js` | Word, spreadsheet and slide sources become readable text with row, cell and slide markers, and every visual unit carries a hashed crop, so the usual loose sources and the pictures a visual decision needs are never lost (C-039, C-040) |
| `tim/src/backlog/render/{page,card,sections,answer-sheet,decisions}.js` | A requirement card lists N sources with role and tier; the answer sheet stands alone; the decisions page is a view of the JSON, never a third hand-written copy (C-046, C-052) |
| `.claude/workflows/backlog-distil.js` | A thin interpreter over `advance --distil`: model phases run as spans and one cheap step agent per gap runs everything else, and the gates are tested code rather than relays (C-002, C-074) |
| `.claude/workflows/backlog-build.js` | A thin interpreter over `advance`: run (a span), act, handback or stop. Resume and holds cannot diverge from the tested rules (C-002, C-074) |
| `.claude/workflows/stages/*.md` | Written fresh from the 8.1 contract, with no executor text, so neither executor is made worse to reach parity (C-007) |
| `.claude/workflows/executors/{claude.md,codex.md,presets.json}` | Each executor's preamble and deltas in one place the implementor owns, so switching executor needs no backlog edit (R5, R6) |
| `.claude/skills/code-style/assets/routing.json`, `.claude/skills/review/assets/routing.json` | Routing becomes data each skill owns and reads live, testable by golden output and hashable for parity (C-037) |
| `docs/agent-rails/GUARD-RAILS.md` | One canonical text for the rails, enforced byte for byte by a test, so the copies cannot drift again (H §5) |
| `docs/knowledge/journey-frontends.md` | The journey traps become knowledge a programme's registry entry names, read at plan time, instead of sentences in generic prompts (B §8) |
| `tools/codex/home/config.toml`, `hooks.json`, `tools/codex/guard.sh` | Every Codex task runs with a known model and configuration and nothing inherited from `~/.codex`, and meets the same per-call guards as Claude where Codex honours them (C-009, C-010) |
| `tools/backlog/record-read.sh` | Claude's reads are recorded per session, which is what makes the reading audit exact (C-011) |
| `tools/backlog/self-test.sh` | The two remaining bash scripts' `--self-test` and execute bit are checked in one committed rung (C-066, C-079) |
| Distiller personas and `source-types/*.md` | Each persona is cut from a named source for its method (5.9) and carries none of the frontend engine's vocabulary or per-run notes (D §2.2). The distiller owns its prose personas, `PROSE_EDITOR`, `PROSE_CLAIM_VERIFIER` and `ATOM_DEDUPER`, with slot rules and budgets for requirement prose, so a parity edit can never silently change distillation (C-038). `RESLICER` turns migrated layer rows into vertical atoms (C-034). Source types for spreadsheets, slides, prototypes and live services cover the usual loose sources (C-040) |
| `assets/REQUIREMENT-CONTRACT.template.md` | Source roles, precedence per fact kind, the acceptance rule and "what is not a requirement" are stated before any author runs, which is the main defence against recipes entering the requirements |
| `tools/backlog/registry.json` (from `tools/parity/corpora.json`, history kept) | One registry for parity corpora and programmes, and the home for repo how-facts the backlog must not carry (C-058) |

---

## 5. The distiller skill: `requirements-distiller`

### 5.1 Identity

- **Folder:** `.claude/skills/requirements-distiller/`, a new folder holding only the distiller (C-041). Moving journey-builder would put its frontend-engine vocabulary, target profiles and frozen-corpus tools inside the skill every distiller agent loads. Lineage is recorded per persona in 5.9's "Cut from" column instead. `.claude/skills/journey-builder/` stays in place, frozen, with a one-line banner pointing to `requirements-distiller` and `backlog-implementor`; its BUILD mode retires in inc-040.
- **Triggers:** "distil requirements", "digest requirements", "turn these sources into a backlog", "gather the requirements for", "add a source to", "resume the distillation", "rule on the questions", "apply my rulings", "recombine the backlog", "rebuild the requirements report".
- **Not for:**
  - building increments: use `backlog-implementor`;
  - comparing two running services screen by screen: use `parity`, whose corpora can feed the distiller as a source;
  - one ticket: use `ticket-creator` or `ticket-refiner`.
- **Dispatcher:** `tim backlog phase <p> status --json` prints `MODE:` and `Next:` (skill pattern 2). The mode is decided by the same tested command that reads the phase ledger, so the skill and the ledger can never disagree about where a distillation stands.
- **Workflow args:** `{programme, phases: [from, to], at, runLabel, workspace: {abs, tilde}, scope, firstStep?}`, where `scope` optionally narrows to `{sources[], slices[]}` and `firstStep` is the step `tim backlog run open` returned when its light work already reached a span. `run open` prints these args itself. The script parses a string, throws naming any missing key, and logs the resolved configuration as its first act (S §7.1.3). There is no FALLBACK. Like the build, it refuses to start unless the workspace is on the programme's state branch (7.3).
- **State commits.** No distil agent commits. After each launch, `tim backlog run close` commits the programme's tracked files by name on the state branch, with the commit rule (4.6), and returns the state push, when `record` is `commit-push`, as a visible call the skill runs as its own Bash call, with the push rule (C-075, C-076).
- **Every launch** is `tim backlog run open <p> --label <runLabel> --mode distil --at <ISO> --trailer-file <f>`, then the Workflow with the args it printed (passing `firstStep` when it returned one), then `tim backlog run close <p> --label <runLabel>` after the Workflow returns (C-080).

| Mode | When | What the skill does |
|---|---|---|
| `NEW` | No workarea | Interviews with `references/INTERVIEWER.md`: ask only what cannot be discovered, propose then confirm, lettered options, at most five questions (purpose; sources and their roles; delivery strategy and ticketing; who besides Sam may rule; combine `apply` or `propose`). It proposes each repo's `consumes` from the `docker/stack` service dependencies and asks the person to confirm it (C-026). Then `tim backlog init`, `source add`, `acquire`, `seal`, the contract drafted, `tim backlog run open` (passing `firstStep` into args when it returns one), and the first launch of `backlog-distil.js`, which drives `advance --distil` from `heads` to `slice`; after the Workflow returns, `tim backlog run close` |
| `RESUME` | The ledger has an unfinished phase | `tim backlog run open`, then relaunches the range the ledger names, with the same args, and `resumeFromRunId` when they are identical; after the Workflow returns, `tim backlog run close` |
| `ADD-SOURCE` | Sam names another source | Registers, acquires and seals it, then `phase reset characterise --scope source:<id>`. That source is characterised and verified afresh, and everything from `slice` on is reset |
| `REFRESH` | A seal drifted | Re-acquires, runs carryover against the new version, re-runs from `characterise` for that source, and re-verifies atoms that cite changed units |
| `RULE` | Sam gives rulings | Captures the page batch, the answer sheet or conversation, mapping his words to slugs and showing the mapping before writing. Calls `tim backlog rule` per answer, reversal or release, then launches `apply-rulings` (only when effects are pending) and `combine` to `report` |
| `RECOMBINE` | Discovered work was absorbed, or Sam asks for a regroup | `phase reset combine`, then launches `combine` to `report` |
| `REPORT` | Any time | `tim backlog report` only |

### 5.2 Phases, in order

The phase table is profile data in `requirements-v2`. It generalises the parity ledger (`phase.sh:29-44`) with G1 §3's distiller table and the grafts. Distillation runs through `tim backlog advance --distil` (C-002): every tim command in the table, gates included, runs in-process inside `advance`, and model phases come back as `run` steps naming a judgement span. A run of in-process phases between two model spans costs one step agent, and none at the start or end of a launch when its work is light (C-074, C-080). The "Runs in" column names each phase's step run (DM0 to DM10, one cheap step agent each), its judgement span (DJ1 to DJ11, whose later tasks start as soon as their upstream is accepted, C-077), or the launching session ("main"). "Tier" is the Claude default: think is Opus at high effort, doer is Sonnet and step is Haiku at low effort. **Every phase with model tasks can be bound to Codex**, one role at a time (5.11, 8.2). A dagger (†) marks the phases the `distil-codex` preset binds; the others stay on Claude under that preset and are bound with `run bind --role <role>=codex`.

| # | Phase | Needs | Runs in | Tasks and tier | Output | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `setup` | none | main | the skill | workarea, header skeleton, registered sources, contract drafted | contract sections present |
| 1 | `heads` | setup | in-process, DM0 | `tim backlog heads --write` | `distil/heads.json` | re-read with `--strict` before `combine` and `report` |
| 2 | `acquire` | setup | main for pasted material; in-process, DM0, for network sources (a network call is `heavy`) | `source acquire` (with docx, xlsx and pptx conversion, C-040); the skill for pasted material | raw copies, versions, seals | every source acquired or stated absent |
| 3 | `characterise` † | acquire | span DJ1 | one task per source; doer, think for docx, pdf, spreadsheet, slides, transcript, design and policy sources. The task calls `tim backlog source crop` for every visual unit (C-039) | `characterisation.md` (structure found, what could not be read, pointers not followed), `units.json` (token, verbatim text, `readAt`, `{crop, box, sha256}` for visual units, utterance class and speaker for conversation) | every unit has a token and text; every visual unit has a crop file whose hash is recorded |
| 4 | `extract-verify` † | characterise | span DJ1, each started when its own source's characteriser is accepted; P1 then in-process, DM1 | a **different** task per source, same tier as its characteriser (quality; judges 1 to 3) | `extract-verify.json`: per unit `stands`, `corrected`, `missing-added`, `garbled`, `split` or `merged`, plus what it searched for | **P1** `units --strict` |
| 5 | `carryover` † | characterise | span DJ1, after every characteriser, only when earlier work exists | one task, think; only with a previous distillation or an existing-backlog source | `carryover.json`: `carries`, `retired`, `changed` or `recheck`, with the settling unit (G1 §1.9) | every earlier item has a verdict |
| 5a | `reslice` † | carryover; only when `migrate` wrote `distil/reslice.json` | its own span, prepared by DM1; a step agent then prepares DJ2 | RESLICER (think), then a **different** task that verifies each vertical atom (C-034) | one vertical atom per behaviour, `carriedFrom[]` naming every legacy row it absorbs; the layer atoms become `superseded` by it. Built legacy rows stay done; where part of a behaviour is built, the atom cites that code as current-behaviour evidence and still covers the whole behaviour | every unbuilt legacy row is superseded by a verified vertical atom or recorded as not a requirement; ingest refuses a migrated row still layer-split |
| 6 | `slice` | extract-verify, carryover, reslice | span DJ2, `then: close`; P2 in `run close` in the launching session | one task, think | `slices.json`: units from all sources, one cross-cutting slice, and per slice `{id, behaviour, units[], brief, crossCutting}`. `behaviour` is one sentence naming the observer and the outcome; a slice is an area of behaviour, never a repo, layer, technology or source (C-028) | **P2** `slices --strict` |
| 7 | `contract` | slice | main, between the two launches; then `run open` prepares DJ3 and returns `firstStep` | the skill, and Sam for any PER-RUN answer only he can give. It shows the slice list with each behaviour sentence, and the repos with role and `consumes` (C-026, C-028) | `CONTRACT.md` whole | no PER-RUN marker left (`FINDING-CONTRACT.template.md:10-25`) |
| 8 | `author` † | contract | span DJ3, pipelined with `verify` per slice as `after` edges (the pattern of `author-workflow.js:259-278`) | one task per slice, think | atom files; `authoring/<slice>.json` with `notRaised[]`, `premisesDisproved[]`, `unitsCovered[]` | none yet |
| 9 | `verify` † | author (same slice) | span DJ3, started when that slice's author is accepted; the added-atom verifier `when` the first verifier added atoms | a **different** task per slice, think | verdicts stamped into each atom (`stands`, `corrected`, `disputed`, `added`); a verdict on every "not a requirement" dismissal (agree, or dispute with an added atom, C-049); an `edgeChecks` record on every cross-repo `dependsOn` edge (C-025); added atoms go to a second verifier | every atom verified by a task other than its author; every dismissal ruled on |
| 10 | `yield` † | verify | yield in-process, DM3; gap auditor then its verifier per flagged slice, span DJ4; the gates in-process, DM4. With no flagged slice DJ4 and DM4 go and DM3 prepares reconcile | `tim backlog yield`, then a GAP_AUDITOR task (think) per flagged slice, whose additions a separate verifier checks | added atoms, or a written reason the slice is genuinely quiet | **P3** `yield --strict`; `coverage --strict` |
| 11 | `reconcile` | yield | span DJ5 | one task, think | conflicts, precedence picks, assumptions, invariants lifted (cross-cutting rules stated once), disputed atoms marked, variant atoms, draft questions | every disagreement has a conflict; every `gap` criterion has a question or an assumption |
| 12 | `panel` | reconcile | precedence picks in-process, DM5; span DJ6, only when a conflict survives precedence; `rule --by panel` and escalations in-process, DM6 | per docket three judges then a chair, think, one docket at a time; then one critic | `panel/<docket>.json`, `panel/critic.json`; rulings through `rule --by panel`; escalations as must-answer questions | every docket item has exactly one ruling or escalation; no standing ruling breached |
| 13 | `dedupe` | panel or reconcile | `duplicates` in-process, DM6 (DM5 when no panel ran); span DJ7 | `tim backlog duplicates` (all pairs, in-process), then one task, think, following the distiller's own `ATOM_DEDUPER.md` (C-038) | survivors kept; absorbed atoms `superseded`, naming the survivor; nothing deleted | every candidate answered, with what was looked at |
| 14 | `ingest-atoms` | dedupe | in-process, DM7 | `tim backlog ingest --atoms` | `requirements[]` | **P7** refuses unverified, homeless and cyclic atoms; freezes statements at this first adopted ingest; runs the atom-level P11 checks: tests-only atoms and the five layer-chain rules of 3.12 (C-025) |
| 15 | `trace` | ingest-atoms | in-process, DM7 | `tim backlog trace --strict` | trace matrix | **P4** |
| 16 | `critic` | trace | span DJ8, with 17 | one fresh task, think, that never saw the authors' notes | `critique.json`: gaps, blind spots per source kind, counter-numbers; new atoms go back to `author` and `verify` for their slice, at most two rounds | none; findings enter report sections 3 and 11 |
| 17 | `questions` | critic | span DJ8, `when: critic.newAtoms = 0` (otherwise the span ends and `advance` returns the new atoms to author and verify); lint and `rule --by default` in-process, DM8 | QUESTION_EDITOR (think), then QUESTION_CRITIC (doer, a different task); `tim backlog question lint` (in-process); then `rule --by default` for every defaulted question (C-044) | question records; clusters; classes (`must-answer` or `defaulted`); blast radius; a `defaulted` decision per defaulted question | **P8** lint and critic pass on every question |
| 18 | `combine` | questions | candidates in-process, DM8; combiner then combine verifier, span DJ9 (the combiner again and a second verifier only when the first found failures); ingest and check in-process, DM9 | `tim backlog candidates --combine` (in-process); COMBINER (think); COMBINE_VERIFIER (doer, a different task), which also writes `edgeChecks` on lifted edges (C-025); `ingest --increments` and `check --strict` (in-process) | `distil/increments/*.json`, each with predeclared `reverseEffects` (C-047); `combination.declined[]` | **P9** every adopted atom in exactly one increment; every candidate applied or declined with a reason; ceiling respected. **P11** no layer split; every product increment carries its integration proof |
| 19 | `prose` | combine | span DJ10, each claim verifier started when its editor is accepted; rewrites through `set` in-process, DM10 | per section group: PROSE_EDITOR (doer), then PROSE_CLAIM_VERIFIER (doer, a different task), the distiller's own personas (C-038) | rewritten prose fields through `tim backlog set`, within each slot's budget; claim-check records | every machine-written prose field has both records; a failed claim check reverts the rewrite; `report --lint` slot budgets pass |
| 20 | `report` | prose | in-process, DM10; then span DJ11, `then: close`; the final render in `run close` | `tim backlog report` and `report --lint`; then COLD_READER (doer), a fresh task that reads each section on its own (C-050); a section that renders empty is recorded "not read: empty" (C-081) | `report/` | render exits 0; every count derived; lint passes; the cold reader finds no term a section uses without defining |
| R1 | `apply-rulings` | a ruling flagged effects-pending | one span plus one step agent, only when a free-text ruling is pending | one task, think: RULING_APPLIER | an effects file applied through `rule --effects-file`; new wording as proposed variant atoms that a verifier checks | every new decision has effects, or "no effect because" a stated reason |

**Why this cut of fan-out** (G1 §4 item 3; S §7.7). Characterise and extract-verify fan out per source, because that work is local to a source. Authoring fans out per slice of the service, so one author sees every source's view of that part. Reconcile, dedupe, critic, question editing and combining are single agents, because cross-cutting judgement spread over N workers is anti-pattern A5.

**Gates run inside `advance --distil`**, in-process, as tested code (C-002). A failed gate returns `{status: stop, stopReason: gate:<name>}` with the command's list of what is missing, and the workflow returns it. The skill then acts: it re-briefs a slice, adds a source or asks Sam. A gate is where a span ends, and so where a step agent is spent.

**Agents per distil run** (C-074, C-077, C-080; `consolidation/decision.md` section 4). Under `distil-claude`, a typical run spends 56 judgement tasks (4 characterise and 4 extract-verify, 1 slice, 14 author and verify, 2 gap, 1 reconcile, 5 panel, 1 dedupe, 3 critic and questions, 2 combine, 8 prose, 11 cold readers) and 10 step agents (DM0, DM1, DM3 to DM10): 66, against 81 before. With no flagged slice DJ4 and DM4 go: 64. The launching session runs `run open`, the contract and `run close` and spends no agent.

### 5.3 The run contract

`distil/CONTRACT.md` is seeded from `.claude/skills/requirements-distiller/assets/REQUIREMENT-CONTRACT.template.md`, which is derived from `tools/parity/templates/FINDING-CONTRACT.template.md`. It must be whole before any author runs (the phase 7 gate). Its sections, with PER-RUN marking the ones only a person can finish:

1. Purpose and audience.
2. Source roles and precedence per fact kind (PER-RUN). This is the main defence against recipes entering the requirements (S §6.1 item 3).
3. What is not a requirement (PER-RUN): recipes, house style, legacy architecture, a target data model, code-level consistency, and a store, collection, endpoint or list that only this programme's own frontend reads (that is one layer of the behaviour that reads it, C-029), each named by example. This is the section "that saves the most agent time" (`FINDING-CONTRACT.template.md:195-215`).
4. What acceptance may name (PER-RUN): external routes, consumed document shapes, mandated GDS components and quoted copy.
5. The atom grain rule, verbatim: "One requirement, one thing a person could build and prove. If your sentence contains 'and also', you have two requirements." There is one exception: a whole absent page or block is one atom with its content enumerated inside it (`FINDING_AUTHOR.md:130-137`). The grain is also vertical (R7): an atom is the thinnest end-to-end behaviour a user or system can observe, across every repo that behaviour needs. "The backend half of X" and "the tests for X" are never atoms.
6. The slices, each with its behaviour sentence; the cross-cutting slice, and what every other slice must not raise (PER-RUN). The repos, each with its role and `consumes`, proposed from the `docker/stack` service dependencies and confirmed by a person (C-026, C-028).
7. Confidence semantics and the role checks (3.12).
8. Question rules: the must-answer categories, the two behaviours (must-answer, or defaulted with the default applied at once, C-044), and the four parts.
9. Volatile values never to quote or compare (PER-RUN).
10. The firewall of paths not to read, and knowledge already paid for (PER-RUN; `RUN-BRIEF.md` §1 and §7, G1 §1.8).
11. Combination mode (`apply` or `propose`), and any ceiling override with its reason (PER-RUN).

### 5.4 Pass 1: authoring, verification and completeness

**The author** (REQUIREMENT_AUTHOR, cut from `FINDING_AUTHOR.md`):

- reads the contract whole;
- checks its brief ("disproving one is a result, not a problem");
- stays inside its slice;
- quotes verbatim or marks `gap`;
- writes each atom as a thin vertical slice, naming every repo the behaviour needs, with at least one criterion a person could see proved end to end (R7). Its brief lists the header repos with role and `consumes`, so it can see which repos a behaviour passes through (C-030);
- chooses the atom's kind by who observes it (3.4, C-029): `data` only for a shape a system outside the programme, or an operator, observes at a boundary; `reference-data` for values a user sees or a rule applies; `integration` only for an exchange with a system outside the programme's repos;
- writes observable acceptance;
- runs its own falsifier before filing;
- never writes `backlog.json`;
- returns `notRaised[]` and `premisesDisproved[]` ("PROMOTE WHAT YOU NOTICE", `author-workflow.js:144-147`).

**The verifier** (REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER, cut from `FINDING_VERIFIER.md`, the trace workflow's verify stage and the ticket-refiner criteria rubric):

- never verifies a slice it wrote;
- asks only whether each atom is correct, observable, single, sourced and a vertical slice, merging any atom that only tests, documents or supplies one layer of another into the atom it serves;
- merges an atom whose only observer is another atom of the same programme (for example "the backend stores X", read only by this programme's frontend) into the behaviour that observes it (C-029);
- asks of every criterion "which repo would a person have to change for this to be observed?", and adds any repo the author missed to the atom's surface; the report counts these per increment (C-030);
- writes an `edgeChecks` record `{on, observableWithout, why, by}`, with `by` its own task identity, on every cross-repo `dependsOn` edge: "can this atom's acceptance be observed at its boundary before `on` lands?" (C-025);
- refuses a `hygiene` atom whose statement adds behaviour: hygiene restores what was already promised, and is the only single-repo product atom allowed (C-032);
- rules on every "not a requirement" dismissal the author made in its slice: agree, or dispute with an added atom. The author alone never decides that a unit drops (C-049);
- treats a missing requirement as a finding (`added`);
- carries the recipe smell and the acceptance rule in its rubric;
- does "not invent corrections to seem useful" (`trace-to-requirements.workflow.js:850-927`);
- writes a verification line on every atom, including untouched ones, because "nothing in this pipeline distinguishes a verifier that found nothing from a verifier that looked at nothing" (`FINDING_VERIFIER.md:83-108`).

**Completeness proofs.** All are mechanical and all appear in report section 6 with their results (quality; judge 1).

| Proof | Command | Fails when |
|---|---|---|
| P1 every source read | `units --strict` | A source has no units and no stated absence, a unit has no extract-verify verdict, or a pointer unit on a requirement-role source is not registered as a source, stated absent with a reason, or raised as a question (C-040) |
| P2 every unit owned once | `slices --strict` | A unit is in no slice or in two, or there is not exactly one cross-cutting slice |
| P3 no truncated author | `yield --strict` | A slice is under 0.4 of the median without an audit, or an atom is homeless, strayed, unverified or self-verified |
| P4 every unit accounted for | `trace --strict`, `coverage --strict` | A unit is not cited, ruled not a requirement, a side of a conflict, excluded or a duplicate; or a "not a requirement" dismissal has no verifier verdict (C-049) |
| P5 every criterion evidenced or questioned | `check --strict` | A criterion has no evidence and no gap link, or its confidence disagrees with its source's role |
| P6 cross-cutting stated once | `check --strict` | The same criterion text appears in three or more atoms instead of an invariant reference |
| P7 independent verification | ingest | `verifiedBy` equals `authoredBy`, or names a run nobody registered |
| P8 questions well formed and closed | `question lint` and the critic record | A `gap` criterion or a needs-a-person conflict has no question, or a question fails lint or the critic |
| P9 combination accounted | `check --strict` | An adopted atom is in no increment or in two, a candidate has no decision, or a group breaks the ceiling |
| P10 evidence still true | `source check`, `heads --strict` | A seal or head moved since `heads`. Drift is reported, not blocking, except that `combine` refuses while a cited code blob has changed and its atom is not re-verified |
| P11 vertical slices | `check --strict` | A row is tests-only; a row breaks one of the five layer-chain rules of 3.12 (a `dependsOn` edge between rows with disjoint non-empty product repo sets; a chain of two or more edges from provider to consumer by `consumes`, or ending in a tests-only row; a cross-repo edge with no `edgeChecks` record, or one whose dependency is provider-only and not observable by its own actor; class `test` on a non-hygiene row), unless a decision names the edge; a product increment has no integration-proof criterion or no tests repo, other than a single-repo `hygiene` increment with a regression criterion; or a tooling `feat` or `fix` has no `e2e` criterion running the tool's real entry point (3.12, R7; C-025, C-032, C-033) |

### 5.5 Reconcile, panel and escalation

- **Precedence first.** The reconciler applies `precedence[]` per fact kind and records every pick as a conflict with `resolution.by: precedence`. Each pick appears in report section 2 as a call Sam may reverse.
- **The panel is triggered** only by conflicts precedence cannot settle that are not in an escalation category. With none, the phase is marked done with the note "no conflict needed a panel" (Q12).
- **Shape** (EUDPA-409 `panel-*.json`, `panel/README.md:11-22`). The reconciler groups conflicts into themed dockets. For each docket, three judges rule in parallel and a chair reconciles. One critic then reads across all dockets for contradictions, vocabulary drift and breaches of standing rulings, and the chair folds in the fixes. The applier is `tim backlog rule --by panel`, not an agent editing JSON.
- **Concurrency.** Dockets run one at a time, so no more than three Opus judges run at once, within the Opus cap of five (S §7.4).
- **Authority** (Sam question S1; default A). The panel may rule anything the sources, the precedence table and Sam's standing rulings can settle. It must escalate, as a must-answer question carrying its recommended option:
  - security, access control, data integrity, legal or policy;
  - a change of scope;
  - anything contradicting one of Sam's rulings;
  - any call whose reversal would touch work already built.

  Every panel ruling appears in report section 2 with the vote and "To reverse".

### 5.6 Questions reach Sam well asked

**QUESTION_EDITOR** (think) does six things:

1. It clusters raw questions and merges duplicates into one decision with many callers (CHED-P's 16 etag blocks become one question, F P5).
2. It splits anything that is two decisions.
3. It writes the four parts, with the sources per option; tim derives each option's counts by role and prints each source's precedence tier (C-050).
4. It classes each question as one of two behaviours (C-044): `must-answer` (no default; it blocks what it touches) or `defaulted` (a default applied at once by `rule --by default`). It prefers `defaulted` with an assumption wherever the options do not change what is built (F K22). Security, access control, data integrity, legal and policy questions are always `must-answer`. When the default differs from the recommendation it writes `defaultWhy`.
5. It ranks by blast radius: increments affected times reversal cost (judge 1).
6. It attaches the crop of every cited visual unit (C-039). For every option that changes behaviour it writes variant atoms whose acceptance encodes the option, and effects that supersede to them (C-044).

**QUESTION_CRITIC** (doer, a different task) fails a question that:

- is not a question;
- bundles two decisions;
- has overlapping options or leaves an obvious answer out;
- states a vague default ("further discussion"), or is `defaulted` while its category demands `must-answer`;
- has a default whose effects do not do what its consequence text says (C-044);
- leads with an id or an unexplained abbreviation instead of a headline;
- cannot be answered by its audience;
- restates a settled decision.

**`tim backlog question lint`** checks the mechanics:

- the headline is a question of 90 characters or fewer;
- there are at least two options, each with evidence or marked "no source";
- a default is present unless the question is must-answer, and `defaultWhy` is present when the default differs from `recommended` (C-044);
- no two options have identical effects, and every option that changes behaviour carries an effect that makes the change: `supersede` to a variant atom for a new behaviour, or `adopt`, `park`, `reject`, `defer-increment` or `drop-increment` for an atom or increment that already exists. An option whose only effect is `set-assumption` passes only when the current atoms already encode it (3.6, C-044);
- a question with an assumption sets it to the option's own letter in every option, so the assumption always names the option in force;
- a question in a visual cluster carries a crop, with box and hash, for every cited visual unit (C-039);
- the cluster is set and the blast radius is derivable;
- every effect names an existing atom.

### 5.7 Pass 2: combining

**The question.** The COMBINER generalises `DUPLICATE_SWEEPER.md` one step up its own scale (G1 §1.5). Dedupe asks "would one person, doing one piece of work, close both?" Combine asks "is this different work one person should do together, as one reviewable unit, with a red in one still attributable to it?"

**Candidates.** `tim backlog candidates --combine` is deterministic and generalises `duplicates.js`. It proposes pairs and groups that share a surface area, the same `consistentWith` target, a shared decision or assumption, or the same acceptance boundary (one end-to-end journey slice or one contract test). It also proposes neighbours in the dependency graph, and an atom that only proves or documents another. Every signal is about behaviour and carries a signal id. Sharing repos is not a signal: under R7 almost every product atom touches the same repos, so it would be either no signal or a licence to combine unrelated behaviour (C-027). Each candidate is scored and explained, and exclusions are applied with the rule named.

**What the combiner reads** (C-027). `COMBINER` reads only the atoms, the ledgers and the `candidates --combine` output: never `build/plans`, DESIGN documents or code. It groups by what a person would observe together, not by what would be convenient to implement together, and `combination.why` must cite candidate signal ids.

**Rules** (header `combination.rules`; defaults from `combination-proposal.md:13-15`, restated in requirement terms, F P13):

| Rule id | Rule |
|---|---|
| `cr-same-surface` | Members share a surface area someone observes: a page group, endpoint group, workflow, command group or skill |
| `cr-same-acceptance-boundary` | One journey slice or one contract test proves all members |
| `cr-no-layer-split` | A must-combine rule: an atom that only proves, documents or supplies one layer of another is merged into it, never left apart. The verifier should have merged it in pass 1; this rule catches survivors and migrated legacy rows (R7) |
| `cr-attributability` | Never combine where a red in one member would be misattributed to another |
| `cr-no-need-boundary` | Never combine an atom with an open question with one that has none, so a question blocks only its own work |
| `cr-no-checkpoint-boundary` | Never cross a milestone or a checkpoint |
| `cr-one-new-surface` | Never two new user-facing surfaces: the "never two add-page" rule without the recipe word |
| `cr-never-started` | Never regroup an increment that has started or been ruled to stand alone |
| `cr-ceiling` | Never exceed the ceiling below |

There is no rule keyed on repos, and `check --strict` refuses one if a programme's header adds it (C-027).

**Ceiling** (`combination.ceiling`, data, overridable with a reason in the contract): at most 12 acceptance criteria, 1 new user-facing surface and 2 end-to-end journeys. A combined increment must be expressible as one end-state proof the consistency reviewer can run. This bounds the review surface that FA's 1,076-line s17 plan showed (A §8). There is no repo limit, because repos follow from the behaviour (R7).

**Pass 2 never re-splits.** Combining only joins slices. No rule, ceiling or re-combine may divide an atom's behaviour by layer or by repo (R7).

**Output.**

- The combiner writes one file per group: `{title, outcome, why, members[keys], class, milestone, dependsOn (explicit only), checkpoint, size, combination{rules, why, risk, attributability, reverseEffects}}`. `reverseEffects` is predeclared as a `split` effect, so Sam can undo the group with one command (C-047). Atoms left on their own become solo increments at ingest; a solo increment stores no title or outcome, which are rendered from its member (C-062).
- It lists every candidate pair it left apart in `declined[]`, with the rule and the reason. This is "considered and left alone", as valuable as the combinations themselves (G §6).
- COMBINE_VERIFIER checks every group against the rules and the ceiling, and checks that every declined reason is real. It fails a group whose only shared signal is its repos, a group justified by a shared implementation component ("same lock", "one task-folder contract"), and a `declined[]` entry whose reason is a repo or layer difference (C-027). It writes an `edgeChecks` record on every cross-repo edge lifted onto an increment (C-025). A failure goes back to the combiner once.

**Applied automatically and reported** (Q13). The memory rule is "make the call, flag it after", and re-combining costs nothing. Every combination appears in report section 2 with "To reverse". Setting `combine: propose` in the contract stops the automatic apply: the phase writes the proposal and ingest waits for Sam.

**Re-derivable after every ruling.**

- A ruling or discovery that adds, drops or reshapes atoms resets `combine`.
- The combiner starts from the previous `distil/increments/*.json` and keeps every group whose members are unchanged. Groups are matched by the set of their members' root atoms (resolved through `supersededBy`), and ingest carries keys forward whatever the combiner writes, so a variant written by a ruling changes no increment id (4.4, C-054).
- A started increment is never regrouped, and the combiner records it as "left alone: already started".
- A mid-build re-combine regroups only unstarted `todo` and `blocked` increments.
- `combination.basis.atomsSha` records the atom set that was combined, and the report shows the changes since the last combine.

### 5.8 Rulings flow back

1. **Capture.** Sam rules by the page's copy-batch commands, by answer-sheet lines (`q-notification-list-scope: A  own org only`, `c-004: reverse`, `inc-015: release walked through`), or in conversation. For conversation, the skill maps his words to slugs and shows the mapping before writing. An answer is tentative only on the precise rule of 6.4: "?" directly after the option letter, or a hedge word (maybe, probably, not sure, perhaps). It then becomes `--tentative` with his words, never a ruling, and the mapping says which token made it tentative (C-046).
2. **Record and apply.** `tim backlog rule` appends the decision, applies the option's predeclared effects, recomputes `needs` and statuses, strips dependencies on dropped increments with a note, and resets `combine`. The reset cascades to `prose` and `report`. A ruling on a question with a default in force supersedes the `defaulted` decision (C-044). A ruling whose effects touch a member of a started or done increment is refused unless it carries `reopens`; then the change arrives as a variant atom and a follow-up increment, and the built one stays done (C-055).
3. **Free text.** An answer with no predeclared effects flags the question `effects-pending`. RULING_APPLIER (think; `APPLIER-BRIEF.md` and `RULING_RULE`, "Never reopen the question, never soften the ruling into an option, never widen it", `FA:160-163`) writes an effects file. Any new wording arrives as a proposed variant atom that a verifier checks before it is adopted, so a machine's rendering of a person's words is never adopted unchecked.
4. **Re-combine and re-render.** The distil workflow runs `combine` to `report`. Ids stay stable. The question leaves section 1 and appears in section 10 with its effects ("unblocked 2, superseded 1"). Unblocked increments turn `todo`.
5. **Build side.** Every build task's brief expands the decisions that bind its members, and every build stage brief carries `RULING_RULE`.
6. **During a build.** A judge's deferral that needs a person becomes a question raised by the judge, and appears in section 1. Other deferrals become `proposed` atoms through `discover`, adopted only after a separate `REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER` task and the recipe lint pass (C-059).

### 5.9 Personas, and where each comes from

| Persona | Cut from | Change |
|---|---|---|
| `INTERVIEWER.md` | parity `CORPUS_INTERVIEWER.md` | Asks about purpose, sources and roles, delivery, delegates and combine mode; proposes each repo's `consumes` from the `docker/stack` service dependencies for a person to confirm (C-026); loaded by the parent session |
| `SOURCE_CHARACTERISER.md` | `SOURCE_EXTRACTOR.md:1-47` (method only), parity `SCREEN_ENUMERATOR.md` (enumerate from the source itself) | Writes units with tokens and `readAt`; calls `source crop` for every visual unit (C-039); says what could not be read; pointers it did not follow become units of kind `pointer`, and a SharePoint or OneDrive link makes it ask for an export (C-040) |
| `source-types/{brief,docx,pdf,markdown,spreadsheet,slides,confluence,jira,image-board,design,prototype,live-service,repo,code,trace-corpus,transcript,chat,email,existing-backlog,parity-corpus,openspec,web}.md` | `SOURCE_EXTRACTOR.md:28-32, 125-133`, `EUDPA-409/rules.mural.md`, trace workflow `:103-124`; parity's capture specs for `prototype` and `live-service` | Mechanics per kind, including `spreadsheet`, `slides`, `prototype` and `live-service` (C-040). Instance notes go to the run's `characterisation.md`, never back into the skill (F P1) |
| `EXTRACT_VERIFIER.md` | trace verify (`:850-927`), parity `FINDING_VERIFIER.md` | A verdict per unit and what it searched for |
| `CARRYOVER_TRIAGER.md` | parity `SKILL.md:667-690` | `carries`, `retired`, `changed`, `recheck`, with the settling unit |
| `SLICER.md` | parity slicing practice, `dr1c-parity/slices.json` | A slice is an area of behaviour someone observes (a journey section, a page group, a capability, an exchange with an outside system), never a repo, layer, technology or source. Each slice has a `behaviour` sentence naming the observer and the outcome. Units from code sources with roles constraint, consistency-anchor or current-behaviour go to the behaviour slice they inform. Slices own units from every source; one cross-cutting owner; a brief per slice (C-028) |
| `REQUIREMENT_AUTHOR.md` | parity `FINDING_AUTHOR.md`, trace extract rules (`:168-179`) | N sources per atom; the grain rule, single and vertical; kinds defined by who observes them (C-029); observable acceptance with an end-to-end criterion; `notRaised[]`, `premisesDisproved[]` |
| `REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER.md` | parity `FINDING_VERIFIER.md`, trace verify, the ticket-refiner criteria rubric | Recipe smell, single-requirement and layer-split checks; merges atoms observed only by another atom of the programme (C-029); the missed-repo question (C-030); `edgeChecks` (C-025); the hygiene rule (C-032); a verdict on every dismissal (C-049). Also verifies discovered atoms before adoption (C-059). Written early and used on this programme's own atoms |
| `RESLICER.md` | `COMBINER.md`'s grouping method, cut down to one job | Authors one vertical atom per behaviour from migrated legacy layer rows and `distil/reslice.json`, with `carriedFrom[]` naming every row absorbed; never combines behaviours (C-034) |
| `GAP_AUDITOR.md` | DR1C coverage auditors (G1 §1.4) | Justify the quiet, or add atoms that a separate verifier checks |
| `RECONCILER.md` | `SPEC_RECONCILER.md:19-30` without the engine vocabulary, `RECONCILER-BRIEF.md:30-76` | Target-neutral; conflicts, precedence, parked not rejected, variant atoms, draft questions |
| `PANEL.md` (judge, chair and critic sections) | EUDPA-409 `panel-*.json`, `panel/README.md` | Formalised from workarea files into the skill |
| `RULING_APPLIER.md` | EUDPA-409 `APPLIER-BRIEF.md`, `RULING_RULE` | Writes effects, never edits JSON |
| `COMPLETENESS_CRITIC.md` | trace critic (`:1435-1443`), `ched-p/completeness-critique.md` | Counter-numbers and blind spots per source kind |
| `QUESTION_EDITOR.md`, `QUESTION_CRITIC.md` | the `c0f99985` question shape; Ralph's lettered options (`prd/SKILL.md:24-55`) | 5.6 |
| `COMBINER.md`, `COMBINE_VERIFIER.md` | parity `DUPLICATE_SWEEPER.md`, `combination-proposal.md:13-88` | 5.7; joins slices, never divides one; justified by behaviour signals only, never repos or implementation components (C-027) |
| `ATOM_DEDUPER.md` | method from parity `DUPLICATE_SWEEPER.md` ("would one person, doing one piece of work, close both?") | The distiller's own dedupe persona, over atoms rather than screen findings (C-038) |
| `PROSE_EDITOR.md` | method from parity `COPY_EDITOR.md` | Rewrites machine-written requirement prose into GDS plain English within the distiller's slots and budgets (C-038) |
| `PROSE_CLAIM_VERIFIER.md` | method from parity `CLAIM_VERIFIER.md`: quote conservation (I5) and the polarity list (I10) | Checks each rewrite against the frozen statement and the sources; a failure reverts the rewrite (C-038) |
| `COLD_READER.md` | new | Reads each report section on its own and fails any term that section uses without defining (C-050) |

**The distiller owns its prose personas** (C-038). Parity's `COPY_EDITOR.md`, `CLAIM_VERIFIER.md` and `DUPLICATE_SWEEPER.md` are finding-shaped and screen-shaped (`COPY_EDITOR.md:21-67` slots, `CLAIM_VERIFIER.md:17-19`'s EUDPA-328 path), so running them over requirements would give the distiller's prose no slot rules or budgets, and a parity edit would silently change distillation. Each distiller persona carries a "Method from" line naming the parity file by path, and defines the distiller's slots and budgets:

| Slot | Budget |
|---|---|
| Question headline | a question, 90 characters or fewer |
| Option text | 40 words |
| Option consequence | 40 words |
| If nobody answers | 40 words |
| Increment outcome (combined increments only) | 60 words |
| `combination.why` | 40 words |

Parity's own files stay untouched: they gain no distiller section, and `CLAIM_VERIFIER.md:17` is not edited.

Every task's spawn prompt starts with the GUARD RAILS constant, then says "Read `<task>/prompt.md` and follow it". `prompt.md` names the persona by path and the run's `CONTRACT.md`.

### 5.10 Sources: kinds, acquisition, provenance, roles and retention

| Kind | Acquired by | Provenance token | Default role and confidence | Retention |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Brief (the requester's own instructions) | pasted or copied to `raw/` by the skill, sealed | `section:<slug>/para:<n>`, or `speaker@timestamp` for spoken instructions | requirement; `stated`. Accepted as a requirement source without a ruling (C-045) | tracked: the requester's own words |
| Confluence | `source acquire` through `confluence-client`, using `body.view` (D §2.1) | row id such as `NNS-UN-021`, or `h4:<heading>/li:<n>` | requirement; `stated` | tracked |
| Jira issue, epic and comments | `source acquire` through `jira-client`, in the `prepare-refinement.sh` fetch shape (F P4) | `EUDPA-123#desc/para:<n>`, `EUDPA-123#comment:<id>` | requirement; `stated` for descriptions and criteria, `inferred` for comments unless a named authority decides | tracked, except comments by anyone other than the requester, which are `local` like any third party's words (C-045) |
| Word, PDF, markdown | copied to `raw/`. Word by `source acquire`, which unzips with `fflate` and writes `raw/<id>.txt` with paragraph, table-row and cell markers (the method of `SOURCE_EXTRACTOR.md:28-32`, now tested code, so no agent runs `unzip` or a sed chain, C-040); PDF through the Read tool by page | `section:<slug>/para:<n>/item:<k>`, `annex:<n>/reg:<ref>`, `page:<n>/para:<k>`; `alsoStatedAt` for repeats | requirement; `stated` | tracked |
| Spreadsheet (xlsx, csv) | `source acquire`, converting each sheet to `raw/<id>.txt` with sheet, row and cell markers (C-040) | `sheet:<name>/row:<n>/col:<letter or header>` | requirement; `stated` | tracked |
| Slides (pptx) | `source acquire`, converting to `raw/<id>.txt` with slide and shape markers; speaker notes kept apart (C-040) | `slide:<n>/shape:<k>`, `slide:<n>/notes` | requirement; `stated` | tracked |
| Images and design boards | copied; read one at a time with the Read tool; regions recorded, and `tim backlog source crop` cuts and hashes a crop for every visual unit (C-039) | `file:<short>/region:<label>/item:<n>[/crop:<name>]` | requirement (design); `stated` for signed-off designs | tracked |
| Prototype | a repo pinned at a commit, plus a rendered capture through parity's capture specs (C-040) | `<prototype>:<route>/<region>@<sha>` | requirement; `observed` for rendered behaviour, `stated` for copy | tracked |
| Live service | a URL plus a capture through parity's capture specs, sealed (C-040) | `<service>:<route>/<region>@<capturedAt>` | current-behaviour, or requirement when registered as the target; `observed` | tracked |
| Repos and existing code | never copied; read with `git show <pin>:<path>` at a recorded pin (`citations/resolve.js:6-30`) | `<repoKey>:<path>:<lines>@<sha>`, with blob | constraint, consistency-anchor or current-behaviour; never a feature requirement on its own (D §7.13) | tracked |
| Trace corpus | a private directory per agent; `fill` values redacted (trace workflow `:103-105, 113-124`) | `trace:<hash>/action:<id>` | current-behaviour; `observed` | tracked |
| Transcripts, chat, email | pasted or exported to `raw/` by the skill | `speaker@timestamp`, `msg:<id>` | each utterance classed requirement, decision, question, opinion or context; `stated` only for a decision by a named authority, otherwise `inferred` | keyed on the speaker (C-045): the requester's own utterances are a brief and tracked; other people's utterances stay `local` whatever the container, their quote is `null` in git and a paraphrase is tracked |
| Existing backlog or parity corpus | read-only, through `tim backlog show` or `tim parity` | `<programme>:<id>` | decision-record for rulings; requirement for verified findings (`inferred` unless verified); enters through carryover | tracked |
| OpenSpec | files under `openspec/specs/**` | `REQ-...`, `SCN-...` | requirement; `stated` | tracked |
| Web page | WebFetch by the skill, snapshot sealed | `url#heading` | as registered | tracked |

**Retention keys on the speaker, not the source kind** (3.8, C-045). A Jira comment, an email or a chat line from anyone other than the requester is treated as a third party's words, whatever carries it. `decisions[].words` is always tracked, as the ruler's own words. Whether third parties' words may ever enter git is a must-answer question; until it is ruled they never do.

**No linked source vanishes** (C-040). A pointer unit on a requirement-role source (a linked workbook, a SharePoint or OneDrive file, a prototype) must be registered as a source, stated absent with a reason, or raised as a question; P1 fails until it is. For a SharePoint or OneDrive link the characteriser asks for an export.

Credential-shaped values are redacted at characterisation. `tim backlog check` refuses any that slip through, so the backlog stays readable past the Sonar Read hook (H §2.3).

### 5.11 Model tiers, fan-out and Codex

- **Tiers.** Every `agent()` call sets its tier explicitly (FA:95-97): think is Opus at high effort, doer is Sonnet and step is Haiku at low effort.
- **Opus concurrency** is capped at five by a small in-script semaphore, below the memory rule's "about 5 or 6" (S §7.4). The cap also governs span walking: a span task whose `after` tasks are accepted still waits for a slot.
- **Codex inside `advance`.** Codex-bound distil phases run inside `advance --distil` through `tim backlog codex run` (C-078), so under `distil-codex` they cost no Claude agent. DM0 and DM1 merge into one step agent, and after the contract `run open` returns `handback` rather than `firstStep` because the author span is heavy, so DM3 and DM4 merge with the step agent that takes over: about 8 step agents plus handbacks on long Codex phases and 32 Claude judgement tasks, about 40 to 42 agents against 57 before.
- **Codex binding: one rule.** Every distil phase with model tasks is bindable to Codex, role by role, through the same stage machinery, and every such role has a sandbox row in 8.2. The preset `distil-codex` binds the heavy local phases, the ones 5.2 marks †: characterise, extract-verify, carryover, reslice, author, verify and yield's gap-audit (judge 3). Because every transition is decided by `advance --distil`, the preset works the same in a Codex-orchestrated session, which loops `advance --distil` (which runs the Codex phases in-process) and the listed outward commands with no Claude session (C-002, C-078). No distil task writes outside its own task folder. Author and characterise tasks bound to Codex run with writes confined to that folder (8.2), and `stage accept` moves their atom and unit files into place. Verifying tasks run read-only and return verdicts that `stage accept` applies.
- **Judgement phases** (slice, reconcile, panel, dedupe, critic, questions, combine, prose, the report's cold reader and apply-rulings) are bindable in the same way, but no shipped preset binds them: they stay on Claude think or doer under `distil-codex` until a distil parity check shows Codex holds the bar there too, and meanwhile any one of them is bound with `run bind --role <role>=codex`. When that check passes, they join `distil-codex`, a one-line preset change.

### 5.12 Resumability

- The phase ledger is the resumption record, because "half a slice's findings on disk look exactly like a finished slice" (`phase.sh:13-14`). `advance --distil` reads it and returns the next step, so a relaunch, a new session or a Codex-orchestrated session all resume at the same point by the same tested rule (C-002).
- `done` records a fingerprint of the phase's outputs. A phase whose outputs changed after `done` shows as stale, and `gate` re-checks fingerprints.
- `reset` cascades, which fixes DR1B's contradictory ledger (G1 §2 item 4).
- Within a phase, task receipts are the unit of progress. A relaunch skips every task with an accepted receipt.
- Fact records and `upstreamShas` are part of the resume point: a step agent that dies mid-run is replaced and carries on at the first sub-step with no fact record. Distil in-process phases write fact records in the build's shape and MAC, at `distil/runs/<label>/<phase>/facts/<step>.json` (C-082), so both pipelines resume by one rule.
- `resumeFromRunId` with an identical script and args replays completed agents from the journal.
- The handover names the workarea, the phase status output, the exact args and the run id.

---

## 6. The distillation report

### 6.1 Rules

1. **Generated, never edited.** `tim backlog report <p>` renders every view from `backlog.json`, `distil/`, `build/` and the seals. No agent edits a report file, and a refresh is a re-render. The alignment report drifted into a changelog because an agent edited it in place (`FA:1256-1268`, G §1.3).
2. **A decision surface first and a record second.** Section 1 is what needs Sam. Section 2 is what the machine decided that he may want to reverse. History comes after both (G §1, commit `c0f99985`).
3. **Headline first, id second.** Every question, increment and atom is named by its headline. The id is a handle for answering, never the only label (`feedback_reference_by_headline_not_ticket_number`).
4. **Every section stands alone.** Each section says what a thing is on first mention (`feedback_updates_stand_alone`). A `COLD_READER` task checks this on every render, reading each section on its own (6.6, C-050). The answer sheet and the decisions page stand alone too: neither shows an id without its headline (C-046, C-052).
5. **Every number is derived** from state, and nothing is typed in (`page.js:222-231`). Progress is "N of TOTAL (P%)", with deferred and dropped increments named separately and counted in no total (`feedback_report_progress_count_on_landing`).
6. **Nothing is silently dropped.** An unknown value renders under its raw name (`page.js:267-269`). Superseded, parked and rejected items stay visible "so nobody re-raises them" (`page.js:368-377`).
7. **Evidence is in reach.** Each citation shows its quote and resolves to a permalink or a snippet at the pinned blob (`sections.js:54-97`). A question about something visual carries the picture (`project_eudpa328_decision_visual_evidence`).
8. **Honest about itself.** Before the backlog, the page says where the evidence is weak and what the method cannot see (`SPEC-GATE.md:18-42`, the trace critic).
9. **Plain English.** GDS style: short, active, front-loaded sentences, abbreviations expanded on first use, dates written as "18 September 2026", no em dashes and no time estimates. Size is stated as increments, criteria, files and risk.

### 6.2 Sections

| # | Section | Contents | Generated from | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Masthead | Title and purpose; one derived headline sentence; the sources read, with version or pin and the date read; a drift panel when any seal or head moved since the last render (P10); the freshness stamp | header, `distil/seals.json`, `distil/heads.json`, counts | Stands alone; the parity masthead and drift panel (`page.js:109-139, 287`) |
| 1 | Needs your ruling | **(a) Questions,** in two groups (C-044): "Must be answered before anything it touches is built", by blast radius; then "Has a default in force", by blast radius. Every question, whatever its group, shows all four parts: the question, what is in play, the lettered options with the sources on each side counted by role and tier (C-050), and "If nobody answers" (C-048). A must-answer card adds what it blocks by headline (`blastRadius.blocks`), what stays buildable meanwhile, pictures and batch-ruling controls. A defaulted question renders as a compact card: headline, one line of "In play", lettered options each with its count and consequence, then "In force: A" and how many rows rest on it (`restsOnDefault`). Questions are grouped by cluster within each group. **(b) Waiting on you to act:** walkthrough checkpoints; holds only a person can release (awaiting merge or approval, changes requested, a sync conflict needing a judged port, a platform fix owed, a proof owed), each with its release command; tentative answers to confirm; and, when every increment is done, the merge plan: each PR in merge order from `consumes`, providers first, under the rule "all green, then merge in this order, no partial merge" (C-026) | `questions[]`, `build/state.json` holds, checkpoints, `report --merge-plan` | The one thing the report exists for (G §8.2 row 1) |
| 2 | Calls we made that you may want to reverse | Precedence picks, panel rulings, defaults in force, combinations applied automatically, and discovered work the judge adopted. Each gives the call, the rule that made it, the other side's case, "To reverse:" costed in increments and files, and the generated command that reverses it (`rule --subject conflict:<id> --choose <side>`, `rule --subject combination:<key> --split`, or `rule <q> --option <X>` for a default), with a checkbox that adds it to a copied batch (C-047) | `conflicts[]` and their `reverseEffects`, `decisions[]` where `decidedBy` is not a person, `assumptions[]`, `combination`, discovered atoms | `feedback_headless_make_calls_dont_gate`; the alignment report's section 2 (G §1.2) |
| 3 | Read this first: where the evidence is weak | Atoms resting on one source; criteria at `inferred` or `gap`; atoms whose evidence is only legacy; the number of units from requirement-role sources dismissed as not a requirement (C-049); the critic's findings and counter-numbers; what each source kind cannot show; what would upgrade each | atoms, `critique.json`, source characterisations, dismissal verdicts | `SPEC-GATE.md:18-42`; the completeness critique |
| 4 | The backlog | Increments in build order with status, outcome headline, members (expanding to each atom's statement, acceptance and sources), dependencies by headline, needs, checkpoint and size; N of TOTAL (P%) | `increments[]`, `requirements[]` | The build plan, stated as requirements |
| 5 | How increments were combined | The rules and the ceiling; each combined increment with its members, rules, reason, risk and attributability; "Considered and left apart", with the rule and reason for each pair; increments left alone because they had started; what changed since the last combine; atoms against increments ("120 atoms became 40 increments") | `combination`, `distil/increments/`, `combination.basis` | `combination-proposal.md:5-88` |
| 6 | Coverage | P1 to P11 with each result and its counts; the matrix of source to atom to increment; units in each of the five trace buckets; the gap count; "not a requirement" dismissals grouped by reason, each expanding to its units, their quotes and the verifier's verdict (C-049); and the total of repos the distiller missed, that plans later added (C-030) | the check commands' JSON, dismissal verdicts, `state.plan.reposAdded` | Nothing is dropped (G §8.2 row 6) |
| 7 | Conflicts | Those needing a person (linked to section 1), then those settled by precedence or the panel (linked to section 2), each with both sides quoted | `conflicts[]` | `SPEC-GATE.md:97-131` |
| 8 | Out of scope, parked, rejected, superseded | Each item with who ruled, when and why | `scope.out[]`, atom statuses, decisions | `page.js:368-377` |
| 9 | Noticed and not raised | Each author's `notRaised[]` with its reason, and the premises the authors disproved | `distil/authoring/*.json` | "so a quiet author has to say what it looked at and declined" (S; judge 1) |
| 10 | Rulings applied | Newest first, one plain-English line each: the ruler's words verbatim, who, the date and the effects ("unblocked 2, superseded 1"). The full note is collapsed | `decisions[]` | Kept short, unlike the alignment report's 410 lines (G §1.3) |
| 11 | How this was checked | Per phase: who authored and who verified, verdict counts (stands, corrected, disputed, added), extract-verify counts, panel votes, question-critic results, the combine verifier's result, prose-edit, claim-check and cold-reader records (sections read, and sections not read because they rendered empty, C-081), and the gates passed | provenance, receipts, phase ledger | Graft 19; judges 1 to 3 |
| 12 | Build status | Per increment: done and how, the executor for each stage (from receipts), files reviewed, findings raised, verified, fixed and rejected, the judge's rulings, rung results, acceptance per criterion with the passed test that proves it, the integration-proof record (what ran, on which stack sources, proving which criteria, C-020), the repos the distiller missed that the plan added, with why (C-030), PR links, CI state fetched at render with its time, and SonarCloud's result from PR checks or "no report" | `build/state.json`, receipts | Graft 19; `feedback_report_progress_count_on_landing` |
| 13 | Where everything is | Every file path, the commands that regenerate each view, run labels and run ids for resume, and the footer stamp (backlog hash, tool version, heads, render time) | registry, runs, stamp | Alignment section 6; parity footer (`page.js:379-405`) |

**The decisions page is a view of the JSON** (C-052). `tim backlog report --decisions` renders a standalone page of section 1 alone from `questions[]`, with `blastRadius.blocks` and `restsOnDefault` printed for each. No hand-written copy of the open questions is kept once the renderer exists; until then any bootstrap copy is stamped as a hand-written view of `questions[]`, and a check compares its ids, defaults and blocked increments with the JSON.

### 6.3 Skeleton, with example content

This is the markdown twin (`report/report.md`) for an example plants programme. The HTML page has the same sections, with batch controls, crops and expanding cards.

```markdown
# High-risk plants notifications: what needs a ruling

This page leads with what needs your ruling, then the calls we made that you may want to
reverse. Everything below that is the record. It was generated from the programme's state
on 18 September 2026 at 14:10. Nothing on it was typed in.

**120 requirements in 42 increments. 4 questions are open: 2 must be answered before
anything they touch is built, and 2 have a default in force that you can change. Built so
far: 0 of 39 (0%). 2 deferred and 1 dropped are counted in no total.**

Sources: plant health notification policy paper (v1, August 2026, read 18 September 2026);
the Confluence requirements space (version 212, read 18 September 2026); the Mural journey
board (exported 12 September 2026); the live-animals service at 4f1c9e2, used only to keep
behaviour consistent.

Evidence changed since the last render: none.

## 1. Needs your ruling

### Must be answered before anything it touches is built

#### Should a user see only their own organisation's notifications?

q-notification-list-scope | access control | blocks 1 increment

The dashboard lists notifications. Which notifications may a signed-in user see?

In play: the dashboard list and the notification view, in the plants frontend and backend.

| Option | What happens | Sources |
|---|---|---|
| A, recommended by the panel | Only notifications created under the user's current organisation. Colleagues in one organisation see each other's work. Nobody sees another organisation's. | 2 requirement sources: policy paper section 14 (tier 1); Confluence "Dashboard" (tier 2) |
| B | Only notifications the user created, across organisations. An agent working for two importers sees both. Colleagues see nothing of each other's. | 1 requirement source: user research session 7 (tier 3) |

If nobody answers: nothing that lists notifications is built. "Users see only the
notifications they may see" stays blocked.
Meanwhile: every other dashboard increment can still be built.

[Picture: the dashboard list, from the Mural board]

#### Is the commodity code entered once per notification or once per line?
...

### Has a default in force

These defaults were applied so work could go on. Each stays here until someone rules.

#### What title does the error summary use?

q-error-summary-title | content | 1 increment rests on this default
In play: the error summary on every form page.
- A. "There is a problem", the GOV.UK default. 1 design source (tier 1). Nothing changes.
- B. A title naming the page. No source. 1 increment changes its copy.
In force: A.
To change it: tim backlog rule high-risk-plants q-error-summary-title --option B --by sam --at <now> --words "<your words>" --note "<why>"

#### Should the service remind importers about a missing document?

q-missing-document-reminder | scope | 1 increment rests on this default
In play: what happens after a notification is submitted without every document.
- A. No reminder in the first release. 1 requirement source (tier 2). Nothing is added.
- B. Remind the importer by email. 1 current-behaviour source: the old service. 1 increment is added.
In force: A.
To change it: tim backlog rule high-risk-plants q-missing-document-reminder --option B --by sam --at <now> --words "<your words>" --note "<why>"

### Waiting on you to act

- Walkthrough: "Importers give the port and expected date of arrival on one page" has
  landed on the programme branch. The build stopped here for your walkthrough.
  To release it: tim backlog state high-risk-plants inc-015 release --note "walked through"

## 2. Calls we made that you may want to reverse

### The policy paper wins on which countries may send ware potatoes
The policy paper lists four countries. The Confluence page lists five, adding Cyprus. We
applied the precedence rule for values, which ranks the policy paper first. The case for
Confluence: it was edited after the paper was published.
To reverse: one requirement's acceptance changes, and one increment is rebuilt if it has
already landed.
[ ] tim backlog rule high-risk-plants --subject conflict:c-004 --choose confluence-requirements --by sam --at <now> --words "<your words>" --note "<why>"

### Port of entry and arrival date are built together
Three requirements describe one page's questions, and one journey test proves all three.
To reverse: one increment becomes two, and nothing built is affected.
[ ] tim backlog rule high-risk-plants --subject combination:arrival --split --by sam --at <now> --words "<your words>" --note "<why>"

Tick the calls you want to reverse and press "copy batch" to copy their commands together.

## 3. Read this first: where the evidence is weak

- 9 requirements rest on a single source. 4 of them came from a user research session only.
- 14 acceptance criteria are inferred, not stated. The largest group is error messages:
  no source gives their wording. A content designer's review would settle 11 of them.
- 23 units from requirement sources were ruled not requirements. A verifier agreed with
  each; the reasons are listed under Coverage.
- What this method cannot see: behaviour the old system has that no source describes.
  The critic found one: the old service reminds importers about a missing document.
  It is now a question in section 1.

## 4. The backlog

Built so far: 0 of 39 (0%). 2 deferred and 1 dropped are counted in no total.

| # | Increment | Status | Members | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| inc-014 | Ware-potato notifications accept only the four permitted countries | to do | 1 | Commodity lines can be added |
| inc-015 | Importers give the port and expected date of arrival on one page | to do | 3 | none |
| inc-021 | Users see only the notifications they may see | blocked: needs a ruling | 2 | Signed-in dashboard |

## 5. How increments were combined

120 requirements became 42 increments: 25 combined and 17 on their own. 78 fewer builds.
Rules applied: same surface; one acceptance boundary; never across a milestone, a
checkpoint or an open question; at most one new page; at most 12 criteria. Sharing repos
is never a reason to combine.

Considered and left apart:
- "Port of entry" and "vehicle registration": transport has its own reference data, and
  a red there would stall arrival (attributability).

## 6. Coverage

| Proof | Result | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| P1 every source read | pass | 5 sources, 1,104 units, every unit verified by a second agent |
| P4 every unit accounted for | pass | 612 cited, 401 not requirements (every one agreed by a verifier), 38 in conflicts, 20 excluded, 33 duplicates |
| P11 vertical slices | pass | 0 layer splits; 14 cross-repo edges, each with an edge check; every product increment carries an end-to-end proof |

Not requirements, by reason: 212 navigation and page furniture; 96 house style; 61 legacy
architecture; 32 other. Each group expands to its units and quotes.

Repos the distiller missed, that plans later added: 0.

## 7. Conflicts

## 10. Rulings applied

- 18 September 2026, Sam: "Great Britain, not England, everywhere." Unblocked 2.

## 11. How this was checked

## 13. Where everything is

Generated by tim backlog report high-risk-plants. Backlog sha256 9f1c... at 14:10 on
18 September 2026, tim 0.9.0.

### 6.4 The answer sheet, and how rulings flow back

`report/answer-sheet.md` is generated with one answer line per open question, most urgent first, then one line per call Sam may reverse and per hold only he can release. It stands alone (C-046): above each answer line, generated comment lines give the headline, each lettered option in one line with its source count by role, the recommendation and "If nobody answers", so Sam can answer from the sheet without the page. The report lint runs over the sheet too.

```
# Answers for high-risk-plants, generated 18 September 2026
# One line each: <id>: <option letter> <your words, optional>
# To record an answer as tentative, put "?" straight after the letter ("B?"), or use
# maybe, probably, not sure or perhaps. Any other "?" in your words is just punctuation.
# Leave a line blank to skip it.

# Should a user see only their own organisation's notifications?
#   A. Only notifications created under the user's current organisation. 2 requirement sources.
#   B. Only notifications the user created, across organisations. 1 requirement source.
#   Recommended: A. If nobody answers: nothing that lists notifications is built.
q-notification-list-scope:

# Is the commodity code entered once per notification or once per line?
