```
#   A. Once per notification. 1 requirement source.
#   B. Once per commodity line. 2 requirement sources.
#   Recommended: B. If nobody answers: the commodity pages are not built.
q-commodity-code-grain:

# What title does the error summary use? (default in force: A)
#   A. "There is a problem", the GOV.UK default. 1 design source.
#   B. A title naming the page. No source.
#   Recommended: A. If nobody answers: A stays in force.
q-error-summary-title:

# Calls you may reverse. Write "reverse" and your words to reverse one.
# The policy paper wins on which countries may send ware potatoes.
c-004:
# Port of entry and arrival date are built together.
combination:arrival:

# Holds only you can release. Write "release" and your words to release one.
# Walkthrough: importers give the port and expected date of arrival on one page.
inc-015:
```

**Tentative is parsed precisely** (C-046). An answer is tentative only when "?" directly follows the option letter, or when one of the hedge words maybe, probably, not sure or perhaps appears. Any other question mark is Sam's punctuation: "C. Why would we accept less?" is a firm C. The mapping the skill shows says "recorded as tentative because: <token>" whenever it does so.

**Reversals and releases** (C-047). `c-004: reverse <words>` maps to `rule --subject conflict:c-004 --choose <the losing side>`, `combination:arrival: reverse <words>` to `rule --subject combination:arrival --split`, and `inc-015: release <words>` to `state inc-015 release --note <words>`.

Sam answers in any of three ways:

- **On the page.** He picks options on several cards and presses "copy batch". The page emits one `tim backlog rule` command per line, with `--at` stamped from the page's own clock at the moment of copying and `--by sam`. This is the parity batch pattern (`page.js:186-219`, `card.js:9-33`) with the new writer.
- **On the sheet.** He fills in lines such as `q-notification-list-scope: A own org only` and pastes them into the conversation.
- **In conversation.** He answers in his own words. The skill maps each answer to a slug and shows the mapping before writing anything.

In every case the skill's `RULE` mode:

1. Shows each answer's mapping: question headline, option, his words, the option's effects and, for a tentative answer, the token that made it tentative.
2. Writes each answer, reversal and release with `tim backlog rule` or `tim backlog state`. A hedge becomes `--tentative`, never a ruling.
3. Launches `apply-rulings` when an answer has no predeclared effects, then `combine` to `report`.
4. Prints the re-rendered section 1 and the rulings it applied.

Questions raised during a build reach the same page. The judge's `needsPerson` items become questions through `tim backlog question add`, and the record stage re-renders the report after every increment.

### 6.5 The renderer

**Modules.**

- A sibling renderer in `tim/src/backlog/render/`: `page.js` (section order, masthead, drift panel, footer), `card.js` (the requirement card, the increment card and the compact defaulted-question card), `sections.js` (sections 1 to 13), `answer-sheet.js` and `decisions.js` (the decisions page, C-052).
- Shared with parity through `tim/src/backlog/render/shared/`: `theme.js`, `prose.js`, `artifact.js`, `citations.js` and `snippet.js` (4.1).
- Parity keeps its two-sided card (`card.js:272-296`).

**The requirement card:**

- the headline and the statement;
- each acceptance criterion with its witness and a confidence chip;
- a sources strip listing every source that asserts the requirement, with role and quote;
- `consistentWith`;
- "This requirement is wrong if" (the falsifier, as parity's card has, `card.js:358`);
- "How this was checked", the verification record verbatim and collapsed (`sections.js:138-156`);
- the question block when `needs` is set.

**The increment card** shows the outcome (for a solo increment, rendered from its member, C-062), the members (expandable), why they were grouped, the risk and attributability, and a build panel.

**Emitters:**

| File | What it is | Tracked |
|---|---|---|
| `report/index.html` | The static local page with batch-ruling controls | no |
| `report/artifact.html` | One self-contained file. The skill publishes it as a private Artifact when Sam wants a link, and the page names what an artifact cannot carry (`page.js:294-298`) | no |
| `report/report.md` | The markdown twin, for `git diff` review and terminal reading | yes |
| `report/answer-sheet.md` | The answer sheet | yes |

**Tests** (vitest, on inputs and outputs), using a fixture programme:

- section order;
- every number on the page equals a count computed from the fixture, so nothing is typed in;
- an unknown enum value renders under its raw name;
- a must-answer question renders first;
- every question, whatever its class, shows all four parts: the question, what is in play, the lettered options and "If nobody answers" (C-048);
- option counts are shown by role and tier, never summed across roles (C-050);
- every `visual[]` entry resolves to an existing crop file whose sha256 matches the recorded one (C-039);
- every section 2 entry carries a generated command, and that command parses under `tim backlog rule`'s own argument parser (C-047);
- a superseded atom renders in section 8;
- the markdown twin and the HTML agree on every count;
- the answer sheet parses back to the same question ids, every sheet id comes with its headline and every option letter, and "C. Why would we accept less?" records as a firm C while "B?" and "maybe B" record as tentative (C-046);
- the decisions page's ids, defaults and blocked increments equal those in `questions[]` (C-052);
- no template string contains an em dash or an en dash.

### 6.6 Prose quality

- **What is machine-written:** question headlines, option text and consequences, "If nobody answers", combined increments' titles and outcomes, and the reasons for each combination. A solo increment's title and outcome are rendered from its member, so they are never a separately written field (C-062). Section introductions are templates in the renderer, reviewed with its code.
- **The `prose` phase** uses the distiller's own personas (5.9, C-038):
  1. PROSE_EDITOR rewrites each machine-written field into GDS plain English through `tim backlog set`, within the slot budgets of 5.9.
  2. PROSE_CLAIM_VERIFIER, a different task, checks each rewrite against the frozen statement, the sources and the writing invariants whose method it takes from parity (quote conservation I5, the polarity list I10; `parity/SKILL.md:395-428, 903-919`).
  3. A failed claim check reverts the rewrite and records why.
  Statements are never rewritten: they are frozen at the first adopted ingest.
- **`tim backlog report --lint`** runs over the report and the answer sheet, and exits 1 when any of these appear (C-038, C-050):
  - an em dash or an en dash;
  - a question headline that is not a question;
  - an id shown without its headline;
  - a duration phrase next to a number ("3 days", "two hours") outside a quote;
  - a slot over its budget (5.9);
  - a sentence over 25 words outside a quote;
  - an abbreviation used without its expansion on first use in that section. The glossary of expansions lives in the `requirements-v2` profile.
- **The cold reader** (C-050). After each render, a `COLD_READER` task that has seen nothing else reads every section with content on its own (a section that renders empty is recorded "not read: empty" and costs no task, C-081), as a reader arriving from a link would, and fails any term the section uses without defining it there. Its record appears in section 11. This checks what the lint cannot: that each section really stands alone (6.1 rule 4).

---

## 7. The implementor skill and the build workflow

### 7.1 The skill: `backlog-implementor`

- **Folder:** `.claude/skills/backlog-implementor/`, a new skill written fresh. It takes the good ideas from `build-orchestrator`: the one-tier driver and its reason, that a subagent cannot invoke Workflow (`build-orchestrator/SKILL.md:12-25`), the landed check, the stop taxonomy and the handover prompt. `build-orchestrator` stays as it is, driving the legacy loop for live programmes, until inc-040 retires both (C-041, C-002). The new skill's commit message names that lineage.
- **Triggers:** "build the backlog", "build N increments", "build inc-012 to inc-020 with Codex", "Codex for the rest", "resume the build", "release inc-015", "why is inc-017 not building", "hand over the build".
- **Not for:** writing or changing requirements (use `requirements-distiller`); one agreed change to one repo (use `frontend-change` or `ticket`); comparing services (use `parity`).
- **Dispatcher:** `tim backlog next <p> --json` with `tim backlog run show <p> --json`. It prints `MODE:` (`START`, `RESUME`, `RELEASE`, `STOPPED` or `DONE`) and `Next:`.

**Procedure, in the main session:**

1. **Bindings from Sam's sentence.** Map the words to `tim backlog run bind` and `run set` (8.9). Print `tim backlog run explain` for the first increment affected, then write the binding and make the binding commit (7.6).
2. **Open the run:** `tim backlog run open <p> --label <runLabel> --mode build --at <ISO> --trailer-file <file> [--pin] --json` (C-080). One call does what were four steps: the preflight, the pin, `run start` and the stage schemas export, then any leading `light` sub-steps. The preflight inside it checks:
   - The workspace checkout is on `delivery.stateBranch`.
   - `tim` resolves this workspace, and prints the root it used. A `tim` linked from an older clone fails this (CLAUDE.md, `tim` section).
   - Codex is logged in when any binding names Codex: `tim backlog codex run --check` prints the effective configuration from the pipeline's Codex home and runs one real no-op resume (C-009, C-078).
   - Under `toolPin: launch-commit`, the pinned tim answers `--version --json` without a permission prompt; if it would prompt, the run stops with `environment` (7.12, C-064).
   It writes the args file and prints the Workflow args, with `firstStep` when it reached a span. `run start` is still the step inside it, and can be called on its own.
3. **Launch:** `Workflow({scriptPath: "<ABS>/.claude/workflows/backlog-build.js", args: {...}})`, with the args `run open` printed. Args are passed as a JSON object, never a string, because gap-4's one reproduced failure was stringified args.
4. **On return:**
   1. Read the returned value.
   2. Run `tim backlog run close <p> --label <runLabel> --json`. It runs any trailing `light` sub-steps that end in a stop, then `run end`, `counts` and the handover print. When `record` is `commit-push`, it returns the state push, which the skill runs as its own visible call (C-076).
   3. Print one line per increment built. For example: "inc-017 landed. Build drain. Built on Claude. 12 files reviewed, 3 findings fixed, CI green. 17 of 40 (43%)".
   4. Act on the stop reason (7.11).
5. **Handover.** At every stop, `run close` prints `tim backlog handover <p> --executor claude`; the skill shows it in a fenced block. Once per run, the skill also prints the command that regenerates the standing Codex handover, `tim backlog handover <p> --executor codex --md --out <workarea>/build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md`, so Sam can switch to Codex with no Claude session (C-003).

The skill's guard rails are the ones the orchestrator already has: "You orchestrate. You do not implement." (`build-orchestrator/SKILL.md:396-400`). It never edits state by hand and never merges by hand.

### 7.2 Cadence, and why there are two workflow files

**The script is a thin interpreter** (C-002). `.claude/workflows/backlog-build.js` is written new; it is not a moved copy of `increment-build-loop.js`, which stays working for legacy backlogs until inc-040. Its loop is (C-074, C-077):

1. Take `firstStep` from args when `run open` returned one; otherwise spawn a step agent. The step agent is Haiku at low effort with one fixed prompt (4.6, STEP AGENT): loop `tim backlog advance <p> --run <runLabel> --agent <runLabel>:<n> --json` through the pinned prefix (7.12), call it again on exit 75, run each `act` command verbatim as its own Bash call and stop at the first non-zero exit, and return the step on `run`, `handback` or `stop`. It makes no decision. `<n>` is the script's counter.
2. On `{status: run, span, tasks[], count, stageSha, then}`: check the relayed count and `stage.json` sha (8.2, C-070); walk the span graph with the one walker helper (4.6 contract item 6), spawning Claude tasks through the Claude adapter, and one Haiku Codex runner beside them when the span has Codex-bound tasks (8.2). A task starts when every `after` task has an accepted receipt and its `when` holds on their counts. An upstream that dies or is refused ends the walk for its dependants; the next `advance` re-derives them.
3. When the span is walked: if `stage.json` says `then: close`, return to the launching session; otherwise spawn a step agent.
4. On `{status: handback}` (the step agent reached `quality.stepBudget` calls, counted by tim per `--agent`): spawn a fresh step agent. On `{status: stop, stopReason}`: return the stop reason and the run summary.

There is no `act` and no `wait` branch in the script: the step agent runs outward calls itself and loops exit 75 itself. Every local, reversible step (fetch, merges of base, branches, rungs, the stack, manifests, commits, fact records, audits) runs inside `advance`, so a step agent is spent per gap between judgement spans, never per action, repo, rung, shard or PR.

Every transition rule lives in `advance`'s stage table, tested with vitest on fixture folders: verify-skip, fix rounds, repair budgets, `ciFixAttempts`, the baseline-red hygiene rule, `systemicHaltAfter`, holds and the resume point. The script holds none of them, so a Workflow run and a Codex-orchestrated session (8.11) cannot follow different rules. Merit: resume logic in untested workflow JS is where the legacy loop has failed before.

- **`drain` (default).** One Workflow run builds increments one after another. `advance` re-reads `next` after each increment and the bindings at every stage prepare, "so appended items are picked up without relaunch" (S). A ruling or a new discovered increment takes effect at the next increment. A change of executor takes effect from the next stage prepared (7.8, C-003).
- **No nested workflows.** One level of `workflow()` exists, but whether a child re-reads its file per call and resumes across children is "inferred, not documented" (G4). The drain needs no child.
- **The agent ceiling.** The script counts the agents it spawns, step agents included.
  - Before starting an increment, it stops cleanly with `agent-ceiling` when fewer than 200 agents remain under `quality.agentCeiling` (850 by default, below the runtime's cap of 1,000). A typical increment spends about 43 agents (39 judgement, 4 step agents; 8.8), so a run covers about 16 increments before the 200-agent reserve.
  - Before walking any span, it checks that the span's task count fits under the runtime cap. If it does not, it stops with the increment resumable at that span.
  - The skill relaunches straight away with a new run label, and the next run resumes from receipts on disk.
  - **Owed to Sam before the first build run:** raise "Dynamic workflow size" in `/config`. One increment uses about 43 agents, and agents cannot change that setting. Until it is raised, the drain meets the runtime's own size limit long before the agent ceiling. This is owed item 1 in 9.2.
- **`cadence: one`** builds exactly one increment, then stops with `count-reached`. It is for canaries and debugging.
- **Two files, not one.** Distil and build share the task adapter and the rails, but not their stage tables, args or cadence.
  - One file would put a distil edit in the path of a running build.
  - Each loop reads on its own: a person or agent resuming a build reads only the build's stages, args and stops, and one resuming a distillation only the distiller's.
  - The shared helpers are duplicated by design between marker comments. `workflow-contract.test.js` asserts that the copies are byte-identical, as it does for the rails (4.6).

### 7.3 The args contract

```jsonc
{
  "programme": "requirements-pipeline",     // required: a registry key
  "runLabel": "build-rp-2026-09-18a",        // required: registered by tim backlog run open
  "at": "2026-09-18T15:00:00Z",              // required: the script has no clock
  "commitTrailer": "Co-Authored-By: ...",    // required: from the launching session's attribution
  "workspace": {                             // required: absolute for Read and Write, tilde for Bash
    "abs": "<absolute workspace path, from pwd -P>",
    "tilde": "~/git/defra/trade-imports-workspace" },
  "cadence": "drain",                        // drain | one
  "count": "all",                            // a number, or "all"
  "from": null,                              // optional: start here if it is buildable
  "pin": null,                               // {commit, prefix} when run.json sets toolPin: launch-commit
  "mode": "build",                           // build | review-only (stages 0 and 12 to 15 on landed work, for 8.10)
  "firstStep": null                          // optional: the span step run open reached, so no step agent is spent first
}
```

- **First lines.** `const A = typeof args === 'string' ? JSON.parse(args) : args`. The script then throws, naming each missing required key, and logs the resolved configuration as its first act (S §7.1.3). There is no FALLBACK object: FA's `FALLBACK` (`FA:46-59`) is what turned stringified args into a silent run on defaults (G4).
- **The trailer comes from args.** The attribution line changes with the model, and FA hard-coded it (`FA:134-138`).
- **Preflight** (stage 0) runs in `tim backlog run open`, in the launching session, before the Workflow starts (C-080). It checks three things:
  - The workspace is on the state branch. So tim's auto-pull, which acts only on `main` (`tim/src/exec/auto-pull.js:24-48`), never moves files under a running build.
  - `tim` resolves the same root as `workspace.abs`.
  - With `args.pin` set, the pinned tim answers `--version --json` headless, without a permission prompt. If it would prompt, the run stops with `environment` (7.12, C-064).
- **`firstStep` and resume.** A `firstStep` in args makes them differ from the earlier run's, so `resumeFromRunId` does not apply to a relaunch that carries one. That costs nothing: resume never depends on replaying the journal, because `advance` derives the resume point from state, receipts and fact records on disk (7.8).

### 7.4 The build stages

This is the canonical stage order (change list, "Canonical build stage order"). `advance`'s stage table in tim holds the same numbers, and 2.1 mirrors them. Each stage names its tier on the Claude side. Bindable stages follow `run.json`. "tim, `<macro>`" means `advance` runs the stage in-process through that tested macro (4.2, C-075), which a person or a Codex-orchestrated session can also call on its own. "Step agent" means the one cheap agent for the run of mechanical sub-steps the row sits in (C-074). "Visible call" means an outward command, generated by tim after its own refusals pass, that the step agent runs as its own Bash call and the next `advance` observes (C-076). "Run or span" names the mechanical run (M0 to M5) or judgement span (J1 to J4) the stage belongs to, as in the 2.1 build box; sub-step ids with a letter (7f, 8f, 11r, 26d, 26s) are rows of tim's stage table, so holds, fact records, receipts and resume keep that granularity. "Hold" means `advance` records `tim backlog state hold` with that reason, then the drain moves to the next buildable increment unless the stop rules in 7.11 apply.

| # | Stage | Run or span | Kind | Runs as | Bindable | Writes, through tim | On failure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Resolve (once per run) | M0 | mechanical, light | `tim backlog run open`, launching session: preflight (7.3), the pin, `run start`, `stage schemas --out`; no agent (C-080) | no | run registration | throw: a configuration error |
| 1 | Next | M1 | mechanical | tim, `prepare`, inside `advance`, with the bindings | no | none | stop `all-done` or `none-buildable`, with the reasons |
| 2 | Ticket | M1 | outward | visible call `tim jira create`, generated by `prepare` when `ticketing` is set; observed by the ticket carrying label `<p>/<inc>` | no | `state.ticket` | hold `stage-failed` |
| 3 | Branch | M1 | mechanical | tim, `prepare`: check out, track or cut the same branch name in every repo of the surface (CLAUDE.md rule 2); the sync merge is pushed with land | no | `state.branches` | hold `stage-failed` |
| 4 | Baseline | M1 | mechanical | tim, `prepare`: `heads --for <inc> --strict`, tracked files only, the registry's `generated` excluded (C-065) | no | `state.heads` | hold `dirty-tree` |
| 5 | Sync | M1 | mechanical | tim, `prepare`: parallel fetch, `merge-tree --write-tree` probe, a local merge of base; any conflict aborts, leaves no partial merge and holds, never resolved by an agent (`FA:613-686`). No push: the merge travels with land (7.6, `quality.pushSyncMerge`) | no | merge commits, fact records | hold `sync-blocked`, naming the repo |
| 6 | Ladder facts | M1 | mechanical | tim, `prepare`, via `rung` per rung of every touched repo, in parallel across repos except `exclusive` rungs; sliced with exit 75 | no | `state.baselineLadder`, fact records | a red rung becomes a hygiene item through `discover`, carrying the regression criterion (C-032); this increment is held `baseline-red` with `releasedBy` naming it |
| 7 | Plan | J1 | model | think, or Codex high, read-only | yes | `build/plans/<id>.md`, `state.plan` with seams, invariants and `reposAdded[]` (7.5) | see stage 8 |
| 8 | Plan audit | J1 | model | think, or Codex high, read-only; a different task, started in the span when the plan is accepted; on `revise`, one more plan and audit in the same span | yes | audit record | one revise round, then hold `plan-refused` |
| 7f, 8f | Finalise the plan | M2 | mechanical | tim, `advance` | no | `build/plans/<id>.md`, `state.plan` | hold `stage-failed` |
| 9 | Added-repo set-up | M2 | mechanical | tim, `prepare --repos <keys>`, only when the approved plan names a repo not in `state.branches`: the surface amendment, then stages 3 to 6 for the added repos (C-031) | no | the member atom's `surface.amendments[]`, `state.plan.reposAdded`, the added repos' branches, heads and baseline ladder | as stages 3 to 6 |
| 10 | Implement | J2 | model | doer, or Codex medium, workspace-write with network; standards are the union of planned-file routing and repo-level routing for every surface repo (C-037) | yes | the working tree; `state.implement` | wip commit, hold `implement-failed` |
| 11 | Implement check | M3 | mechanical, with resume | tim, `check --after implement`: the browser and Docker rungs of the changed repos and every command invariant (C-017, C-008); on red, `advance` returns the same implement task as `run`, resumed with the log and `error-context.md`, at most 2 rounds for both executors | resume only | `state.implementCheck`, fact records | wip commit, hold `implement-failed` |
| 12 | Manifest | M3 | mechanical | tim, `check`: `stage manifest`, diffed from the attempt's pinned heads (C-068), then `stage prepare --span review` | no | the manifest; `rulesGap[]` (C-037); proof that no commit or ref move happened; refusal of protected-path changes (C-010) | hold `stage-failed`, also for a changed repo not in the plan (C-031) |
| 13 | Review | J3 | model | per content-changed file: style and code (doer, or Codex medium), each with `input/slice.json` (C-022); one consistency task over every touched repo with a verdict per plan seam (think, or Codex high) (C-023); all read-only | yes | findings; seam verdicts | a missing receipt retries that task once; a `completed: false` output gets one continuation (C-006); then hold `review-failed` |
| 14 | Verify | J3 | model | per file with findings, started in the span when both of that file's reviews are accepted, plus one verify-consistency task when the consistency output has findings (C-024): doer, or Codex medium, read-only; default refuted; `when: findings > 0` (C-081) | yes | verdicts | a dead task leaves its findings recorded as "unrefuted" (fail open) |
| 15 | Judge | J3 | model | think, or Codex high, read-only; rules on file and consistency findings; runs when any finding stands or is unsure | yes | rulings: fix now, defer or reject; `discovered[]` | hold `judge-failed` |
| 26d | Discovered-atom verifier | J3 | model | `REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER`, think, a different task; `when: discovered > 0` (7.10) | yes | a verdict per discovered atom | the atom stays unadopted; `record` holds `record-failed` naming it |
| 16 | Fix | J3 | model | doer, or Codex medium, workspace-write; `REVIEW_ITEM_FIXER` files may span repos (C-024); `when: fixNow > 0`; its `stage accept` saves the fix diff from the pre-fix fingerprint | yes | the working tree; the fix diff | hold `fix-failed` |
| 17 | Fix verify | J3 | model | doer, or Codex medium, read-only; a different task, in span J3 before the post-fix check, reading the saved fix diff; the consistency task runs again when a fix touches a seam file (C-021) | yes | a confirmation per item | one more fix round and fix-verify in the span, then hold `fix-failed` |
| 11r | Implement check after the fix | M4 | mechanical, with resume | tim, `check --after fix` (C-017); on red, `advance` resumes the fixer, and fix-verify runs again because its `upstreamShas` no longer match the final fix diff | resume only | `state.implementCheck`, fact records | wip commit, hold `fix-failed` |
| 18 | Ladder | M4 | mechanical, with repair | tim, `prove`, via `rung`: registry rungs then the plan's `ladderExtras` (command invariants included, C-008); on red, `advance` returns a repair task (doer, or Codex medium) as `run`, at most 3 across the ladder, each followed by `check --after repair` and a repair-review span (C-021); a rung timeout is recorded `could-not-run:timeout` (C-019) | repair only | `state.ladder`; repair-review receipts; fact records | wip commit, hold `ladder-red`; a timeout holds `environment`, naming the rung and its release command |
| 19 | Secrets | M4 | mechanical | tim, `prove`: `secrets --inc` | no | `state.secrets` | hold `secrets-red` |
| 20 | Integration proof | M4 | mechanical, with repair | tim, `prove`, sliced with exit 75: `tim docker dev` with the include list; one `rung --shard i/n` per shard; one retry of failed shards; `tim docker down` always last (C-019); then `stage prepare` for acceptance. e2e-repair is a bindable model task, followed by `check --after repair` and a repair-review span | repair only | `state.integrationProof`, the record in the stage details (C-020) | hold `e2e-red`; a timeout holds `environment`; no Docker in a Codex-orchestrated session: hold `proof-owed` (8.11) |
| 21 | Acceptance | J4 | model | a fresh task: think, or Codex high, read-only; never receives the plan (C-021) | yes | per-criterion results, with a passing test per `e2e` or `contract` criterion | hold `acceptance-red` |
| 22 | Land | M5 | mechanical, then outward | tim, `land`: commits with `-F`, the trailer and a `Backlog-Increment:` trailer, in merge order; branch proof and fast-forward check against the observed remote ref; then one visible `git -C … push -u origin refs/heads/<b>:refs/heads/<b>` per repo (`FA:125-138`, C-002, C-076) | no | `state.commits`; the push observed by `git ls-remote` | hold `land-failed`, naming the repos that did and did not land |
| 23 | PR | M5 | outward | visible `tim github pr ensure-draft` per repo, generated only where tim observes no open PR | no | `state.prs` | hold `pr-failed` |
| 24 | CI | M5 | mechanical | tim, `ci-wait`, sliced with exit 75: every PR's checks for its head through octokit; SonarCloud from the PR checks, silence recorded as "no report". A red check returns the CI fixer (doer, or Codex medium, workspace-write with network) as `run`, at most `ciFixAttempts`, each fix followed by a repair-review span. When `quality.integrationProofSource` is `ci`, stages 20 and 21 run after this one (C-035) | fixer only | `state.ci`, `state.sonar` | hold `ci-red`; an owed platform fix is `platform-blocked` and the drain continues |
| 25 | Merge | M5 | outward | visible `tim github pr merge --expect-head` per repo, in `consumes` order, generated only when every PR is green and approved where required and `delivery.merge` carries a person's decision (7.6, C-026) | no | `state.merged`, observed | stop `awaiting-approval`, `awaiting-merge` or `changes-requested`; hold `main-red` or `pr-left-open` |
| 26 | Record | M5 | mechanical | tim, `record`: adopts atoms the verifier in span J3 confirmed (7.10); `tim backlog status <id> done --done-by build`, which runs the stage audit; `tim backlog report`; `handover --executor codex --md --out build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md` (C-003); the state commit by name | no | status, report, handover, commit | hold `record-failed`, listing the audit's problems |
| 26s | State push | M5 | outward | visible `git -C … push` of the state branch, generated by `record` when `record` is `commit-push` | no | observed by `git ls-remote` | hold `record-failed` |
| 27 | Checkpoint | M5 | mechanical | tim, in-process; in a drain the same step agent carries on into the next increment's M1 | no | none | stop `checkpoint` |

The old routing stages (repo level, planned files and changed files) are gone (C-037). `stage prepare` resolves standards in-process through `tim backlog standards` for every task it prepares (8.6), so routing costs no agent and records content hashes.

**Agents per increment.** J1 to J4 spend the judgement tasks; M2, M3, M4 and M5 (which carries the next increment's M1 in a drain) spend one step agent each, so a typical increment spends 4 step agents, 5 when it is the first of a run. The count does not grow with repos, rungs, shards, PRs or files. Each red implement check, repair round, CI fix or post-fix red adds one step agent. Under `quality.integrationProofSource: ci`, M4 runs the ladder and secrets only, M5 lands, publishes and waits, then returns J4 as `run`, and a second M5 agent records: one more step agent per increment.

**Stage details:**

- **Prove, then accept, then land** (C-021). The ladder (18), secrets (19), integration proof (20) and acceptance (21) all run before anything is pushed, so a slice that fails its proof is never landed. `needs-e2e` no longer exists: acceptance runs after the proof and sees its per-test results.
- **Acceptance never sees the plan** (C-021). Its inputs are `brief.json`, the manifest diffs, the ladder and proof logs and the per-test results. `stage prepare` never copies the plan or the plan's output into an acceptance task, and the stage audit checks that. For each `e2e` or `contract` criterion, acceptance names a passing test it has read and judged to exercise the criterion, without using `acceptanceMap`. With no such test, the criterion is `not-met`. Judgement invariants (7.5) are assessed here, from the header invariants in `brief.json`.
- **The integration proof record** (R7, C-020). Stage 20 writes `state.integrationProof`:

  ```jsonc
  { "mode": "local",                                  // local | ci
    "stack": [{ "service": "plants-backend", "repoKey": "plantsBackend", "source": "local", "ref": "<head sha>" }],
    "specs": [{ "path": "tests/plants/origin.spec.js", "title": "refuses a country outside the list", "result": "passed" }],
    "newOrChanged": ["tests/plants/origin.spec.js"],
    "run": "build-rp-2026-09-18a", "at": "2026-09-18T16:10:00Z" }
  ```

  - `specs` come from the runner's JSON reporter, never from an agent's summary.
  - Every touched repo's service is built from its head for the attempt, or from an image resolved to its branch tag, never a `:latest` fallback. Every untouched service comes from `:latest` or a clean checkout of its base. `tim docker dev` takes an include list, so an untouched mid-spike checkout is never built from source.
  - At least one spec is new or changed, unless the plan's evidence check names an existing spec that already proves the criterion.
  - The stage audit checks all of this (4.5).
- **Sharding** (C-019). Each shard is one `tim backlog rung --shard i/n` sub-step running the standard `test:docker-compose` script, never raw npx. `n` is sized from the last recorded suite duration so every shard ends under 540 seconds. The proof is the merged shard fact records, logs and JSON reporter output. A Sonnet runner exercised no judgement, and its result was a model's report (principle 8), so the suite now runs inside tim.
- **Where the proof comes from** (C-035). `quality.integrationProofSource` in `run.json` (7.7) says `local`, `ci` or `either`. Under `ci`, stages 20 and 21 run after stage 24, reading the proof from the named CI workflow's end-to-end check against branch-tagged images (`FA:1307-1366`), with the same record. It lives in run state, not in `delivery`, because it is a run choice (R5).
- **`localE2e: auto`** runs stage 20 for every increment that touches a product repo. It skips, recording why, only when nothing the stack serves changed (`FA:1146-1152`).
- **Tooling increments** (C-033). A `feat` or `fix` increment whose repos are all workspace or tooling proves integration by running the tool's real entry point (CLI, workflow or skill) against a fixture programme, through `tim backlog rung`. The record holds that run's exit code and output.
- **A hygiene fix in one product repo** (C-032). The item stage 6 writes carries a regression criterion: the rung that was red is green and, when the stack serves the repo, the existing end-to-end suite is green on the slice's stack. That is its proof; it needs no tests-repo change.
- **Repair review** (C-021). Every repair diff (ladder-repair, e2e-repair, ci-fix) gets a style and a code review per changed file, plus the consistency task again when the diff touches a plan seam or more than one repo. Findings go through verify and judge. The stage audit requires a repair-review receipt for every repair diff.
- **Added repos** (C-031). Stage 9 runs after the plan audit approves. `advance` calls `tim backlog rule --subject surface:<atom> --add-repo <key> --why <text> --by build` for the member atom, records `state.plan.reposAdded[{repoKey, why}]`, then runs stages 3 to 6 for the added repos only. The existing repos' stages are not repeated. The plan's file list never writes the backlog.
- **Merge and done.** Stage 25 runs only under `per-increment` delivery. Under `programme`, the done point is CI green after land (3.9).

### 7.5 The plan, and the plan audit

**The plan outline**, from FA's (`FA:687-731`) with the requirement-facing items added:

| # | Section | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Decisions | Every choice the requirement leaves open, and how it was settled |
| 1 | Evidence check | For each citation `tim backlog evidence` flags as changed or missing, whether the requirement still holds against the live code. A requirement the code already meets is recorded as met, with its proof, and never silently skipped |
| 2 | Moves | from and to, for every moved or deleted file; "None" is an answer |
| 3 | Edits | Per file: what changes and why |
| 4 | New files | Intent, and the existing file of the same kind read first (R3) |
| 5 | Tests | Tests that move, change and are new, and what each pins, including the integration proof (R7) |
| 6 | Invariants to prove | The header invariants this change could break. Each is a `command` invariant with a runnable rung, or a `judgement` invariant that acceptance assesses (C-008) |
| 7 | Seams | Every contract between repos the change touches: the provider, the consumer and what changes (C-023) |
| 8 | Acceptance map | Every member criterion, the proof that will show it and where. It guides the build; acceptance never reads it (C-021) |
| 9 | Out of scope | What to leave alone, including neighbouring increments' work |

There is no lifecycle content: no branch, ticket, PR, push or merge steps (S: "The planner can overreach into lifecycle").

**Plan output schema:**

```jsonc
{
  "ok": true, "planMarkdown": "...",
  "repos": [{ "repoKey": "plantsBackend", "why": "the saved notification refuses the country" }],
  "files": [{ "repoKey": "plantsFrontend", "path": "src/...", "change": "edit" }],
  "ladderExtras": [{ "repoKey": "tests", "dir": ".", "tool": "npm", "name": "test:fit" }],
  "acceptanceMap": [{ "member": "req-022", "criterion": "ac-1", "proof": "e2e", "where": "tests: origin journey" }],
  "invariants": [
    { "id": "inv-copy-bilingual", "kind": "command", "repoKey": "plantsFrontend",
      "rung": { "dir": ".", "tool": "npm", "name": "test:copy-keys" }, "why": "the Welsh copy file has every new key" },
    { "id": "inv-no-pii-in-logs", "kind": "judgement", "repoKey": null, "rung": null,
      "why": "the new log lines carry no importer details" }],
  "seams": [{ "id": "seam-1", "kind": "http",
    "provider": { "repoKey": "plantsBackend", "what": "PUT /notifications/{id}/origin, field countryOfOrigin" },
    "consumer": { "repoKey": "plantsFrontend", "what": "origin controller's backend client" },
    "change": "the backend refuses a country outside the list with 422 and code ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED" }],
  "integrationProof": { "kind": "e2e", "where": "tests repo compose suite", "why": "the country is refused end to end" },
  "outOfScope": ["..."], "notes": "..."
}
```

- **Invariants** (C-008). A `command` invariant names an npm script or a committed script `tim backlog rung` can run; `stage accept` refuses one that does not. It joins `ladderExtras`, so it runs at stage 11 (implement check) and stage 18 (ladder), and its result is a fact record tim wrote (C-082), never a model's claim. A `judgement` invariant is assessed by the acceptance task from the header invariants in `brief.json`.
- **Seams** (C-023). `kind` is one of `http`, `message`, `document`, `reference-data` or `test-contract` (the tests repo's page objects, fixtures and API clients against the DOM and API).
- **Persisting.** `stage accept --finalise` writes `build/plans/<id>.md` from `planMarkdown` and persists `state.plan`, fixing "`planFile` is returned but never persisted" (S).
- **Repos the plan adds** (C-030, C-031). Repos follow the behaviour (R7), so the plan may add a repo, with a reason. The addition persists as `state.plan.reposAdded[{repoKey, why}]`, and stage 9 (7.4) amends the atom's surface and sets the repo up. The report counts these as "repos the distiller missed" (6.2).

**The plan audit** is a different task. It reads the backlog row (`input/brief.json`: the members' current acceptance, invariants and rulings) and the plan, but not the planner's notes. It derives the requirement from the row, never from the plan's restatement of it (C-060). It returns `{verdict: approve | revise, objections[{kind, criterion, detail}]}`. It asks for a revision when:

- a criterion is dropped, narrowed or widened, or has no proof in the acceptance map;
- the plan contains lifecycle content;
- the plan splits the slice: it leaves out a repo the behaviour needs, or defers a layer to "a later increment" (R7);
- a product increment has no integration proof;
- the plan touches two or more product repos and names no seam (C-023);
- an invariant has neither a runnable rung nor the `judgement` kind (C-008);
- the plan does work that belongs to another increment;
- the plan contradicts a ruling. `RULING_RULE`: "Never reopen the question, never soften the ruling into an option, never widen it" (`FA:160-163`);
- `build-cost-reasoning`: a plan choice is justified by effort, file count, diff size or new surface, rather than by what it is like in use (R8, C-001);
- `template-adoption`: the plan adopts a cited file as a template without a stated decision. Citations in `brief.json` are labelled "evidence of current behaviour, not a design to copy" (C-063).

One revise round follows: a second plan task, under the same binding, receives the objections verbatim. A second `revise` holds the increment `plan-refused`, with the objections in the journal.

### 7.6 Lifecycle and merge, by delivery strategy

| | `programme` (default, Q24) | `per-increment` | `local` |
|---|---|---|---|
| Branch | `delivery.branch`, the same name in every repo | `<class>/<ticket or NO_JIRA>-<slug>` per increment, the same name in every repo | as configured; never pushed |
| Ticket | none by default | one per increment when `ticketing` is set | none |
| Pull requests | one per repo, draft, reused across increments; exit 3 from `tim github pr ensure-draft` when only a merged or closed PR exists | one per repo per increment | none |
| Merge | `never`; Sam merges the programme at the end (`feedback_stack_programme_on_one_branch`) | by `merge.policy`: `never` pauses at `awaiting-merge`; `on-approval`; `on-green` only with a person's decision | none |
| Done point | landed, and CI green | merged | landed as a local commit |
| Red work | a wip commit made by tim, then its push as a visible call that `advance` generates after the branch proof and fast-forward check and observes by `git ls-remote`, then the hold (C-075, C-076) | as `programme` | a stash of named paths only (`git stash push -u -- <paths>`), then a hold (`feedback_build_loop_rollback_non_destructive`) |

**Landed as one unit** (R7):

- The land stage commits every touched repo in merge order inside `tim backlog land`, then generates one visible push per repo in the same order (C-075, C-076). The sync merge of base travels with this push; it is never pushed on its own unless `quality.pushSyncMerge` is set (7.7). tim records each push by reading the remote ref. If a push is not observed, the increment is held `land-failed`, naming what landed. The next run completes the rest and never re-commits a landed repo: `land` finds its own commit again by its `Backlog-Increment:` trailer.
- Under `per-increment` delivery, every PR is green, and approved where required, before any PR merges. A failed merge after a successful one stops with `pr-left-open` (`increment-build-loop.js:303-309, 1803`).

**Merge order** is the topological order of the header's `consumes` (3.3, C-026), providers first: a repo merges after every repo it consumes. Roles alone cannot say which repo provides and which consumes. The tests repo consumes every product repo, so it merges last: backend, then frontend, then tests. This departs from the loop's `MERGE_RANK` (`:299-301`), which put tests before the frontends, on merit: merged first, the tests repo's main would carry specs for behaviour no product repo's main has yet, and every other build's E2E against main would go red until the frontend landed. Merged last, each main only ever exercises behaviour that is already there. Only a ruling that writes `delivery.merge.order` with explicit pairs changes it. `check` refuses a cycle in `consumes`, so the order always exists. The same order drives the land stage's pushes and `tim backlog report --merge-plan`, which the handover carries (7.11).

**The state commit.** After each record stage, `tim backlog record` commits these files by name on `delivery.stateBranch`, with the commit rule, and returns the state push as a visible call when `record` is `commit-push` (stage 26s, C-076):

- `backlog.json`;
- `build/state.json` and `build/journal.jsonl`;
- `build/run.json`, which holds the executor bindings and run knobs in force (7.7);
- `build/plans/<id>.md`;
- `report/report.md` and `report/answer-sheet.md`;
- `build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md` (C-003).

It never uses `git add -A`.

**The binding commit.** `build/run.json` also changes outside a record stage, when Sam's sentence becomes a `run bind` or a `run set` (8.9). After each such write the skill commits `build/run.json` and `build/journal.jsonl` by name on `delivery.stateBranch`, with the commit rule and a message quoting the `--said` sentence (for `run set`, naming the knob and its value), and pushes with the push rule, before it launches or resumes a run. So a binding is in git before any stage is prepared under it, and a session that starts from the handover, on either executor, reads the same bindings. A write made while a run is live is committed the same way; the next record stage's state commit then finds `build/run.json` unchanged or includes the later write.

### 7.7 `build/run.json`

```jsonc
{
  "schemaVersion": 1,
  "defaultPreset": "claude",
  "bindings": [
    { "ids": ["inc-012", "inc-020"], "preset": "codex",
      "said": "build inc-012 to inc-020 with Codex", "by": "sam", "at": "2026-09-18T15:00:00Z" },
    { "from": "inc-021", "to": null, "roles": { "judge": "claude" },
      "said": "keep the judge on Claude from here", "by": "sam", "at": "2026-09-18T16:20:00Z" }
  ],
  "cadence": "drain",
  "codex": { "model": null },
  "quality": {
    "ciFixAttempts": 2, "localE2e": "auto", "systemicHaltAfter": 3, "agentCeiling": 850,
    "codexParallel": { "readOnly": 8, "write": 2 }, "opusConcurrency": 5, "reviewCap": null,
    "acceptanceCheck": true, "readingAudit": true,
    "integrationProofSource": "local", "integrationProofWorkflow": null,
    "stepBudget": 40, "rungParallel": true, "pushSyncMerge": false
  },
  "checkout": "live",
  "record": "commit-push",
  "toolPin": "none"
}
```

**How bindings resolve:**

- Bindings apply in order, and the last one that matches wins, over `defaultPreset`.
- `ids` is an inclusive range by position in `increments[]`.
- `from: "next"` is resolved to a concrete id when the binding is written, so a binding never moves after the fact.
- A binding names a preset or a set of role overrides.

**Quality knobs:**

| Knob | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ciFixAttempts` | 2 | CI and end-to-end fix rounds before a hold (`FA:409`; the loop's default is 3, `:123`) |
| `localE2e` | `auto` | Run the integration proof for every increment that touches a product repo (R7). `never` is refused by `check` for a programme with product repos unless a decision allows it |
| `systemicHaltAfter` | 3 | Stop when this many increments in a row are held for the same reason: that is a pipeline fault, not an increment's |
| `agentCeiling` | 850 | Clean stop below the runtime's 1,000-agent cap (7.2) |
| `codexParallel` | `{readOnly: 8, write: 2}` | Codex tasks run at once by `tim backlog codex run`, by sandbox. Read-only tasks cannot collide, so more run at once; writing tasks share working trees, so fewer do (C-013) |
| `opusConcurrency` | 5 | Opus tasks at once (S §7.4) |
| `reviewCap` | `null` | No cap. A number caps the files reviewed, and every file beyond the cap is listed in the report as not reviewed; nothing is dropped silently (judge 1, graft 22) |
| `acceptanceCheck` | `true` | Run stage 21 |
| `readingAudit` | `true` | Enforced at accept: a missing "must" file is a problem inside the three correction rounds, for both executors. Summarised after each run (8.10, C-011) |
| `integrationProofSource` | `local` | `local`, `ci` or `either`: where the integration proof comes from (7.4, C-035). A run choice, so it lives here and never in `delivery` (R5). A full-access Codex orchestrator can run `local` itself (8.11) |
| `integrationProofWorkflow` | `null` | The CI workflow whose end-to-end check is the proof under `ci` or `either`: the reference `delivery.crossRepoE2e` used to hold |
| `stepBudget` | 40 | Bash calls one step agent makes before `advance` returns `handback` and the script spawns a fresh one. tim counts the calls per `--agent` id in run state, because a low-effort agent counting its own calls is not reliable (C-074) |
| `rungParallel` | `true` | Rungs of different repos run at once inside `prepare`, `check` and `prove`, except registry rungs marked `exclusive` (4.2), which run alone. `false` runs every rung in turn |
| `pushSyncMerge` | `false` | The sync merge of base travels with the increment's push at land (7.6). `true` makes `prepare` return a visible push after a clean merge: twice the pushes, and an unproven merge exposed early |

**`checkout: live | clones`** (C-065). `live` builds in the repos under `repos/` and the workspace checkout. `clones` builds in fresh clones under `workareas/clones/`, as FA's mode does. `clones` is the default when the programme changes the pipeline's own tools (7.12), because another session's work in a shared checkout then never meets the build's own tracked changes. Under either, fingerprints and dirty checks use tracked files only, and the registry's `generated` globs (including `workareas/shared/<p>/**`) are excluded (4.5).

`record: commit-push | commit | none` sets what the state commit does. `toolPin` is covered in 7.12. `codex.model` overrides the model named in the pipeline's `tools/codex/home/config.toml` (8.4, C-009).

### 7.8 Holds, retry and resume

- **A hold** is recorded under the increment in `build/state.json` as `{reason, stage, attempt, run, note, releasedBy, release, heldAt}`. `release` is the exact command that releases it. `next` withholds a held increment.
- **Hold reasons**, one closed list that the schema enforces:
  - before the change: `dirty-tree`, `sync-blocked`, `baseline-red`, `plan-refused`;
  - making and checking it: `implement-failed`, `review-failed`, `judge-failed`, `fix-failed`, `ladder-red`, `secrets-red`, `acceptance-red`;
  - landing it: `land-failed`, `pr-failed`, `ci-red`, `platform-blocked`, `e2e-red`, `proof-owed`;
  - waiting on a person: `awaiting-approval`, `awaiting-merge`, `changes-requested`;
  - after merging: `main-red`, `pr-left-open`;
  - anything else: `record-failed`, `stage-failed`, `environment` (a port in use, a stack that will not start, a missing login, a rung that timed out, C-019).
- **Hold mapping.** Which failure becomes which hold, and at which stage, is a rule in `advance`'s stage table, tested with vitest on fixture folders (C-002). The workflow script maps nothing.
- **Release.** `tim backlog state <p> <id> release --note` releases a hold. A hold with `releasedBy` releases itself when that increment is done, as with a red baseline and its hygiene increment.
- **Retry.** `tim backlog state <p> <id> retry --from <stage>` starts a new attempt at that stage. Receipts from the earlier attempt are reused only while the repos' heads match the heads that attempt pinned.
- **Resume within a run.** `resumeFromRunId`, with the same script and args, replays completed agents from the journal. It replays harmlessly, and nothing depends on it: args that carry a `firstStep` differ from the earlier run's, so it does not apply to such a relaunch, and `advance` finds the resume point from disk anyway (7.3).
- **Resume within a mechanical run** (C-082). Each macro skips a sub-step whose fact record exists at the current heads and tree fingerprint, so resume starts at the first sub-step without a fact record at the current heads. Outward steps already observed (a remote ref, a PR, a merge, a ticket) are not repeated. A step agent killed mid-macro is replaced, and the next one carries on at the next sub-step.
- **Resume across runs.** `advance` works out where to resume from persisted state alone (C-002). The rule lives in its stage table, not in the script, and is tested on fixtures:
  - Before land: the first stage of the latest attempt with no accepted receipt, if the heads still match; otherwise a new attempt from stage 4.
  - After land: the loop's rule, ported and tested. All PRs merged means done. PRs present means CI. Commits but no PRs means the PR stage (`increment-build-loop.js:938-946`).
  - Never from the ticket's status (S: "resumeAt derived only from persisted lifecycle fields").
- **Across executors** (C-003). R6's "Codex for the rest" is said when Claude runs out, often mid-increment, and presets already mix executors inside one increment, so the increment boundary serves no quality purpose. Bindings resolve per task at every stage prepare, and at every `advance`, and each task's executor is recorded in `stage.json`:
  1. A running task finishes on its executor.
  2. Prepared but unstarted tasks are re-prepared under the new binding.
  3. Partial Codex task folders are archived as `abandoned-codex-<n>/`, as today.
  4. When a writing stage changes executor after a partial attempt, a dirty tree becomes a wip commit, `wip(<programme>): <inc> handed over from <executor>`, and the new writer receives that commit's manifest and the plan.
  5. The stage audit accepts only receipts whose executor equals the executor `stage.json` recorded for that task.

### 7.9 Nothing succeeds silently, and the done gate

| Failure | What happens today | What catches it |
|---|---|---|
| An agent dies and returns null | `reviewResults.filter(Boolean)` (`increment-build-loop.js:1250`) reads as fewer findings | A missing receipt fails the stage audit; review retries the task once |
| A Codex reviewer crashes | The loop throws (`build-orchestrator/SKILL.md:390-394`) | `tim backlog codex run` exits 1 naming the task, and the stage fails |
| A step agent relays a wrong count, exit or span graph | Trusted | The audit re-derives it from disk; exits are hints, results are observed (C-070, C-076) |
| A conditional task is skipped wrongly | Not checked | The next `advance` re-derives the span and runs it; the audit fails otherwise (C-081) |
| A mechanical step's result is edited or invented | Unseen | Every sub-step has a fact record whose `factMac` the audit verifies (C-082) |
| Codex commits or pushes | Unseen | The manifest proves heads and remote refs are unmoved (judge 3) |
| "Pre-existing" red | Accepted in prose (G §2.1) | `stage accept` refuses it unless a baseline record shows the same failure on base |
| "The ladder is green" | The agent's word (`FA:996`) | Fact records written by `tim backlog rung` (C-082) |
| "CI is green" | The agent's word | `tim github pr checks` exit codes, the same function `ci-wait` calls: 2 for unresolved, 4 for no checks; neither is ever green |
| "It was pushed" | The agent's word | The remote ref read back with `git ls-remote`; the PR, merge or ticket read back through octokit or `jira-client` (C-076) |
| SonarCloud says nothing | Assumed clean | Recorded as "no report" |
| The judge drops a finding | Unseen | The audit requires a ruling for every verified finding, file and consistency alike (C-024) |
| A cross-repo finding goes nowhere | `contractFindings[]` never reach a verifier, the judge or a fixer | Consistency findings take the review finding shape and go through verify, judge, fix and fix-verify (C-024) |
| A seam is never checked | The consistency reviewer checks peer patterns only | A seam verdict other than `not-checked` for every plan seam (C-023) |
| A reviewer runs out mid-review | Reads as complete | `completed` and `notReached` on every checking output; one continuation, then hold `review-failed` (C-006) |
| A fix is not applied | Unseen | Fix-verify confirms each item |
| A repair lands unreviewed | Ladder, e2e and CI repairs reach done unreviewed | A repair-review receipt for every repair diff (C-021) |
| A criterion is never checked | Unseen | Acceptance records one result per criterion, and the audit requires all to be met |
| An e2e criterion is "met" by a green suite that never exercises it | The plan's `acceptanceMap` is the only link | Acceptance names a passing test it read for each `e2e` or `contract` criterion, or records `not-met` (C-021) |
| The integration proof is skipped | FA's local run was opt-in (`FA:64`) | The audit requires `state.integrationProof` for every product increment, with its stack sources, per-test results and new or changed specs (R7, C-020) |
| Standards files are not read | Self-reported `rulesRead[]` | The reading audit, enforced at accept inside the correction rounds (4.5, C-011) |
| A changed file's rules were never given to its writer | Unseen, and Codex writers got rules for planned files only | The manifest's `rulesGap[]` must be empty, for either executor (C-037) |
| A read-only task edits a repo | Unseen | Tree fingerprints before and after the stage, tracked files only (4.5, C-065) |
| A task edits `.claude/`, the backlog or `build/` | Unseen | Accept and the manifest refuse protected paths, as hold `stage-failed` (C-010) |
| A receipt is written by hand | The done gate cannot tell | `acceptMac` over task id and `outputSha256`, verified by the audit (C-012) |
| A plan invariant is "checked" by a model | A claim in prose | A rung fact record for a `command` invariant, an acceptance verdict for a `judgement` one (C-008) |

**The done gate.** `tim backlog status <p> <id> done --done-by build` runs `stage audit`. When the audit fails, the command exits 5 with the problems, and the record stage holds the increment `record-failed` with that list.

### 7.10 Discovered work, through one channel

The judge returns `discovered[]`, and only the judge. The schema:

```jsonc
{ "title": "...", "statement": "... MUST ...", "why": "...",
  "acceptance": [{ "text": "...", "witness": "e2e" }],
  "surface": { "service": "...", "area": "...", "repos": ["..."] },
  "kind": "capability", "source": { "finding": "f-12", "file": "...", "line": 40 },
  "needsPerson": false, "question": null }
```

- The judge's accept calls `tim backlog discover` for each item. The atom is written `proposed` (C-059).
- A separate `REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER` task (5.9, think) runs in span J3 as stage 26d, `when: discovered > 0`, started once the judge is accepted, so it costs no step agent (C-077). The recipe and executor-vocabulary lint (3.14) runs at its accept. `tim backlog record` (stage 26) then adopts only an atom that passed both. The judge's say-so is not verification: discovered atoms are verified like any other.
- `source.file` and `source.line` go to `build/state.json` and the journal, never into the atom's sources, so the atom carries no code coordinates that would act as a recipe.
- An adopted discovered atom gets a solo increment at the end of `increments[]`, which the next combine may regroup because it has not started.
- An item with `needsPerson` also becomes a question. The question lint derives its class, so a security question is must-answer.
- Discovered atoms pass the same checks as any other, including P11: the judge writes them as vertical slices.
- Nothing is deferred into prose notes. This replaces the three channels the synthesis found (C §2.6).

### 7.11 Stops and the handover

| Stop reason | Meaning | What the skill does |
|---|---|---|
| `all-done` | Every increment is done, or ruled out | Report, then the handover |
| `none-buildable` | Nothing passes the buildability rule | Print `next`'s reasons: questions, holds with release commands, unverified atoms |
| `checkpoint` | A checkpoint increment landed | List the walkthrough in section 1 and wait for Sam |
| `count-reached` | `count` increments were built | The handover |
| `awaiting-approval`, `awaiting-merge`, `changes-requested` | A PR needs a person | Print each PR link and what it needs |
| `red` | The current increment is held and `systemicHaltAfter` has not been reached, but nothing else is buildable | Print the hold and its release command. Never relaunch over a red |
| `systemic` | `systemicHaltAfter` increments in a row were held for the same reason | Stop. This is a pipeline fault to investigate before any relaunch |
| `agent-ceiling` | The run stopped cleanly below the runtime cap | Relaunch at once with a new run label; no question |
| `platform-blocked` | Everything left waits on an owed platform fix | Name each fix (`feedback_platform_blocked_increment_park_and_move_on`) |
| `claude-unavailable` | Two or more Claude tasks in one stage died with no receipt while Codex tasks in the same run succeeded: Claude's limit is likely spent (C-003) | Print the standing Codex handover's path, `build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md`, and the `tim backlog run bind <p> --from next --preset codex --said "<his words>" --by sam --at <ISO>` command. It does not count toward `systemicHaltAfter` |
| `codex-stalled` | Two `tim backlog codex run` calls in a row made no progress in `runner.json` (C-013) | Print the stage folder and the runner's last notes. Suggest `tim backlog codex run --check` |
| `environment` | Preflight in `run open`, before any increment started, found the run cannot operate under the rails: the pinned tim would prompt for permission, or Codex is not logged in (7.3, 7.12, C-064) | Print what is missing. When it is a permission, list it in "Owed to you". Never relaunch until preflight passes. It does not count toward `systemicHaltAfter`. Distinct from the hold `environment`, which holds one increment mid-build |

**The handover** is generated by `tim backlog handover <p> --executor claude | codex`. It holds:

- the programme, workarea and state branch;
- the last run label, the run id for `resumeFromRunId`, and the exact args object;
- the stop reason, and the last increment landed with its PRs;
- each hold with its release command;
- the must-answer questions still open, with what each holds;
- the merge plan from `tim backlog report --merge-plan`: the PRs in merge order, with the rule "all green, then merge in this order, no partial merge" (C-026);
- progress, N of TOTAL (P%).

**The standing Codex handover** (C-003). The record stage and every hold write `build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md` with `tim backlog handover <p> --executor codex --md --out <workarea>/build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md`, and the state commit includes it. So when Claude runs out mid-run, Sam can start a Codex session from the file with no Claude session at all.

It keeps the orchestrator's "Push first" rule (`build-orchestrator/SKILL.md:337`) and names "backlog path, the `args` object verbatim, and the run id for `resumeFromRunId`" (gap-4).

### 7.12 The tool pin

A programme that changes the pipeline's own tools cannot let a running build call half-edited versions of them: "never edit a script running subagents call" (H). Those tools are `tim/`, `tools/backlog/`, `tools/codex/`, `.claude/workflows/`, the stage briefs and the executor profiles. This programme is one.

With `toolPin: launch-commit` in `run.json`:

1. At launch, `tim backlog run open --pin` calls `tim backlog pin --commit <HEAD of the state branch>` (C-079). That clones the workspace at that commit into `workareas/clones/pipeline-pin/` and installs tim's dependencies through the canonical real path (`pwd -P`), the rule `npm-in-repo.sh --clone` follows, so the lockfile trap cannot corrupt it. An intact pin at the same commit is reused, not cloned or installed again.
2. `run open` puts the printed prefix in `args.pin`.
3. What is pinned (C-064):
   - **tim**, called in the allowlisted `npm --prefix` form: `npm --prefix ~/git/defra/trade-imports-workspace/workareas/clones/pipeline-pin/tim run --silent tim -- --workspace ~/git/defra/trade-imports-workspace backlog <verb>`. `--silent` keeps npm's own lines out of stdout, so `--json` output parses. `--workspace` is required, because a tim run from another checkout walks up to the wrong workspace (CLAUDE.md; `tim/src/cli.js:54-66`).
   - **The stage briefs, executor profiles and workflow scripts**, as files: `prompt.md` and the Workflow `scriptPath` point inside the pin.
   - **The two bash hook adapters** that stay (`tools/codex/guard.sh`, `tools/backlog/record-read.sh`), under `workareas/clones/pipeline-pin/tools/`, reached by the pinned Codex home and hooks, never by an agent's Bash call.
4. Every command a step agent runs is the pinned tim in the allowed `npm --prefix` form, or a native `git -C ~/…` push; both are allowlisted (`settings.json:83, 85`), so the pin needs no allow rule of its own. Stage 0 preflight checks that the pinned tim answers `--version --json` headless and stops with `environment` if it would prompt (7.3).

Pinning runs the tools as they stood at launch. The alternative, committing before each call, would run half-built tools, and the root copy is denied while it differs from HEAD.

The Workflow runtime reads the script once, at launch. Each relaunch re-pins, so every run uses the tools as they stood when it started, and an increment's own change is used from the next run on.

---

## 8. Executors

### 8.1 One stage contract

Every model stage, build or distil, is a set of tasks in folders. Both executors read the same inputs, follow the same brief and personas, and are judged by the same schema and the same accept command.

```
build/runs/<runLabel>/<inc>/a<attempt>/facts/<step>.json   (distil: distil/runs/<runLabel>/<phase>/facts/<step>.json)
                           one fact record per mechanical sub-step, written by tim only (C-082):
                           {step, argv, cwd, exit, log, logSha, startedAt, endedAt, headsBefore,
                           headsAfter, observed, factMac}; `observed` holds the remote ref, PR, check,
                           merge or ticket read back after an outward call (C-076)
build/runs/<runLabel>/<inc>/a<attempt>/<stage>/        (distil: distil/runs/<runLabel>/<phase>/)
  stage.json               the task list: id, executor (as bound when prepared, C-003), tier or effort,
                           sandbox, schema; pinned baseline heads (C-068); tree fingerprints; for a span
                           (C-077), each task's `after[]`, `when` and the span's `then`
  schema.json              the stage's output schema, Codex-strict, exported from tim's zod source
  .run.lock/               Codex only: held by tim backlog codex run while it runs, {pid, startedAt} (C-014)
  <task>/
    input/brief.json       tim backlog show --brief: the increment, members, acceptance, sources, rulings;
                           code citations labelled "evidence of current behaviour, not a design to copy"
    input/standards.json   the skill's SKILL.md, personas, routed best-practice files, rules and their pointers,
                           CLAUDE.md chain, memory, knowledge: each by live path, with its blob sha for
                           audit only (C-037, 8.5)
    input/upstream/        plan.md and the accepted outputs of earlier stages this task needs (C-022); in a
                           span, references by path to upstream tasks' outputs, resolved once accepted (C-077)
    input/slice.json       per-file review tasks only: the plan's seams and every changed file in every repo
    input/extras/          per-file diffs, evidence-check.json, ladder and proof logs, findings to verify,
                           accept-problems-<n>.json after a rejected draft (C-004)
    prompt.md              task header, executor profile block, what to read, where to write
    codex.json             Codex only: sandbox, network, effort, model, working directory, --add-dir list
    output.draft.json      the task's result
    rejected-<n>.json      a draft accept refused, kept for the record (C-004)
    output.json            written by stage accept once the draft is valid
    receipt.json           written by stage accept
    log.txt, events.jsonl, last-message.json, runner.json     Codex only
```

**Inputs per stage** (C-022). `stage prepare` gives each stage exactly the inputs its persona needs, from the `inputs[]` column of `advance`'s stage table, and the stage audit checks that nothing else was copied in:

| Stage | `brief.json` | `upstream/plan.md` | Other inputs |
|---|---|---|---|
| plan | yes | no | evidence check, ladder facts |
| plan-audit | yes | yes | none |
| implement | yes | yes | implement check logs on resume |
| review-style, review-code | yes | yes | the file's diff, `slice.json` |
| consistency | yes | yes | every touched repo's diffs |
| verify, verify-consistency | yes | yes | the findings to verify, the diffs they cite |
| judge | yes | yes | verified findings, file and consistency |
| fix | yes | yes | the rulings to apply |
| fix-verify | yes | yes | the rulings and the fix diffs |
| ladder-repair, e2e-repair, ci-fix | yes | yes | the red logs, the baseline record |
| acceptance | yes | **never** (C-021) | manifest diffs, ladder and proof logs, per-test results |

The personas read `ticket.md` for acceptance and intent; in a workflow task that is `brief.json` plus the plan's approach, decisions and out-of-scope list (8.5). The judge needs the out-of-scope list to tell a deliberate deferral from a fix-now.

**`prompt.md`** is written by `stage prepare` from one template for both executors:

```markdown
# Task review-code:src/server/app/origin/controller.js, increment inc-014, attempt 1

You are the CODE REVIEWER for one file. Your inputs are in <taskDir>/input/.

<!-- profile:start -->
(the executor profile: .claude/workflows/executors/claude.md or codex.md, inlined)
<!-- profile:end -->

Read and follow, in this order:
1. <workspace>/.claude/workflows/stages/review-file.md (the stage brief)
2. <workspace>/.claude/skills/review/SKILL.md (the skill's method, read live; its
   "When a workflow runs this skill" section says which of its steps the workflow replaces)
3. <workspace>/.claude/skills/review/references/FILE_REVIEWER.md (the persona, read live)
4. Every file listed under "must" in input/standards.json

Your result is one JSON object matching <stageDir>/schema.json.
```

`<workspace>` is always the live workspace, `~/git/defra/trade-imports-workspace`, never the tool pin (7.12): the skills are not pipeline tools and are never pinned or copied. Lines 2 and 3 come from the persona map in 8.5, so each role names its own skill's `SKILL.md` and persona.

**Prompt parity.** With the profile block removed, the two executors' prompts for the same task are byte-identical. `tim backlog compare` checks this for every task in the parity proof (codex; judges 1 and 3).

The stage brief for per-file review says: read `input/slice.json`, and read the counterpart of a seam file in the other repo before ruling on it (C-022).

**Output schemas, build stages.** Every schema is Codex-strict: every key required, optional values nullable, no extra keys (H; `stage schemas`). The zod source uses `.nullish()`, never `.optional()`, so a Codex draft with every nullable field `null` and a Claude draft that omits it are both accepted; a contract test per schema proves it both ways. A `.refine` is allowed only when the same rule is stated in the neutral stage brief, so neither executor meets a rule it was never told (C-016).

| Stage | Output |
|---|---|
| plan | 7.5 |
| plan-audit | `{verdict: approve \| revise, objections[{kind, criterion, detail}]}` |
| implement | `{ok, summary, changedFiles[{repoKey, path}], notes, deferred[]}` |
| review-style, review-code | `{file, findings[{id, severity, line, rule, claim, failureScenario, fix, confidence}], rulesRead[], verdict, completed, notReached[]}`. Confidence is a field, not prose (R6 names "a confidence value folded into prose") |
| consistency | `{repos[{repoKey, verdict, missing[], unique[]}], seams[{seamId, verdict: agrees \| disagrees \| not-checked, providerRef, consumerRef}], findings[{id, severity, claim, failureScenario, fix, confidence, refs[]}], completed, notReached[]}`. `refs` are `repo:path:line` (C-023, C-024). It holds no invariant results (C-008) |
| verify, verify-consistency | `{verdicts[{findingId, verdict: confirmed \| refuted \| unsure, evidence}], completed, notReached[]}` |
| judge | `{rulings[{findingId, ruling: fix-now \| defer \| reject, why}], discovered[] (7.10), summary}`, over file and consistency findings |
| fix | `{applied[{findingId, files[{repoKey, path}]}], notApplied[{findingId, why}]}` |
| fix-verify | `{items[{findingId, confirmed, evidence}], completed, notReached[]}` |
| ladder-repair, ci-fix, e2e-repair | `{ok, diagnosis, changed[], flake, baselineRef}`. `baselineRef` must name a baseline record before anything is called pre-existing |
| acceptance | `{criteria[{member, criterionId, result: met \| not-met, where, evidence, test: {path, title} \| null}], invariants[{id, result: met \| not-met, evidence}], completed, notReached[]}`. `test` is required for every `e2e` or `contract` criterion (C-021); `invariants` covers the plan's `judgement` invariants (C-008) |

**Finishing is reported, never assumed** (C-006). The six checking outputs (review-style, review-code, consistency, verify, fix-verify and acceptance) carry `completed: boolean` and `notReached: string[]`. A `completed: false` output gets a partial receipt, then one continuation: a resume for Codex, or one follow-on task for Claude naming `notReached`. A second partial output holds `review-failed`. The stage audit requires `completed: true` on all six.

**Output schemas, distil phases** (fields that matter): characterise `{units[], notRead[], pointers[]}`; extract-verify `{verdicts[{unit, verdict, correction, searchedFor}]}`; carryover `{verdicts[{item, verdict, unit}]}`; slice `{slices[{id, behaviour, units[], brief, crossCutting}]}`, where `behaviour` is one sentence naming the observer and the outcome, never a repo or a layer (C-028); author `{atoms[], notRaised[], premisesDisproved[], unitsCovered[]}`; verify `{verdicts[{atom, verdict, correction, question}]}`; gap-audit `{reason, atoms[]}`; reconcile `{conflicts[], precedencePicks[], assumptions[], invariants[], questions[], variants[]}`; panel judge and chair `{docket, rulings[{conflict, option, why, escalate}]}`; panel critic `{contradictions[], breaches[]}`; dedupe `{pairs[{a, b, verdict, survivor, lookedAt}]}`; critic `{gaps[], blindSpots[], counterNumbers[]}`; question-editor `{questions[]}`; question-critic `{results[{question, pass, failures[]}]}`; combiner `{groups[], declined[]}`; combine-verifier `{results[{group, pass, failures[]}]}`; prose-editor `{rewrites[{id, slot, text}]}`; prose-claim-verifier `{results[{id, slot, pass, why}]}`; ruling-applier `{effects[], variants[]}`.

**Receipt** (`receipt.json`, written only by `stage accept`):

```jsonc
{ "schemaVersion": 1, "accepted": true, "taskId": "review-code:src/server/app/origin/controller.js",
  "stage": "review", "role": "review-code", "executor": "codex", "tier": "medium", "sandbox": "read-only",
  "codexVersion": "<codex --version>", "model": "<from config.toml>", "effort": "medium", "configHash": "…",
  "standardsShas": { "…": "…" }, "standardsChanged": [], "completed": true, "readingClean": true,
  "outputSha256": "…", "acceptMac": "…", "attemptsToAccept": 1, "problems": [], "acceptedAt": "2026-09-18T15:42:10Z",
  "counts": { "findings": 3, "standing": null, "fixNow": null, "added": null, "discovered": null, "newAtoms": null },
  "upstreamShas": { "review-style:src/server/app/origin/controller.js": "…" } }
```

- `counts` holds the six counts a downstream `when` reads (4.5 accept step 7), derived by tim from `output.json`, never from what an agent relays; a count the stage does not produce is `null`. The span walker starts or skips a task on these counts, and the audit re-derives every skip from them (C-077, C-081).
- `upstreamShas` records the `outputSha256` of every upstream task this task read. The audit refuses a task whose upstream shas no longer match its upstreams' accepted outputs, so a fix-verify made stale by a later fix round is run again (C-077).
- `acceptedAt` is stamped by tim, because it records what happened on this machine (4.3).
- `acceptMac` is an HMAC over the task id and `outputSha256`, keyed by the gitignored `build/.accept-key` that `run start` creates (inside `run open`); the same key signs fact records' `factMac` (C-082). The stage audit verifies it and re-hashes `output.json`, so a hand-written receipt fails the done gate (C-012).
- `codexVersion`, `model`, `effort` and `configHash` (a hash of the pipeline's Codex `config.toml` plus any `-c` overrides) are recorded for Codex tasks, `model` and `effort` for Claude tasks. `runner.json` records the same. `compare` fails a measure whose tasks recorded different Codex versions, models, efforts or config hashes, so a configuration change mid-proof cannot pass unnoticed (C-009).
- `attemptsToAccept` is recorded for both executors (C-004).
- `standardsShas` records the blob sha of every must file at prepare time, and `standardsChanged` the ones that changed before accept. Both are audit only (8.5).

### 8.2 Two adapters

**Claude.** One `agent()` per task, spawned by the one helper both workflow scripts carry:

```js
agent(`${GUARD_RAILS}
Read ${task.dirAbs}/prompt.md and follow it in full.
Write your result to ${task.dirAbs}/output.draft.json with the Write tool, then run:
${TIM} backlog stage accept ${P} ${task.dirTilde} --json
If it prints problems, correct the draft and run it again, at most three times.
If it exits 4, run the same command again unchanged: another accept holds the lock.
Never write output.json or receipt.json yourself.
Return the fields of the receipt it printed.`,
  { model, effort, label: task.label, phase: stage.phase, schema: RECEIPT_SCHEMA })
```

- **`${TIM}`** is `tim`, or the pinned `npm --prefix … run --silent tim -- --workspace …` prefix under `toolPin: launch-commit` (7.12, C-064).
- **The first instruction ties the session to the task** (C-011). Reading `prompt.md` first means the committed `PostToolUse` Read hook, `tools/backlog/record-read.sh`, records `{session_id, transcript_path, file_path, offset, limit}` to `build/reads/<session_id>.jsonl`, and the first entry names the task. If inc-019's fixture shows `session_id` is shared across workflow agents, the reading audit falls back to mapping transcripts at record time from the transcript directory the Workflow result names, with hold `record-failed` when that fails.
- **Three correction rounds** (C-004). Accept returns semantic problems, including must-files not read in full (C-011), and the agent corrects its draft. Codex gets the same three rounds through `tim backlog codex run`.
- **Exit 4 means "rerun unchanged"** (C-005). A per-task accept writes only `output.json` and `receipt.json` in its own task folder, so concurrent accepts rarely contend; a lock is never a reason to change the draft.
- The runtime caps concurrency at 16. An in-script semaphore caps Opus tasks at `opusConcurrency`.
- The script trusts only the small receipt. The done gate re-reads every receipt from disk (4.5).
- No agent relays a large JSON array.
- **The relayed span is checked before it is walked** (C-070). `advance`'s `run` step, relayed by the step agent, prints a compact task list with each task's `after` and `when`, the count and the sha256 of `stage.json`. The script re-derives both from the list it received before it spawns anything. A mismatch re-calls `advance` once, then holds `stage-failed`. A dropped row therefore stops the span at once rather than surfacing later as a missing receipt.
- **The span walker** (C-077) starts a task when every `after` task has an accepted receipt and its `when` holds on their receipts' `counts`. It is one helper, duplicated byte-identically in both workflow scripts and run on fixture graphs by the contract test (4.6 item 6). It decides nothing `advance` does not re-derive: a wrongly skipped task is prepared and run on the next `advance`.
- **After the span**, the next `advance` runs `stage accept --stage <stageDir> --finalise` in-process, once per stage in the span, for every shared side effect (C-005).

**Codex.** Codex tasks run inside tim (C-078). `advance` calls `tim backlog codex run <p> <stageDir>` in-process for every task bound to Codex, so a span whose tasks are all Codex-bound costs no agent of its own: the step agent's `advance` call runs it, sliced with exit 75, and the step agent calls again. A span with both Claude and Codex tasks adds one Haiku Codex runner beside the Claude tasks, spawned by the span walker:

```js
agent(`${GUARD_RAILS}
```
