```js
Run ${TIM} backlog codex run ${P} ${stage.dirTilde} --json as a foreground Bash call with timeout 600000.
Exit 75 means unfinished: run the same command again. Keep going while each call reports progress.
Stop after two calls in a row that report no progress, and return "codex-stalled".
Exit 0 or 1: return the runner's summary of accepted and failed tasks.
Exit 3: another runner holds this stage. Return "already-running".
Exit 2: stop, and return the runner's output as the problem.`,
  { model: 'haiku', effort: 'low', label: `${stage.id} codex`, phase: stage.phase, schema: STAGE_RECEIPTS_SCHEMA })
```

The runner does not run accept itself: `tim backlog codex run` accepts each task as its draft arrives (C-004). A fixed call limit could not cover a large stage (61 review tasks, say), so the loop grows with the work and ends only on done, failure or two calls with no progress (C-013). The span walker treats the runner's return as the end of the Codex tasks; their downstream tasks start from the receipts on disk.

**`tim backlog codex run <p> <stageDir>`** (`tim/src/backlog/codex/runner.js`), called in-process by `advance`, in full. It is `run-stage.sh`'s contract in tested JavaScript; the bash script is never written (C-079):

- **Input.** `stage.json`. It runs only tasks whose executor is `codex` and which have no accepted receipt.
- **Environment** (C-009). It sets `CODEX_HOME` to the committed `tools/codex/home/`, whose `config.toml` names the model explicitly and carries no MCP servers, plugins, notify, memories or skills, and whose `hooks.json` runs `tools/codex/guard.sh` (C-010). It symlinks the user's `auth.json` into that home at run time. Nothing is read from `~/.codex`, so hidden context cannot skew parity.
- **One runner per stage folder** (C-014). It takes `mkdir <stageDir>/.run.lock` holding `{pid, startedAt}`. A live lock exits 3, "already running". A stale lock (its pid is dead) is broken, with a note in `runner.json`. Each task's child pid is recorded, and a task whose pid is alive is never restarted.
- **Concurrency.** At most `codexParallel.readOnly` read-only and `codexParallel.write` writing Codex processes at once (7.7). So "Codex fans out one `codex exec` per file too, run concurrently" (R6).
- **Start** (flags taken from `codex.json`):

  ```
  CODEX_HOME=<pin>/tools/codex/home codex exec --json -C <cwd> [--add-dir <repo>]... --skip-git-repo-check \
    -s <sandbox> [-c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true] -c model_reasoning_effort=<effort> \
    [-m <model>] --output-schema <stageDir>/schema.json -o <taskDir>/last-message.json \
    "Read <taskDir>/prompt.md and follow it in full." > <taskDir>/events.jsonl 2>> <taskDir>/log.txt
  ```

- **Session id** (C-011). `--json` writes the event stream to `events.jsonl`. The session id comes from its `thread.started` event, never from grepping a log. The same stream is what the reading audit reads for Codex's commands and their output.
- **Resume.** `codex exec <same flags> resume <session id> "<message>"`. Every flag comes before `resume`, which `codex exec resume -C` rejects (`increment-build-loop.js:768`). The message is "Continue where you left off and finish the task. Write your final result." after a slice ends.
- **Accept after every draft** (C-004). When a child exits 0 with a `last-message.json` that parses as JSON, the runner copies it to `output.draft.json` and runs `tim backlog stage accept <taskDir> --json` (the pinned prefix, 7.12).
  - `accepted`: the task is done.
  - `needs-correction`: accept has moved the draft to `rejected-<n>.json`, written `input/extras/accept-problems-<n>.json` and marked the task in `runner.json`. The runner resumes the task's own session with "tim rejected your output: <problems verbatim>. Correct it and write your final result." At most three corrections, as for Claude; `attemptsToAccept` is recorded for both.
  - Exit 4 (lock): the runner runs the same accept again unchanged (C-005).
  - A task with no output at all gets one fresh start.
- **Slices.**
  - The call ends itself 540 seconds after it starts, below the 600-second ceiling on a Bash call.
  - No new task starts in the last 60 seconds of a call (C-013).
  - At the deadline it stops the whole descendant tree of every child it started, walking every descendant from `ps -A -o pid=,ppid=` (parsed through `exec.js`, no new dependency; a process-group kill would miss a grandchild in its own group), because grandchildren in their own process group would otherwise keep ports and `target/` locks (C-015). It records each unfinished task's session id and slice count in `runner.json`, then exits 75.
  - Slices count by each task's own wall-clock time, so a task that started late in a call does not lose a slice it never used (C-013). A task with no result after 5 slices of its own has failed. Each failed task gets one fresh start before it counts as failed.
  - `runner.json` records progress per call (tasks started, drafts written, tasks accepted), which is what `advance` and the Codex runner read.
  - This moves the kill-and-resume logic out of prompt prose, including the `pkill ... ; grep` compound that breaks the rails (`increment-build-loop.js:762`), into tested tim code.
- **Result.** Anything other than an accepted draft after the rounds above marks the task failed, with the tail of its log.
- **A partial checking output** (C-006). A `completed: false` output is accepted with a partial receipt, and the runner resumes the session once, naming `notReached`.
- **Exit codes:**

  | Code | Meaning |
  |---|---|
  | 0 | Every Codex task has an accepted receipt |
  | 75 | Some are unfinished: call again |
  | 1 | Some failed; stdout lists them as JSON |
  | 2 | Bad input |
  | 3 | Another runner holds this stage's lock |

- **Limits.** It runs no git, writes nothing outside the stage folder, and never runs in the background.
- **`--check`** prints whether Codex is installed and logged in, prints the effective configuration from the pipeline's Codex home, and runs one real no-op resume (C-009). In a Codex-orchestrated session it also verifies the session has full access (8.11, C-002).
- **Tests.** Vitest runs the runner against `tim/src/test-support/fake-codex.js`, selected by `CODEX_BIN`. They cover:
  - a task that finishes in one slice, a slice-and-resume, a task that fails after five slices, and a bad JSON message;
  - a draft valid under the schema but failing a semantic check, then a corrected resume that is accepted (C-004);
  - a live lock and a stale lock (C-014);
  - a fake child that spawns a grandchild in a new process group, which must be gone after the deadline (C-015);
  - every exit code.

  `--check` also checks, against the real binary, that the installed Codex's `exec --help` and `exec resume --help` list every flag the runner uses, `--add-dir` and `--json` included, so a Codex upgrade that renames one fails loudly (C-009, C-010).

**Where each Codex task may write:**

| Mode | Codex flags | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| read-only | `-s read-only`, working directory the workspace | plan, plan-audit, review-style, review-code, consistency, verify, verify-consistency, judge, fix-verify, acceptance; distil extract-verify, reslice-verify, verify, critic, question-critic, combine-verifier, prose-claim-verifier, panel, dedupe, cold-reader |
| task-write | `-s workspace-write` with the task folder as the working directory, so writes stay inside it | characterise, author, reslice, gap-audit, carryover, slice, reconcile, combiner, question-editor, prose-editor, ruling-applier |
| repo-write | `-s workspace-write`, `-C <first planned repo>` and `--add-dir <repo>` for every other planned repo; the workspace root only when the workspace repo is itself in the plan; network on for implement and ci-fix | implement, fix, ladder-repair, ci-fix, e2e-repair |

Every role the stage table marks `bindable` has exactly one row here; the stage table's `sandbox` field (4.5) carries it, and `advance` refuses to bind a role to Codex that has none. Every model role, build or distil, is bindable (5.11), so every model role appears once. Dedupe and the cold reader return only verdicts through `-o`, so they run read-only; reslice authors atom files, so it writes inside its task folder like author, and the task that verifies each resliced atom is read-only (5.2 phase 5a). Every sandbox mode can read the live workspace, so a Codex task reads the skills' `SKILL.md`, personas, routing and best-practice files by path in every mode (8.5).

**Writes are fenced** (C-010). A writing Codex task can reach only the planned repos, never the whole workspace, so it cannot edit `.claude/settings*.json` or hooks and weaken every later Claude agent. Behind that fence, for both executors, accept and the manifest refuse any change under `backlog*.json`, `decisions.json`, `build/**` outside the task folder, `.claude/**`, `docker/stack/.staged/**` or any `.git/hooks/**`, as hold `stage-failed` (4.5).

Every Codex result arrives through `-o` and `--output-schema`, which the Codex CLI writes itself, so a read-only task can still return its output. For large distil outputs, a task-write task may put files in its own folder, and `stage accept --finalise` moves them into place (4.5).

### 8.3 Executor profiles

The profiles are the only executor-specific text. The stage briefs are neutral. Both profiles live in `.claude/workflows/executors/`, owned by the implementor (R6).

| `claude.md` says | `codex.md` says |
|---|---|
| The harness loads a matching `.claude/rules` file when you Read a file. Read the file you will change before you change it, and follow each rule's pointers to the best-practice files it names (R3; rules probe) | Nothing loads by itself: the pipeline's Codex home carries no rules, memory or skills (C-009). `input/standards.json` lists every rule that matches the files in play, the best-practice files each points to, the CLAUDE.md chain with its imports, and the memory index. Read every "must" file before you start (R3, R6) |
| Before you create a file, Read an existing file of the same kind (rules probe: "An agent that creates a new file of a type it" has not read gets no rules) | Before you create a file, read an existing file of the same kind |
| The "must" list in `input/standards.json` is required reading even where the harness has injected some of it. Read each "must" file in full: a partial read does not count (C-011) | Read each "must" file in full, at most once, then write findings instead of re-reading (`codex/review.md:40-45`). A partial read does not count (C-011) |
| Write `output.draft.json` with the Write tool, then run `stage accept`. Never write `output.json` or `receipt.json` yourself (C-012) | Your final message is your output: one JSON object, every key present, `null` for anything that does not apply (H: the `--output-schema` rule). If tim rejects it, you will be told why: correct it and write your final result again (C-004) |
| Always report, even if you did not finish, and list what you did not reach in `notReached` (C-006) | Always report, even if you did not finish, and list what you did not reach in `notReached` (C-006) |
| Browser suites and Docker are run by the pipeline's own commands, not by you. Do not run them; the implement check runs them after you and hands you any failure (C-017) | Browser suites and Docker are run by the pipeline's own commands, not by you (`codex/implement.md:123-132`). Do not attempt a sandbox bypass; the implement check runs them after you and hands you any failure (C-017) |
| The GUARD RAILS in your spawn prompt apply to every Bash call | Your shell is normal. Ignore any Claude GUARD RAILS text you meet in a persona (`codex/review.md:8-12`) |
| Never commit, push, switch branch or stash: the workflow owns git | Never commit, push, switch branch or stash: the workflow owns git, and the manifest proves it |
| The per-call guards (guard-bash, guard-edits, the secrets-read check) apply to you | Never: read secrets; write credentials; edit the backlog, `decisions.json`, `build/`, `.claude/settings*` or hooks, or `docker/stack/.staged`; run `sonar`; install anything globally; touch a CDP platform repo (C-010). Accept and the manifest refuse the protected paths whatever you do |

**How the profiles were written** (C-007). The stage briefs in `.claude/workflows/stages/*.md` are written fresh from this contract; none is a moved Codex brief. Before inc-014, every sentence of `codex/implement.md`, `fix.md` and `review.md` is sorted into one of three places: neutral (a stage brief), Codex-only (`executors/codex.md`) or Claude-only (`executors/claude.md`). The sort table is recorded in the inc-014 plan. A rule that serves quality on one side, such as "browser suites are not yours", is given to both, so parity never comes from making Claude worse or from Codex losing a rule. The three `codex/*.md` briefs stay for the legacy loop and are deleted with it in inc-040 (C-072). A contract test fails a neutral brief that mentions sandbox, Codex, Claude, GUARD RAILS or the Write tool (4.6).

### 8.4 Presets, tiers and sandboxes

**Tiers.** Claude "think" is Opus at high effort, "doer" is Sonnet, and "step" is Haiku at low effort (`FA:95-97`), used only by step agents and Codex runners, which make no decision (C-074). Codex "high" and "medium" set `model_reasoning_effort`. The Codex model is the one named in the pipeline's `tools/codex/home/config.toml`, or `codex.model` in `run.json` when `run set codex.model <name>` names one (C-009). It is never the user's own `~/.codex` default, and every receipt records it.

**Presets** (`.claude/workflows/executors/presets.json`):

| Role | `claude` | `codex` | `codex-plan-on-claude` | `codex-review-only` | `codex-claude-checks` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| plan | think | Codex high | think | think | Codex high |
| plan-audit | think | Codex high | think | think | think |
| implement | doer | Codex medium | Codex medium | doer | Codex medium |
| review-style, review-code | doer | Codex medium | Codex medium | Codex medium | Codex medium |
| consistency | think | Codex high | Codex high | Codex high | Codex high |
| verify, verify-consistency | doer | Codex medium | Codex medium | Codex medium | Codex medium |
| judge | think | Codex high | Codex high | think | think |
| fix | doer | Codex medium | Codex medium | doer | Codex medium |
| fix-verify | doer | Codex medium | Codex medium | Codex medium | doer |
| ladder-repair, ci-fix, e2e-repair | doer | Codex medium | Codex medium | doer | Codex medium |
| acceptance | think | Codex high | Codex high | think | think |

- **Codex does the heavy work.** The `codex` preset puts every model role on Codex, including the judge and acceptance (judge 3, graft 7). Claude keeps only the step agents. The parity proof compares the judges' rulings (8.10).
- **`codex-claude-checks`** is the preset for anyone who wants Claude to keep the checks: plan-audit, judge, fix-verify and acceptance.
- **Distil presets.** `distil-claude` is the default. `distil-codex` binds characterise, extract-verify, carryover, reslice, author, verify and gap-audit to Codex. Every other distil model role is bindable by `--role` (5.11).
- **Where commands run.** Rungs, browser suites, CI waits and the local stack run inside tim, started by the step agent's Bash call, whatever the preset, including the implement check after every writing task (stage 11, C-017). Neither executor runs them itself, so the comparison is symmetric. The Codex sandbox does not reach Docker. Each push, PR, merge and Jira write is its own visible call (0.3, C-075, C-076).
- **No Sonnet or Opus under `codex`** (C-019). With the integration proof run inside tim and every model task run inside `advance`, the `codex` preset spawns no Sonnet or Opus agent at all: about 2 Haiku step agents per increment (one, plus a handback on a long increment).

### 8.5 Personas read live, the consistency reviewer, and the seam

**Everything from the two skills is read live, by path, at run time, for both executors** (R2). This is the rule, with no exception:

- The `review` and `code-style` skills' `SKILL.md`, their `references/*.md` personas, their language-to-bundle routing (`assets/routing.json`) and the best-practice files that routing names are read from the live workspace at `~/git/defra/trade-imports-workspace/`, by path, by the task itself, when it runs. That holds for a Claude task and a Codex task alike, in every sandbox mode (8.2).
- Nothing from them is copied, baked, summarised or inlined into a workflow script, a stage brief, an executor profile, `prompt.md`, `standards.json` or a Codex home. `standards.json` holds paths, not content.
- The skills are never part of the tool pin (7.12). The pin holds the pipeline's own tools; a skill edit reaches the next task prepared, even mid-run and mid-increment, and a skill file edited after a task is prepared reaches that task too, because it reads the file only when it runs.
- **Blob shas are for audit only.** `standards.json` and the receipt record each file's blob sha so a reader can see what a task read and the parity proof can show both executors read the same standards. A sha is never a pin: nothing compares a sha to hold a file back, `stage accept` never refuses a task because a standard changed (it notes the change in `standardsChanged[]`), and `compare` answers a changed standard by asking for a re-run under the current one (8.10), never by keeping the old one.

**What each role reads.** Every review, verify, judge and fix task's `prompt.md` names the owning skill's `SKILL.md` and its persona file inside the live skill, and `standards.json` lists both as "must" files, so the reading audit proves they were read in full (4.5, C-011):

| Task | `SKILL.md` | Persona |
|---|---|---|
| review-style | `code-style/SKILL.md` | `code-style/references/STYLE_FILE_REVIEWER.md` |
| review-code | `review/SKILL.md` | `review/references/FILE_REVIEWER.md` |
| consistency | `review/SKILL.md` | `review/references/CONSISTENCY_REVIEWER.md` |
| verify, verify-consistency | `review/SKILL.md` for code and consistency findings, `code-style/SKILL.md` for style findings | the persona that raised the finding, so the verifier checks it against the method that produced it |
| judge | both | none: the stage brief rules on fix-now, defer or reject; the two `SKILL.md` files give the severity scales and verdict guidelines it rules against |
| fix | `review/SKILL.md` for code findings, `code-style/SKILL.md` for style findings | `review/references/REVIEW_ITEM_FIXER.md` for code findings, `code-style/references/STYLE_IMPLEMENTOR.md` for style findings |
| fix-verify | as fix | as fix |

A `SKILL.md` carries method the personas rely on: what a review covers, the verdict guidelines, the worker list and each worker's granularity. Reading it live means a change to either skill's method, not only to a persona, reaches the pipeline on the next task (R2).

**When a skill changes shape, a contract test fails** (item 5 of `tim/src/backlog/workflow-contract.test.js`, run by the workspace ladder). It reads each `SKILL.md`'s "Worker references" table live and checks it against the stage table (4.5) and the persona map above:

- every persona the pipeline names still exists at its path, and every worker the skill lists is either mapped to a role or listed in the test's own record of workers the pipeline does not run (the walkers and `BATCH_IMPLEMENTOR`, which drive a person's review), with the reason;
- each mapped worker's granularity, as the table states it ("one per file", "one per repo", "one per Fix-disposition item"), matches the stage's `fanOut` or is recorded as a known reading, such as the review skill's "(one per repo)", which describes one consistency reviewer's output rather than one agent per repo (0.4);
- each `SKILL.md` still has its "When a workflow runs this skill" section.

A failure names what changed, so the stage table or persona map is updated to follow the skill. The test never blocks the skill's own change, and until it is followed every task still reads the new `SKILL.md` and personas as they are.

**The skills' own routing, as data each skill owns** (C-037). Routing moves out of bash into two data files, each owned by its skill and read live:

- `.claude/skills/code-style/assets/routing.json` holds the path-to-topic map (today `tools/style/file-topics.sh:34-58`) and the topic-to-files lists (today `tools/style/bake-rules-bundle.sh:37-73`).
- `.claude/skills/review/assets/routing.json` holds the technology detectors and best-practice lists (today `tools/review/detect-tech.sh:53-265`), as data: `fileContains`, `anyFileContains` and `fileExists`.
- The three bash scripts become thin jq readers of those files, with golden tests: old output equals new output on every fixture path, so a person running either skill sees no change.
- `tim backlog standards` (8.6) is the single resolver over both routing files, the rule globs and their pointers, the CLAUDE.md chain and the memory index. `stage prepare` calls it in-process for every task. It bakes nothing: `standards.json` lists each routed best-practice file by its live path, with the blob sha it had at prepare time for audit. Where the code-style skill's own `bake-rules-bundle.sh` builds a per-PR bundle for a person's review, the pipeline lists that bundle's constituent files instead, from the same `routing.json`, so both read the same sources.
- A writing task's standards are the union of its planned-file routing and repo-level routing for every repo in the surface, so a Codex writer creating a file nobody planned still has that file type's rules. The manifest's `rulesGap[]` lists any changed file whose rules were missing from its writer's list, and the stage audit fails on a non-empty gap for either executor.

Merit in use: three routing watcher stages per increment are gone, routing is tested in one place, and the recorded shas let the parity proof show both executors read the same standards. R2 holds, because each skill still owns its `SKILL.md`, personas and routing, and every change to any of them is read live by both executors.

**Granularity, the same for both executors** (R6):

- One style task and one code task per content-changed file. The only exclusions are mechanical: pure renames (`git diff -M100%`), lockfiles and the registry's `generated` globs for the repo, each listed in the report.
- Every per-file task also gets `input/slice.json`: the plan's seams and every changed file in every repo, so a reviewer can read the counterpart of a seam file (C-022).
- One consistency task per increment. It reads every touched repo's diff together and writes a verdict per repo, which is what the review skill itself does (`review/SKILL.md:147-162`; 0.4). It also checks the contract between repos (R7), with a verdict for every plan seam (C-023). Invariants are not its job: command invariants are rung facts, and judgement invariants are acceptance's (C-008).
- One verify task per file with findings, plus one verify-consistency task when the consistency output has findings (C-024).

**The contract between repos** (C-023). The live `CONSISTENCY_REVIEWER.md` checks peer patterns only. R2 forbids putting a contract method into a brief, so it goes into the persona itself, where a person running the review skill gains it too. inc-019 adds a method section, "Contract between repos", to `.claude/skills/review/references/CONSISTENCY_REVIEWER.md`:

- Enumerate every seam in the diffs: HTTP routes and methods with request and response fields, types, optionality, enums, status and error codes; messages and events; persisted documents another service reads; reference-data lookups; the tests repo's page objects, fixtures and API clients against the DOM and API.
- Check that provider and consumer agree on each.
- Cite both sides as `repo:path:line`.
- Report each disagreement as a finding in the review finding shape, so it goes through verify, judge, fix and fix-verify like any other (C-024). `REVIEW_ITEM_FIXER`'s files may span repos.

**The seam.** The personas were written for the skills' own workareas, and parts of them are harness: where inputs live, where the diff comes from, how findings are written, and when to commit. Each persona gains one section, owned by its skill, headed "When a workflow runs this persona". It names each harness part and what replaces it:

| Harness part | Where it is today | In a workflow task |
|---|---|---|
| Inputs | the review workarea (`CONSISTENCY_REVIEWER.md:12-21`, `STYLE_FILE_REVIEWER.md:49-89`) | the task folder's `input/` |
| `ticket.md` (acceptance and intent) | the review workarea | `input/brief.json` (acceptance, decisions, invariants) plus `input/upstream/plan.md` (approach, decisions, out of scope) (C-022) |
| Best-practice source | the per-PR bundle (`FILE_REVIEWER.md:71-81`) | the "must" files in `input/standards.json`, which replace the per-PR bundle (C-037) |
| The diff | `.diffs/` and PR diffs (`CONSISTENCY_REVIEWER.md:31-44`; `FILE_REVIEWER.md:127-146`) | `input/extras/<file>.diff` from the manifest |
| Writing findings | `file-style-add-item.sh`, the verdict scripts, `_consistency-check.md` (`STYLE_FILE_REVIEWER.md:155-188`; `FILE_REVIEWER.md:8-38, 210-224`; `CONSISTENCY_REVIEWER.md:64-125`) | `output.draft.json` in the stage schema |
| Commit and mark outcome | `REVIEW_ITEM_FIXER.md:115-139`; `STYLE_IMPLEMENTOR.md:181-230` | skipped: the workflow lands |
| Refresh mode | both reviewers | not used: every build review is fresh |

The method sections are read unchanged: what to look for, severity, filtering and file-type guidance. That is the part R2 requires to be live. The seam changes nothing when a person runs the skills. It is written in inc-019, where the review stage first runs the personas (req-044).

Each `SKILL.md` gains the same kind of section, owned by its skill, headed "When a workflow runs this skill". It names the steps that are harness (Step 0's workarea set-up, the scripts cheat-sheet, the repository summaries and index, posting to the PR, refresh mode) and says the task's `input/` folder, stage schema and the workflow replace them, and that everything else, the method, the verdict guidelines and the worker granularity, applies as written. It is written in inc-019 with the persona seams, and the contract test above checks it is still there.

### 8.6 Rules, memory and house rules

`tim backlog standards --files <repoKey:path>... [--repo-level <repoKey>...] [--lint]` (renamed from `rules`, C-037) returns one entry per file, and one per repo for `--repo-level`:

```jsonc
{ "file": "plantsFrontend:src/server/app/origin/controller.js",
  "rules": [ { "path": ".claude/rules/node.md", "matchedBy": "<the paths: glob>", "sha": "<blob sha>",
               "pointsTo": ["docs/best-practices/node/code-style.md", "docs/best-practices/node/hapi.md"] } ],
  "topics": ["node"],
  "bestPractice": ["docs/best-practices/node/code-style.md", "<every file code-style/assets/routing.json names for the topic>"],
  "skills": [".claude/skills/review/SKILL.md", ".claude/skills/code-style/SKILL.md", "<the role's persona, 8.5>"],
  "claudeMd": ["CLAUDE.md", "repos/trade-imports-plants-frontend/CLAUDE.md"],
  "imports": ["<each @ import, resolved>"],
  "memory": ["<the auto-memory index path for this workspace>"],
  "houseRules": ["<per S2: memory files by path, or docs/best-practices/house-rules.md>"],
  "knowledge": ["<the registry entry's knowledge files>"],
  "shas": { "<every source path>": "<blob sha>" } }
```

Every entry is a live workspace path; `shas` records each file's blob sha at prepare time, for audit only (8.5). Nothing is baked into a copy. The review skill's routing (`review/assets/routing.json`) supplies the repo's technologies and best-practice files; the code-style skill's (`code-style/assets/routing.json`) supplies each file's topics and the files per topic (8.5). `knowledge` comes from the programme's registry entry (C-058); for this programme it is DESIGN.md.

**How it resolves:**

- Topics and best-practice files come from the two skills' `routing.json` files, read live at every call.
- The skills' `SKILL.md` and persona files come from the persona map in 8.5, by the task's role, as live paths.
- Each `.claude/rules/*.md` file's `paths:` front matter is matched with picomatch against workspace-relative paths. That includes nested rules folders such as `tim/.claude/rules/`, whose globs are relative to `tim/` (0.4).
- Each rule's pointers are expanded, including "Topic dir" with "Key files" and brace lists (rules probe). This is done "at run time, not from a hardcoded table, so a new rule is picked up automatically".
- The CLAUDE.md chain runs from the workspace root down to each file's folder, with its `@` imports ("plus `tim/CLAUDE.md` with its `@` imports", rules probe).
- The memory index comes from the user's project folder. It is a "must" file for every writing and reviewing task, for both executors (C-011).
- `--lint` exits 1 on a pointer to a missing file.

**Who reads what.**

- Claude agents get rules on Read and the CLAUDE.md chain and memory index at start. Their profile tells them to follow pointers and read a sibling first (R3). `standards.json` still lists everything, so the reading audit can check it.
- Codex gets nothing by itself, because the pipeline's Codex home carries no rules, memory or skills (C-009), so it reads the list.
- For both, a "must" file counts as read only when reads cover its full line range, singly or cumulatively. Accept returns any missing must-file as a problem inside the three correction rounds (4.5, C-011).

**House rules** (S2). The loop inlines four house rules for Claude style reviewers only (`increment-build-loop.js:1195-1197`), and Codex never sees them. Judge 3 called this "the most important thing any candidate found". Each rule is one of Sam's memory files: remove explanatory comments, no migration comments, named helpers for pipelines, and explicit names.

- **Default (S2 option A).** `standards.json` names those four memory files by path for every style review, implement and fix task, for both executors (inc-014). Nothing is copied into a script.
- **Option B.** Promote them to a tracked `docs/best-practices/house-rules.md` that the code-style bundles include, so they reach people and CI too (inc-016, deferred under the default in force, d-005, until Sam rules B or C; either ruling supersedes d-005, which reverts the deferral first, so inc-016 returns to `todo` with no further step, 3.7).

### 8.7 What the session hooks did, now explicit steps (R4)

| Hook | What it does in the main session | In workflow agents | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `UserPromptSubmit` secrets scan | Blocks prompts carrying secrets | does not fire | Credential lint at ingest (distil); `tim backlog secrets` (build stage 19) |
| `PreToolUse` guard-bash, guard-edits, the SonarCloud Read check | Deny unsafe commands, edits and secret reads | fires for Claude agents. For Codex, the pipeline's `tools/codex/home/hooks.json` runs `tools/codex/guard.sh` on PreToolUse, which adapts Codex's hook input to `guard-bash.sh`, `guard-edits.sh` and the secrets-read check (C-010). Whether Codex honours a deny in `exec`, and whether it trusts a hook from `CODEX_HOME`, is probed in inc-015 | GUARD RAILS keep Claude agents clear of the guards. Codex is fenced by writable roots limited to the planned repos (8.2), the profile's prohibitions (8.3) and accept's and the manifest's refusal of protected paths, plus tree fingerprints. If the inc-015 probe fails, this row will say plainly: "Codex has no per-call guard; it is fenced by writable roots, the profile's prohibitions and accept's refusals". Credential redaction at characterise keeps the backlog readable past the Read check (H §2.3). Local git writes (branch cuts, merges of base, commits, wip commits, stashes, the state commit) now run inside tim calls, tested on fixtures, so guard-bash sees the tim call rather than each git command; every push, PR, merge and Jira write is still its own call guard-bash sees. These calls are allowlisted (`settings.json:83, 88`), so guard-bash, tim's refusals before it generates a call, and observation afterwards are the gates, not the allowlist (C-075, C-076) |
| `PostToolUse` on push (`sonar-record-push.sh`) | Records pending SonarCloud checks | does not fire for the loop's pushes: its `if` is `Bash(git push*)` (`.claude/settings.json:139`), and the loop pushes with `git -C <repo> push …` (C-042) | Stage 24 reads PR checks instead, so no SonarCloud result is lost; silence is recorded as "no report". `sonar-check-pending.sh` is not used: the records it reads come from this hook, which never fires for the build's pushes (C-076) |
| `PostToolUse` on Read (`tools/backlog/record-read.sh`, new) | Nothing today | fires for Claude agents once wired into `.claude/settings.json`, an edit guard-edits forbids agents, so it is owed to Sam (9.2) | Records each Read's `{session_id, transcript_path, file_path, offset, limit}` for the reading audit (C-011). Codex reads come from `events.jsonl` |
| `Stop`: `sonar analyze agentic` per repo | Local analysis at the end of each turn | never fires | SonarCloud through the PR checks. The report says plainly that no local rule analysis ran (H: "state plainly in reports that no local rule analysis ran.") |
| Rules loading on Read | Injects matching rules | fires for Claude agents only | `standards.json` for both executors (8.6) |
| `SessionStart` | Session set-up | does not fire | Stage 0 preflight, in `tim backlog run open` |

### 8.8 How thin Claude stays

The cost of a run is its agents: each has a fixed start-up cost and spends tokens and wall-clock. So deterministic work never costs a judgement agent, and costs at most one cheap step agent per gap between judgement spans (C-074, `consolidation/decision.md` section 6).

For an increment with F content-changed files, K of them with findings, the Claude model tasks are 8 + 2F + K: plan, audit, implement, consistency, judge, fix, fix-verify, acceptance, plus reviews and verifies; one more verify-consistency task when consistency has findings. A typical increment (3 repos, F = 12, K = 6, consistency findings, one fix round, CI green), `claude` preset:

| Agent | Before, per action (the earlier 7.2) | Before, per stage (the earlier 8.8) | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Opus: plan, audit, consistency, judge, acceptance | 5 | 5 | 5 |
| Sonnet: implement, 24 reviews, 7 verifies, fix, fix verify | 34 | 34 | 34 |
| Haiku relays after a fan-out | 9 | 9 | 0 |
| Haiku action watchers | 49 | 11 | 0 |
| Haiku step agents | 0 | 0 | 4 in a drain (5 alone) |
| **Total** | **97** | **59** | **43** (44) |
| tim start-ups for mechanical work | about 100 (2 per action) plus 9 relays | about 30 | about 10 to 15 `advance` calls, one process each |
| Grows with repos, rungs, shards or PRs | yes | no | no |

A clean increment (reviews find nothing) spends 29 judgement tasks and 4 step agents: 33.

**Other presets and runs:**

| Run | Before (the earlier 7.2) | After | How |
|---|---|---|---|
| `codex` preset | about 58 Haiku | about 2 Haiku | every model task runs inside `advance`; one step agent loops to the stop, plus a handback on a long increment |
| `codex-claude-checks` | about 62 | about 9 | 4 Claude judgement tasks (plan audit, judge, fix-verify, acceptance), one step agent after each, plus handbacks |
| `codex-review-only` | about 69 | about 12 | 6 Claude judgement tasks (plan, audit, implement, judge, fix, acceptance; 8.4's table), 4 step agents, and 1 or 2 Haiku Codex runners for the J3 span, where Codex reviews, verifies and fix-verifies beside the Claude judge and fixer |
| Distil, `distil-claude` | 81 | 66 | 5.2: 56 judgement tasks, 10 step agents; 64 with no flagged slice |
| Distil, `distil-codex` | about 57 | about 40 to 42 | 5.2: 32 judgement tasks, about 8 step agents, plus handbacks |
| Per run, fixed | 1 resolve watcher, 8 main-session steps | 0 agents; `run open`, the Workflow call, `run close` | C-080 |

**Why not fewer.** A Workflow script cannot run a command, so after every judgement span something must accept, finalise and run the next mechanical step, and it must never be a judgement agent. M2 is a real gap (implement's standards depend on the plan's files, and an added repo needs a branch, merge and baseline first); so is M3 (reviews need the manifest, which needs the implement check), M4 (the ladder and proof must pass before acceptance reads the proof record) and M5 (land needs acceptance).

The in-process sub-steps cost no agent of their own, and the three routing watcher stages are gone (C-002, C-037). This is the orchestrator's "18 + n against 15 + 3n" (`build-orchestrator/SKILL.md:383-384`), now with every heavy stage on Codex rather than three, and no Sonnet or Opus agent anywhere in a `codex`-preset run.

### 8.9 Switching is a sentence

| Sam says | The skill writes |
|---|---|
| "Build inc-012 to inc-020 with Codex" | `tim backlog run bind <p> --ids inc-012..inc-020 --preset codex --said "<his words>" --by sam --at <ISO>` |
| "Codex for the rest" | `tim backlog run bind <p> --from next --preset codex --said "<his words>" --by sam --at <ISO>`, with `next` resolved to a concrete id when written. When an increment is in progress, `next` resolves to it, so the switch applies from its next stage |
| "Finish this one on Codex" | `tim backlog run bind <p> --ids <current> --preset codex --said "<his words>" --by sam --at <ISO>`, applying from the next stage prepared (C-003) |
| "Back to Claude after this one" | `tim backlog run bind <p> --from after-current --preset claude --said "<his words>" --by sam --at <ISO>` (C-003) |
| "I'm out of Claude" | `tim backlog handover <p> --executor codex --md`, pointing at the standing `build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md` (7.11, C-003) |
| "Keep the judge on Claude" | `tim backlog run bind <p> --from next --role judge=claude --said "<his words>" --by sam --at <ISO>` |
| "Back to Claude from inc-030" | `tim backlog run bind <p> --from inc-030 --preset claude --said "<his words>" --by sam --at <ISO>` |
| "Just the review on Codex" | `tim backlog run bind <p> --from next --preset codex-review-only --said "<his words>" --by sam --at <ISO>` |
| "Why is inc-017 on Codex?" | `tim backlog run explain <p> inc-017` |

A binding applies from the next stage prepared, even in the middle of an increment. A running task finishes on its executor, and prepared but unstarted tasks are re-prepared under the new binding (7.8).

`run explain` prints one line per role:

```
inc-017 Build drain
  plan          Codex, high effort, read-only        preset codex, from binding 1 ("build inc-012 to inc-020 with Codex")
  judge         Claude Opus, high effort, read-only  binding 2 ("keep the judge on Claude from here")
  review-code   Codex, medium effort, read-only      preset codex, from binding 1
  ...
```

A switch writes only `build/run.json`. The backlog does not change, and req-024's acceptance proves it: binding a range to Codex and back leaves `backlog.json`'s hash unchanged.

### 8.10 The parity proof

R6: "run one small increment each way and compare how thorough the review was, what the judge ruled and what the ladder showed." The judges widened it, and this design takes the wider version.

**Canaries.** Three small, full-stack behaviours taken from a live product backlog:

- Each touches a frontend, a backend and the tests repo (R7), so between them the Node and Nunjucks, Java and Playwright routing families each appear at least twice (judge 1: three families).
- One of the three is a combined increment (judge 1).
- They are carried into a canary programme by `tim backlog migrate <path> --to <p> --from loop-v1` (inc-026), re-sliced as vertical atoms, and verified by a registered verify task.
- The canary backlog is registered three times, as `parity-claude`, `parity-codex` and `parity-claude-2`, from one byte-identical file. All three use `local` delivery and the same base commit.

**Builds.**

1. inc-027 builds the three canaries under the `claude` preset. Sam walks through the first real builds on the new path.
2. inc-028 builds them under the `codex` preset.
3. inc-028 also builds them a second time under the `claude` preset, from the same base, registered as `parity-claude-2`. The two Claude builds give the Claude-to-Claude spread: how far two runs of the same executor differ on findings and judge rulings (C-018).
4. Each build's final diff is then reviewed again by the other executor's reviewers, in a `review-only` run of the build workflow (stages 0 and 12 to 15, 7.3).
5. The reading audit runs on every run.
6. `tim backlog compare --left <claude run> --right <codex run> --spread <claude run 2>` writes `report/parity.md`.

**Measures, per canary and in total:**

| Measure | How |
|---|---|
| Prompt parity | Every task's `prompt.md`, with the profile block removed, is byte-identical between the two runs |
| Review thoroughness | Files reviewed (must be equal, because the manifest decides); findings raised, verified and fixed, by severity |
| Cross-examination | Findings the other executor's reviewers raise on each final diff, and how many of those the judge would rule fix-now |
| Judge | Each judge also rules on the other side's verified findings: agreement on fix-now, defer and reject |
| Ladder and acceptance | The same rung results and the same per-criterion results, with the integration proof green on both sides |
| Reading | `tim backlog audit-reading` matches every "must" file in each task's `standards.json` against the recorded Claude reads (`build/reads/`) and the read commands in Codex's `events.jsonl`, counting a file only when its full line range was read (judges 1 and 3; C-011) |
| Standards | Both sides' `standards.json` record the same blob shas for the same task; when they differ, `compare` names the changed files and marks the pair not comparable, and the proof is re-run under the current standards. The shas are audit records, never a pin: a skill or best-practice edit is never held back to keep the proof comparable (C-037, 8.5) |
| Configuration | Codex version, model, effort and config hash recorded on every Codex receipt and in `runner.json`, the same across the run (C-009) |
| Task counts | Tasks per role, equal on both sides; Claude agents by tier in the Codex run (C-018) |
| Claude usage | Claude agents by tier in each run, counted from the journal, so the saving R6 exists for is measured, not assumed (C-018); and cheap agents per increment, by tier, from the journal, against 8.8 (C-074) |
| Correction and completion | `attemptsToAccept` per role; the `completed` rate and the size of `notReached` (C-004, C-006) |
| Verify and fix | Verifier confirm rate; fix applied rate from fix-verify |
| Backlog | `parity-claude` and `parity-codex` have the same requirement content hash before and after, and their files differ only in row status fields |
| Silent success | Every task has a receipt, and every done increment passed the stage audit |

**The bar** (S4, default option D, measured; C-018):

- **Must hold exactly:**
  - prompt parity for every task;
  - no unread "must" file on either side;
  - identical ladder and acceptance results;
  - the same requirement content hash;
  - no missing receipt;
  - equal task counts per role;
  - no Sonnet or Opus agent in the `codex`-preset run;
  - every review receipt `completed`;
  - the same standards shas on both sides, so both read the same live files (a difference means a standard changed between the builds, and the proof is re-run on the current one, 8.5), and one Codex configuration throughout;
  - no confirmed critical or major finding in either final diff under cross-examination.
- **Must fall within the measured spread** (option D): Codex's fix-now recall and its judge agreement, each measured over the union after cross-examination, fall within the Claude-to-Claude spread from the two Claude builds.
- **Floors,** whatever the spread: Codex's reviewers find at least 90% of the fix-now findings Claude's reviewers find, and the two judges agree on at least 80% of fix-now rulings. Option A, the fixed thresholds on their own, stays open to Sam as an alternative (9.2 S4).

Fixed thresholds alone cannot tell "as good" from "worse" without knowing how far two Claude runs already differ; the spread gives the bar its meaning.

**When a measure fails,** `compare` raises a discovered hygiene increment through `tim backlog discover`, naming the profile, brief or stage at fault. It is verified and adopted like any discovered atom (7.10).

Codex mode is called equal when the bar holds, and `report/parity.md` says so or names each measure that failed. Distil phases bound to Codex are outside R6's proof. They stay optional until a distil parity check shows the same.

### 8.11 The Codex-orchestrated mode

When Claude's weekly limit is spent, Sam can drive the build from a Codex session.

- **The handover.** `tim backlog handover <p> --executor codex --md` generates it from the same commands (judge 1: "Generate the Codex-orchestrated handover from the same commands"). A standing copy is always at `build/HANDOVER-CODEX.md`, written at every record and every hold (7.11, C-003).
- **The loop the Codex session runs** (C-002, C-074) is the step agent's loop (7.2, and the STEP AGENT text in 4.6), with the session as the step agent:
  1. run `tim backlog advance <p> --run <label> --agent <label>:codex --json`, and run it again on exit 75. `advance` runs every Codex-bound task in-process through `tim backlog codex run`, and every local mechanical step itself;
  2. on `act`, run each listed command verbatim, one Bash call each, stopping at the first non-zero exit; the next `advance` observes each effect, so nothing is recorded by hand;
  3. on `handback`, carry on with the same command (the session is its own fresh step);
  4. repeat until `advance` returns `stop`. A task bound to Claude stops it with `claude-needed`.

  There is no "or directly": tasks always run through `tim backlog codex run`, so every task gets the same accept, correction rounds and receipts. `advance` decides every transition, so the session cannot follow different rules from a Workflow run, and `tim backlog status done` is still gated by the stage audit.
- **Full access is required.** The orchestrating session pushes, commits state and starts nested `codex exec`, so it must run with full access. The handover says so, and `tim backlog codex run --check` verifies it.
- **The same stage table, briefs, schemas and done gate** apply. What differs is named in the handover:

| Differs | Consequence |
|---|---|
| No Workflow runtime | Tasks run one span at a time inside `advance`, with `tim backlog codex run` for concurrency |
| No per-call Claude hooks | Guard-bash, guard-edits and the secrets-read check do not see the session's own commands. What remains: the generated commands are exact and allowlisted by construction, tim's branch proof and fast-forward check run inside `land` and `record` before any push is generated, in both modes, and every outward effect is observed afterwards (C-002, C-076), accept and the manifest refuse protected paths, and nested Codex tasks still run under the pipeline's Codex home and `guard.sh` (8.7) |
| No auto-mode classifier | Nothing reviews each push, PR or merge as its own call. The session runs only the commands `advance` generated, and tim's refusals and observation, which are tested code, are what remain; putting them in tim is a gain here too |
| SonarCloud | The push-record hook does not fire for `git -C` pushes in either mode, so this is no loss: stage 24 reads PR checks, and no SonarCloud result is lost (C-042) |
| Docker and browsers | A full-access Codex orchestrator can reach Docker. It runs the integration proof and the browser rungs through `advance`, which runs them inside tim exactly as in a Workflow run, with the same fact records; nothing is owed (C-035) |
| When it cannot reach Docker | The increment holds `proof-owed`, browser rungs included. A `proof-owed` increment is not done, so its dependants are not buildable; independent increments continue. The next Claude session proves each owed increment separately, in build order, on a stack built at that increment's heads, with its own record (7.4). One stack run over several slices could not say which one broke |
| `quality.integrationProofSource: ci` | Available in either mode: the proof comes from the named CI workflow's end-to-end check after CI (7.4, 7.7) |

---

## 9. Decisions

### 9.1 Every open question, answered

The questions are the synthesis's (`00-synthesis.md:630-695`). "Reversible" means that changing the decision later needs no rebuild of built work. "To reverse" gives the cost in files and commands.

| Q | Question | Decision | Why | Reversible | To reverse |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Canonical store | A neutral store: atoms and increments in `backlog.json`, with `specRefs` linking out to OpenSpec. Projections come later (`out-projections`) | Two stores mean two lints, two gates and two reports (D §9) | yes | Add a projection command. No schema change, because `specRefs` already link |
| Q2 | Where a requirement set lives | `workareas/shared/<programme>/`, tracked, found through `tools/backlog/registry.json` | CLAUDE.md already tracks `workareas/shared/`, and a registry removes path-building readers (G5) | yes | One registry entry: `tim backlog registry add <p> --workarea <path>` |
| Q3 | Schema base | Generalise the parity writer's core into `tim backlog`; parity becomes profile `parity-v1`. The core moves by `git mv` only so its history is kept | In use it is proven id-stable and ruling-safe, and one core gives every programme the same locks, op ids and lost-update refusals (G2). inc-004 must also prove it on a requirements-v2 fixture, not on parity alone (C-043) | partly | `git revert` of inc-004, before later increments build on it |
| Q4 | Status placement | Six statuses each for atoms and increments (3.9). All phase detail is in `build/state.json`. `doneBy` separates built from ruled | Readers key only on id, status and dependencies (E §5), and state on the row corrupted headers (S §4.6) | no, in practice | A schema version 3 and a migration; every reader changes |
| Q5 | Splitting `gate` | `needs` (before build) and `checkpoint` (after build) replace `gate` | `gate` meant three things (G5) | yes | Add `gate` to the v2 schema; `parity-v1` keeps it |
| Q6 | What replaces `type` | Atom `kind` and increment `class` (branch prefix and commit type). The route is the plan's choice | `type` was an implementor mode leaking into requirements (G §9) | yes | Add a kind value; the plan already records the route |
| Q7 | Exemplars | No exemplar slot. `consistentWith` names a behaviour; the repo's registry entry `knowledge[]` names repo-owned how-to documents (C-058). Code citations reach the planner labelled "evidence of current behaviour, not a design to copy" (C-063) | Path-shaped exemplars leak recipes (judge 3) | yes | Add `exemplars` to the schema and remove it from the refused list: one schema file, one test |
| Q8 | Size | `size {class, basis}` from requirement proxies, which never gates building. The combine ceiling is 12 criteria, 1 new surface and 2 journeys, with no repo limit (R7) | Size gates stalled work; a ceiling bounds the review surface | yes | Change `combination.ceiling` with a reason, then re-combine |
| Q9 | Atoms after combining | Kept in `requirements[]`; increments hold `members` only | An increment's acceptance is always its members' current acceptance (judge 1) | no | Copying acceptance into increments: a schema and renderer change |
| Q10 | Data-model design | A `spike` atom the implementor owns, never a distil phase | The trace Model phase leaked class names into acceptance (F Q2) | yes | Add a distil phase; the phase table is profile data |
| Q11 | Tests and end-to-end coverage | Part of every increment's definition of done, through each criterion's `witness`, the integration proof and the done gate. There are no tests-only increments | R7; the plants backlog has 10 tests-only rows (0.4) | no (R7) | Only if Sam changes R7 |
| Q12 | Panel authority and trigger | The panel runs only for conflicts precedence cannot settle. Its authority is Sam question S1, default A | 8 dockets of 4 agents is a cost worth paying only when needed | yes | Sam rules `q-panel-authority` |

It is applied automatically and reported, and `combine: propose` in the contract makes it a proposal | "Make the call, flag it after"; re-combining is free and ids are stable | yes | One line in `distil/CONTRACT.md` |
| Q14 | Cross-repo lockstep | Lockstep work is one vertical slice from pass 1, so it never needs combining, and pass 2 never splits by layer | R7 | no (R7) | Only if Sam changes R7 |

### 9.2 The decisions that stay with Sam

Six questions stay with Sam, because each is a policy call, a scope call, a quality call or a choice about a standard only he owns. Everything else was decided and recorded.

This section keeps no copy of them (C-052). Three hand-written copies of the same questions had already started to disagree, so there is one record and one view:

- **The record** is `design/backlog.json` `questions[]`, with the defaults in force recorded as `defaulted` decisions in `decisions[]`. The S numbers used elsewhere in this design map to question ids: S1 `q-panel-authority`, S2 `q-house-rules-source`, S3 `q-dr1u-remainder`, S4 `q-parity-bar`, S5 `q-conversation-retention`, S6 `q-distil-canary-set`.
- **The view** is `design/decisions-for-sam.md`, stamped as a hand-written bootstrap view of `questions[]`. From inc-025 it is generated by `tim backlog report --decisions`. Until then, inc-009 adds a check that the page's ids, defaults and blocked increments match the JSON.
- **What is owed to Sam** that is not a question is listed under "Owed to you" on the same page. Agents cannot edit the workspace settings, hooks or `/config`, so four things wait for him. This list is the single source in the body that the page's "Owed to you" must match, item for item. inc-009's check (req-030 ac-2) compares the page's questions with `backlog.json`; the owed items are not backlog records, so the fix-up review compares them with this list until inc-025 generates the page:
  1. **Before the first build run:** raise "Dynamic workflow size" in `/config` (7.2, the agent ceiling).
  2. **Before inc-019:** wire the `PostToolUse` hook on `Read` that runs `tools/backlog/record-read.sh` (4.6, 8.7, C-011).
  3. **Before inc-014:** extend guard-edits and the secrets-read check to cover `build/runs/**/receipt.json`, `build/runs/**/output.json`, `build/runs/**/facts/*.json` and `build/.accept-key` (4.5 `stage accept` step 7, C-012, C-082).
  4. **Accept or refuse the deviation from CLAUDE.md rule 3:** the pipeline commits without `sonar analyze --staged`, and SonarCloud evidence comes from PR checks, with "no report" shown as "no report" (4.6 COMMIT RULE, C-042, C-076).

  The pin needs no allow rule: `tim backlog pin` installs the pinned tim, which runs through the allowed `npm --prefix` form (`settings.json:85`), and no pinned bash tool is called (7.12, C-079).

Under C-044 every question either must be answered or has its default in force. Five of the six have a default in force now, so nothing waits on them. S5 is the only must-answer question, and it blocks nothing today: the retention rule already in force keeps other people's words out of git (C-045).

### 9.3 Where the candidates disagreed

Every cell below that once rested on reuse, build feasibility or "no change to either skill's tooling" has been re-decided on merit in use (R8). Where that changed the choice, the cell says "overturned by r8-merit" and names the change id from `corrections/change-list.md`.

| Topic | reuse | quality | codex | Chosen, and the merit reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Who owns the stage order | the workflow script | the workflow scripts | `tim build advance` in tim | tim decides and executes; agents judge. `tim backlog advance` holds every transition rule and is tested on fixture folders; the workflow script is a thin interpreter (overturned by r8-merit, C-002). The stage machine was declined only for rebuild cost, which carries no weight. In use, a second copy of the transition rules in prose for a Codex-orchestrated session is lossy, and resume logic in untestable workflow code is where the loop has failed before. The merit-based refusals stand, revised by the consolidation: tim never pushes, merges a PR or writes to Jira itself, and never shells out to `tools/` for an integration (a committed-script rung is a test command, 0.3); it runs every local, reversible step itself and generates each outward action as a visible call (C-075) |
| Cadence | drain in one Workflow, no nesting | nested child per increment, with a canary | nested child per increment | reuse's drain, plus a clean stop at an agent ceiling. In use, a nested child's re-read and resume are undocumented (judge 2), and the drain re-reads bindings every iteration, so "Codex for the rest" works with no relaunch |
| Atoms and increments | `requirements[]` beside `increments[]` with `members` | atoms as rows marked combined | rows only, pass 2 not run | Atoms beside increments that hold members only: an increment's acceptance is always its members' current acceptance, never a stale copy (judge 1) |
| Exemplars | none | `hints.exemplars`, non-binding | `exemplars[]` with file paths | none (judge 3) |
| Run knobs | `run.json` | header `lifecycle.phases` | header `lifecycle` | `run.json` (judges 2 and 3) |
| Output transport | an agent re-emits Codex's JSON | a relay plus a tim check | task folders, `stage accept`, receipts | codex's (judges 1, 2 and 3) |
| Consistency reviewer | one per change | one per repo | one per change | One per increment, reading every repo, with a verdict per repo: the review skill's own step 4 (`review/SKILL.md:147-162`) and R7. This corrects judges 1 and 2, who read `SKILL.md:66` as one agent per repo. The persona gains a "Contract between repos" method with a verdict per plan seam, and its findings go through verify, judge and fix (C-023, C-024) |
| Review coverage | every changed file, with a knob capped at 12 | every changed file | only the plan's `reviewFocus`, capped at 16 | Every content-changed file, with no cap by default; an optional cap lists what it skipped (R6; judge 1, graft 22) |
| Judge in Codex mode | Claude by default | Codex by default | Codex by default | Codex in the `codex` preset, compared in the parity proof (judge 3) |
| Skill routing | the skills' routers plus a new `--list` flag on the baker | the baker unchanged, with a build key | routing rewritten into `routing.json` | codex's: routing is data each skill owns, `.claude/skills/code-style/assets/routing.json` and `.claude/skills/review/assets/routing.json`, read live by the skills' own scripts and by one resolver, `tim backlog standards` (overturned by r8-merit, C-037). The composer was chosen for "no change to either skill's tooling", a least-change reason. In use, data routing removes three routing stages per increment, puts all routing in tested code, records blob shas so the parity proof can show both executors read the same standards, and gives writers repo-level routing as well as planned-file routing. R2 still holds, and golden tests keep the skills' own output unchanged for people who run them |
| Acceptance | a knob beside the consistency reviewer's invariant proofs | an independent stage | a task inside the review fan-out | An independent stage after the ladder, secrets and integration proof, before land; it never receives the plan and names a passing test for every end-to-end criterion (quality; judges 1 and 2; C-021) |
| Workflow files | one file, two modes | three, with a nested child | three, with a nested child | Two thin interpreters, distil and build, over `advance --distil` and `advance`, with a contract test keeping their shared parts identical. In use each reads as its own loop, and every transition rule lives in tim's tested stage tables (C-002) |
| SonarCloud | the PR check, plus a recorded "not run" | not settled | stages that run the `sonar` CLI | The PR check, read by tim's check waiter; silence is "no report". `sonar-check-pending.sh` is not used, because its records come from a hook that never fires for the pipeline's pushes (C-076). Codex's stage cannot run, because `sonar` is not runnable by agents (judge 1). The pipeline commits without `sonar analyze --staged`, a deviation from CLAUDE.md rule 3 recorded for Sam (C-042) |
| Schema strictness | tolerant, with a recipe lint | strict, refusing recipe and executor keys by name | a test that every field has a class | Strict with named refusals (quality; judge 3), plus codex's class test |
| Applying rulings | predeclared option effects in one write | an Opus applier, then ingest | rulings copied onto rows | reuse's one write. The applier only handles free-text answers, and its new wording arrives as variant atoms that are verified before adoption (judge 3 rejected copying rulings onto rows) |
| Distillation on Codex | heavy phases bindable | not addressed | bindable through the same machine | Every distil model phase bindable by role; the `distil-codex` preset binds the heavy local phases, and judgement phases stay on Claude under every shipped preset until a distil parity check (judge 3; 5.11) |
| Lifecycle actions | Claude watchers with `tools/` scripts | Claude watchers | tim pushes and merges | Local, reversible effects (branch cuts, merges of base, rungs, the stack, commits, the state commit) run inside tim as tested code on git fixtures. Each push, draft PR, merge and Jira write is a visible call tim generates only after its own refusals pass (branch proof, fast-forward, expected head, every PR green, a person's merge decision), run verbatim by the one step agent for that gap and observed by tim afterwards. Those calls are allowlisted (`settings.json:83, 88`), so the allowlist is not the gate: tim's refusals, guard-bash on each call and observation are (C-042, C-075, C-076; decision section 7). The push-record hook is not the reason: it matches only `git push…`, so it never fires for `git -C` pushes |
| House rules | not addressed | not addressed | raised as a question | Default A in force now: both executors read the rules by path, so parity holds. S2 stays with Sam. It is defaulted, not must-answer, because it is not a security, access-control, data-integrity, legal or policy question; judge 3 suggested must-answer. The default differs from the recommendation (B) because moving the rules changes shared bundles the team uses, a standard Sam owns; that reason is the question's `defaultWhy` (C-044) |

## 10. Retire and migrate

All changes happen on this programme's branch, `chore/NO_JIRA-requirements-pipeline`. Main keeps the old files until Sam merges the programme.

`git mv` is used only where the moved file keeps doing the same job and its history is worth keeping. It is never a reason for a choice (principle 12). Where the old and new parts do different jobs, the new part is written fresh and names its lineage in its commit message, and the old part keeps working for the live programmes that use it (the plants loop, frontend alignment) until inc-040 retires it. Live programmes keep an operable loop throughout the build (C-002, C-072).

| `.claude/skills/journey-builder/` | frozen in place, then deleted | inc-038, inc-040 | `requirements-distiller` is a new skill folder (inc-029 onwards, completed in inc-038) holding only the distiller; DIGEST's method, rule mode and panel lessons are cut into its personas. In inc-038 journey-builder gains a one-line banner pointing to `requirements-distiller` and `backlog-implementor`. Its spec tools stay for the frozen plants spec. ## 11. The build backlog for this programme

### 11.1 How it gets built

How this programme is built is run state, not backlog content (R5, C-056). Everything in this section lives in `build/run.json`, the journal and the handover. The backlog says only what must be true.

0. **Before inc-001: the bootstrap verification record** (C-053). The design wrote these atoms, so the design cannot verify them, and inc-010 cannot run a registered verification before task folders exist (inc-014). So before inc-001:
   - an agent that did not write the atoms walks `analysis/sam-requirements.md` sentence by sentence, and records for each sentence the atom that cites it, or why it was dismissed;
   - a second agent checks every dismissal;
   - the same pass runs a fresh question critic over `questions[]`. A by-hand critic pass already ran for this revision (each question's `lint` names it, C-051), but the reviser who ran it also reshaped four of the questions, so a critic that wrote none of them must check them again;
   - the results go under `distil/bootstrap-verify/`, where the registered-run guard accepts them as upstream input to inc-010, never as a verification on their own (4.3).
   
   Every `sam-req` citation was resealed from `git hash-object` for this revision. Method quotes were replaced with the needs they serve, and the atoms that still rest only on current-behaviour or constraint evidence are listed in d-003's `appliesTo`.
1. **The bootstrap stretch: inc-001 to inc-024, plus inc-041.** The current loop accepts only `repos/*` paths (`increment-build-loop.js:183-189`), and the new workflow does not exist yet. So these increments are built by a person-driven session, one increment per commit on `chore/NO_JIRA-requirements-pipeline`. This is a fact about the run, recorded in the journal and the handover. It is not a backlog deviation, because the backlog carries no build-mode words (C-056). Each increment gets:
   - its ladder from the programme's registry entry (C-058): tim `format:check`, `lint` and `test`, then `tools/backlog/self-test.sh` once it exists;
   - a review with the review and code-style skills, run the way a person runs them;
   - the done gate, checked against the stage records from inc-014 onwards;
   - `tim backlog check --strict` over this backlog passes before inc-010 (inc-007, C-053).
2. **Verification after inc-014.** Increments up to inc-014 are built from atoms that no registered run has verified yet, checked by the bootstrap record (`as-bootstrap-unverified`). inc-010 follows inc-014. It runs REQUIREMENT_VERIFIER tasks by slice through `stage prepare` and `stage accept`, from a Workflow launched inline by the session that drives the build, because subagents cannot launch Workflow. An atom it corrects whose increment is already built becomes a variant atom, with a follow-up increment that depends on the built one. The built increment stays done, and there is no move from done back to todo (C-055).
3. **Self-hosting from inc-025.** From inc-025, `backlog-build.js` builds the rest with `checkout: clones`, because this programme changes the pipeline's own tools (C-065). It also uses `toolPin: launch-commit`, programme delivery, draft PRs and merge `never`. inc-025, the decision-led report, is the first increment the new path builds, which makes it the path's own canary.
4. **Checkpoints.**
   - inc-027 stops for a walkthrough of the first real full-stack builds on Claude.
   - inc-028 stops for the parity result.
   - inc-038 stops for the distiller canary.

   The canaries of inc-027 and inc-028 are built by their own programmes (`parity-claude`, `parity-codex` and `parity-claude-2`) under `local` delivery. The canaries' product repos (plants frontend, plants backend, tests) are in those increments' surfaces, so P11, the edge checks and the merge plan all see the repos those increments change.
5. **What waits on Sam.** Nothing is blocked:
   - `q-conversation-retention` is the only must-answer question, and it blocks nothing, because the retention rule already in force keeps other people's words out of git (C-045);
   - the other five questions have defaults in force, recorded as `defaulted` decisions d-004 to d-008 (C-044). Under them, inc-016 is deferred (d-005) and inc-039 is dropped (d-006), each until Sam rules otherwise. Each default is reversible by one ruling: the ruling supersedes the default, which restores every target the default touched to its recorded `prior` before the new option's effects run (3.7), so inc-016 or inc-039 returns to `todo` and its member to `proposed`;
   - Sam's ruling on any question supersedes its default.
6. **Executors for this programme.** This programme builds on Claude until inc-028 passes. Which roles the `codex` preset puts on Codex, including the judge and acceptance, is a note in `presets.json`, not a backlog assumption (C-056).

### 11.3 Increments

142 atoms are in the file. Eleven are variant atoms: nine held ready for question options (req-124, req-125, req-128 to req-134) and two for reversing a conflict (req-126, req-127). One is parked by a default in force (req-110). They were combined into 42 increments, of which 41 are live: 33 combined and 8 solo. inc-013 was dissolved into inc-019 before it started (C-036). 11 candidate pairs were left apart. No increment is blocked: inc-016 is deferred and inc-039 is dropped under defaults in force.

This revision re-combined the programme under C-027, C-036 and C-053:

- **Every combination is justified by behaviour** and cites its rule ids. `cr-same-repo-set` is gone. inc-001, inc-006, inc-022 and inc-040 lost it and were re-justified. inc-014, inc-015 and inc-017 were re-examined for attributability and stand, each with a behaviour reason.
- **inc-021 was split.** Acceptance now reads the integration-proof record (C-021), so a red stack inside the same increment would be misread as an acceptance failure. The proof and its stack became inc-041, *Every product slice is proven through the real stack*, which inc-021 depends on. It sits before inc-021 in the file, so array order still reads as build order.
- **The distiller is a walking skeleton.** inc-029 runs one markdown source end to end through `advance --distil`. inc-030 to inc-037 each widen that path, with a criterion that runs the distil workflow on a fixture and shows the new behaviour in the rendered backlog or report.
- **The persona seam joined the review stage** (req-044 moved into inc-019). Declined pair 4 is gone.
- **inc-010 follows inc-014**, and moves to m1.
- **inc-017 carries a multi-repo fixture programme**: a provider, a consumer and a tests repo, as small local repos with a real contract test. req-063 and req-117 to req-119 are proved on it.

The stage consolidation (`consolidation/decision.md`, C-074 to C-082) then added seven atoms, req-136 to req-142, sourced from Sam's steer (`sam-steer`) and `sam-req` line 134. Five joined existing increments whose acceptance boundary they share: req-140 (local git in the writer) inc-017, req-139 (judgement spans) inc-019, req-138 (outward actions visible and observed) inc-022, req-141 (distil tasks start without an agent between them) inc-029, and req-142 (empty sections are not cold-read) inc-037. req-136 and req-137 became a new increment, inc-042, after inc-024 in the file. Its `dependsOn` also names inc-017, because req-137 depends on req-121, which lifts to inc-017; the decision's list left it out, and the lifting rule requires it. Criteria that named bash tools the consolidation never writes were deleted or reworded (req-001 ac-3, req-050 ac-6, req-055 ac-3, req-068, req-077, req-118 ac-4, req-121).

### 11.4 The backlog as JSON

This is the header of `design/backlog.json` in the v2 schema. The ledgers are shown by id, `increments[]` is the table in 11.3 and `requirements[]` is the table in 11.5. The file holds every record in full; this section no longer repeats the increment records, because a second hand-kept copy drifts from the file (the reason C-052 gives for the decisions page).

The repo how-facts that left the header (C-058) live in this programme's registry entry in `tools/backlog/registry.json`, which is not part of the backlog:

- `github`: `DEFRA/trade-imports-workspace`;
- `ladder`: tim `format:check`, `lint` and `test`, then the committed `tools/backlog/self-test.sh`;
- `knowledge`: `workareas/shared/requirements-pipeline/design/DESIGN.md` (this programme's knowledge), `CLAUDE.md`, `tim/CLAUDE.md`, `tim/README.md`, `docs/agent-skills.md`, `docs/git-conventions.md` and the skill-creator's `AUDITOR.md`;
- `generated`: `tim/package-lock.json`, `workareas/**/report/*.html` and `workareas/shared/requirements-pipeline/**`, so the programme's own state never dirties a fingerprint or a manifest (C-065).

```json
{
  "schemaVersion": 2,
  "programme": "requirements-pipeline",
  "profile": "requirements-v2",
  "title": "Requirements distiller and backlog implementor",
  "purpose": "Sam and the team need one pipeline that turns loose requirements from any mix of sources into a verified, combined backlog with a decision-led report, and builds that backlog to the same standard on Claude or Codex. It replaces four drivers, three buildability rules and three ruling mechanisms that drifted apart. It is needed now because Codex mode builds worse than Claude mode while Sam's weekly Claude limit keeps running out.",
  "sources": ["sam-req", "r8-merit", "rules-probe", "synthesis", "gap-1", "gap-2", "gap-4", "gap-5", "h-rails", "g-reports", "judge-1", "judge-2", "judge-3", "workspace-code", "alignment-branch", "sam-steer"],
  "precedence": ["intent", "scope", "flow"],
  "invariants": ["inv-no-recipe", "inv-executor-neutral", "inv-vertical-slices", "inv-parity-untouched", "inv-one-writer", "inv-live-standards", "inv-tim-rails", "inv-plain-english", "inv-merit-only"],
  "direction": "Choose the option that is better in use: output quality, requirement and report quality, Codex and Claude parity, full-stack slices, correctness, and operability under the rails. Build effort, diff size and the number of new files carry no weight (R8). Collapsing an overhead that every run pays counts.",
  "scope": { "in": ["The requirements distiller, its phases and its report", "The backlog implementor, its build workflow and both executors", "The shared writer, schema and stage contract", "Migrating live backlogs and retiring what the pipeline replaces"],
             "out": ["out-parity-v2", "out-cdp", "out-projections", "out-parallel-builds"] },
  "deviations": ["dv-tooling-commands"],
  "milestones": [
    { "id": "m0", "name": "Proven base", "goal": "The proven fixes are on main, and one validated writer holds every piece of state.", "checkpoint": null },
    { "id": "m1", "name": "Build path", "goal": "The build workflow builds an increment end to end on either executor, and nothing succeeds silently.", "checkpoint": null },
    { "id": "m2", "name": "First real build", "goal": "The new path has built real full-stack work on Claude, and the report leads with decisions.", "checkpoint": "walkthrough" },
    { "id": "m3", "name": "Codex is equal", "goal": "Codex mode meets the parity bar.", "checkpoint": "walkthrough" },
    { "id": "m4", "name": "Distiller", "goal": "Any mix of sources becomes a verified, combined backlog and a decision-led report.", "checkpoint": "walkthrough" },
    { "id": "m5", "name": "One pipeline", "goal": "Live backlogs are migrated and the old paths are retired.", "checkpoint": null }
  ],

                "stateBranch": "chore/NO_JIRA-requirements-pipeline", "ticketing": null,

                   "ceiling": { "acceptance": 12, "newSurfaces": 1, "e2eJourneys": 2 }, "declined": "11 pairs, in the file", "basis": { "atomsSha": null } },

```

The product repos are in the header because the canary increments (inc-027, inc-028) change them (C-036). Their `consumes` lists follow the obvious service calls and must be confirmed from the `docker/stack` service dependencies when the programme is registered (5.3). Every combined increment carries `combination.reverseEffects`, a `split` into its members, so Sam can pull any group apart with one command (C-047). Solo increments store no `outcome` (C-062).

### 11.5 Atoms

Every atom is `proposed`, with `verifiedBy: null` and verdict `unverified`, until inc-010 verifies it in a registered run. One exception: req-110 is `parked` under the default in force for S3. req-001 to req-120 were authored by the lead architect; req-121 to req-125 were added by this revision, req-126 to req-134 by the fix-up that gave every behaviour-changing option and conflict reversal its variant, req-135 by the fix-up that made reading both review skills live an atom of its own (8.5), and req-136 to req-142 by the stage consolidation (`consolidation/decision.md`, task `consolidation-judge`); their `authoredBy` says so. Each atom cites its sources verbatim, with line numbers, and every citation was re-checked against its sealed source for this revision (11.6).

This revision re-sourced the atoms (C-053):

- every quote that described a method (a file to add, a tool to call, a stage to insert) was replaced with the need it serves, taken from Sam's requirements, the R8 merit audit or a problem statement in the same source;
- 82 of the 142 atoms now rest on at least one requirement or decision-record source, 61 cite Sam's requirements directly, and 8 cite Sam's consolidation steer (req-121 and req-136 to req-142);
- the other 60 rest only on current-behaviour or constraint evidence. They stay `inferred`, and decision d-003 adopts them as requirements, listing each one in its `appliesTo`, until the bootstrap record and inc-010 check them. The 60 include three variants, req-125, req-127 and req-128. d-003 lists them too, because the 3.12 role check reads an atom's sources, not whether a ruling has swapped it in yet; a ruling that later adopts a variant does not need to adopt it a second time.

These counts come from `jq` over `backlog.json`: an atom counts as resting on a requirement when any source has `role` `requirement` or `decision-record`, and as citing Sam when any source is `sam-req`.

req-124 to req-134 are variant atoms (`variantOf`). req-124, req-125 and req-128 to req-134 are held ready for the question options that change what gets built (option C of `q-house-rules-source`, B of `q-conversation-retention`, B and C of `q-panel-authority`, A, B and C of `q-parity-bar`, and B and C of `q-distil-canary-set`). req-126 and req-127 are held ready for reversing c-002 and c-003 to their losing sides. They belong to no increment until a ruling adopts them, and the ruling that adopts one swaps it into its root's increment without changing that increment's id (C-054).
