### 11.6 The file, and how it was checked

The file is `workareas/shared/requirements-pipeline/design/backlog.json`. For this revision it was checked with `jq`, the way `tim backlog check --strict` will check it once inc-005 lands:

- it parses;
- every atom that is not a variant is in exactly one live increment, and every member exists; the superseded inc-013 is ignored;
- every atom and increment dependency exists, and the increment graph is acyclic. Ids no longer have to point downwards: inc-010 depends on inc-014 (C-053), and inc-041 sits before inc-021 in the file;
- every live increment's `dependsOn` equals its members' lifted dependencies exactly, and its `surface.repos` equals the union of its members';
- every cross-repo dependency, on atoms and on increments, carries an `edgeChecks` record: 24 edges on nine rows, the canary atoms req-082 and req-083, the parity-bar variants req-130, req-131 and req-132 (a variant keeps its original's dependencies, so it keeps its edge records too), req-111, and the increments inc-027, inc-028 and inc-040. Every record's `by` is a task identity object (3.4), never a string;
- only a must-answer question appears in any `needs`. No increment is blocked. inc-016 is deferred and inc-039 is dropped, each naming the defaulted decision that put it there;
- no increment exceeds 12 criteria (the largest, inc-019, has 12), and each `size.basis` states its real count;
- the new source `sam-steer` (`consolidation/sam-steer.md`) is quoted by req-136 to req-142, and its quotes were checked against its lines; the seven atoms are all in the `workspace` repo, so they add no cross-repo edge and no `edgeChecks` record, and inc-042's `dependsOn` equals its members' lifted dependencies;
- every solo increment stores no `title` or `outcome` and has the key `solo--<member key>`; every combined increment has an `outcome`, rule ids in its reason, and a `split` reverse effect naming exactly its members' keys;
- every conflict has a `reverseEffects` key for each losing side: c-002 and c-003 supersede to the variants req-126 and req-127, and c-001, c-004 and c-005 carry an empty list with a `reverseNotes` entry saying why; every decision's subject kind is one of the four in 3.7;
- every question option that changes behaviour carries an effect that makes the change (3.6): a `supersede` to a variant atom (req-124, req-125, req-128 to req-134), or an `adopt`, `park`, `reject`, `defer-increment` or `drop-increment` on an existing atom or increment (q-house-rules-source, q-dr1u-remainder, q-conversation-retention); an option that only restates the current atoms sets its assumption, and every option of a question with an assumption sets it to its own letter;
- every applied effect on a decision carries `prior`, so a ruling that supersedes a default reverts it (3.7): d-005's deferral of inc-016 restores it to `todo`, and d-006's park of req-110 and drop of inc-039 restore them to `proposed` and `todo`. q-dr1u-remainder's options B and C now drop inc-039 explicitly, which their consequence text always said;
- each question's `restsOnDefault` equals the number of atoms citing its default's assumption, by the 3.6 rule: 2 for q-house-rules-source (req-045, req-046) and q-dr1u-remainder (req-005, req-110), 1 for the other defaulted questions, and 0 for the must-answer q-conversation-retention;
- no question has two options with identical effects, a default that differs from its recommendation has `defaultWhy`, `mustAnswer` matches a null default, and every headline is 90 characters or fewer;
- `cr-same-repo-set`, `crossRepoE2e`, the `blocking` question class, option `count` and `blastRadius.increments` are all gone;
- each criterion's confidence is `stated` exactly when it cites a requirement or decision-record source, and each atom's confidence is its weakest criterion's;
- every assumption, decision, source and milestone reference resolves;
- all 181 quotes from markdown sources match their cited lines in the sealed files (`**` emphasis ignored), including every `sam-req` quote against the resealed blob `62b7f410`. The 4 code citations are unchanged from the design and were not re-read for this revision. The quotes the fix-up added for req-126 to req-134 were checked against their cited lines the same way, and the one code citation they reuse (`const cap = 12`) carries its blob. req-135's two `sam-req` quotes were checked against lines 21 and 23 of the sealed blob `62b7f410`. The consolidation's 19 new quotes (req-121 s4 and req-136 to req-142) were checked the same way: the `sam-steer` quotes against lines 5 to 7 of blob `dd27b6ed`, and the `sam-req` quotes against lines 134 and 140 of `62b7f410`;
- the executor and build-mode scan finds no model tier, "sandbox", "by hand" or "main session" in any prose field (a source's verbatim `quote` is evidence, not prose, and is not scanned; req-051's one quote names a sandbox). Executor and preset names appear only as the product's own interface, which `dv-tooling-commands` enumerates;
- there are no em or en dashes.

What the checks cannot show: whether each of d-003's 60 inferred atoms is a real need. That is the bootstrap record's and inc-010's job.

---

## 12. Risks, and what to prove first

### 12.1 Risks

| # | Risk | What contains it | Proven in |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Moving the parity core breaks parity CI or the tracked EUDPA-328 page, or serves parity but not requirement backlogs | The move must keep parity's tests and page unchanged, and must also keep every id stable on a registry-found requirements-v2 fixture (C-043); it is declined from combining with the schema change | inc-004 |
| 2 | A Codex CLI upgrade renames a flag or changes `resume`, or a change to `~/.codex` (model, MCP servers, plugins, memories, hooks) silently changes what every Codex task sees | `tim backlog codex run --check` checks both `exec --help` and `exec resume --help` against every flag used, and fails loudly. Every `codex exec` runs under the pipeline's own `CODEX_HOME` with an explicit model and nothing from `~/.codex`; `--check` prints the effective configuration and runs one real no-op resume. Every receipt records the Codex version, model, effort and config hash, and `compare` fails a measure when they differ (C-009) | inc-015 |
| 3 | Codex's final message is too large for big distil outputs | Task-write mode lets a task write files in its own folder, and accept moves them into place | inc-038 |
| 4 | The Opus session limit under fan-out | `opusConcurrency` 5; panels run one docket at a time | inc-033 |
| 5 | A run hits the runtime's 1,000-agent cap mid-stage | The agent ceiling, a 200-agent reserve, and fan-out checks before each span; about 43 agents a typical increment (C-074) | inc-017 |
| 6 | Increments up to inc-014 are built from atoms no registered run has verified | The bootstrap verification record maps every sentence of Sam's requirements to an atom or a checked dismissal before inc-001; `check --strict` passes over the backlog before inc-010; inc-010 follows inc-014 and verifies every atom; a corrected atom of a built increment becomes a variant with a follow-up increment (C-053, C-055) | before inc-001, then inc-007 and inc-010 |
| 7 | A self-hosting build calls half-edited tools, or its pinned tools stall on a permission prompt | The tool pin lands before the first self-hosted build (`sequence`); `checkout: clones` keeps the build's own writes out of the live checkout; `run open` checks that the pinned tim answers `--version --json` headless and stops with `environment` if it would prompt; the pinned tim runs through the allowed `npm --prefix` form, so no allow rule is needed (C-064, C-065, C-079) | inc-024, then inc-025 |
| 8 | tim's auto-pull moves the workspace during a run | Both workflows refuse to start unless the workspace is on the state branch; auto-pull acts only on `main` | inc-017 |
| 9 | The persona seam changes how people use the review skills | The seam section applies only when a workflow runs the persona, and a person running the skill sees no change (req-044's criterion). It is proved where the review stage runs the personas, in inc-019, so the seam is tested in use rather than on its own | inc-019 |
| 10 | Codex falls short of the bar | The parity report names each failed measure and raises it as named hygiene work; `codex-claude-checks` keeps the checks on Claude meanwhile; S4's default D measures the bar against the spread between two Claude builds, with 90% and 80% floors (C-018) | inc-028 |
| 11 | The layer-split check refuses a legitimate single-repo behaviour | A decision can name the edge; hygiene is exempt from the tests-only check, and a product-repo hygiene increment needs a regression proof instead; messages name both rows | inc-011 |
| 11a | P11 passes a layer split, because the tests repo sits on every product row and no two rows ever have disjoint repo sets | The five layer-chain rules compare product repo sets with the tests repo left out, refuse provider-to-consumer chains by `consumes`, and require an `edgeChecks` record on every other cross-repo edge (C-025). req-115's criteria refuse a backend-and-tests row followed by a frontend-and-tests row for one behaviour, and a chain | inc-011 |
| 12 | End-to-end flakes on a fresh stack | One retry, with `error-context.md` evidence; a flake is recorded, never deleted | inc-041, inc-027 |
| 13 | Machine-written report prose claims something false, or runs past what a reader can take in | The distiller's own PROSE_CLAIM_VERIFIER reverts it; counts are derived, never written; `report --lint` holds every slot to its budget (C-038) | inc-037 |
| 14 | Codex reviews against a thinner standard until S2 is ruled | Default A, in force as d-005, points both executors at the house rules by path | inc-014 |
| 15 | A Workflow runtime change breaks args or resume | A zero-agent canary on the current runtime is inc-003's first acceptance run (gap-4) | inc-003 |
| 16 | SonarCloud never reports on a PR, and the pipeline commits without a local Sonar analysis | "No report" is recorded and shown, never read as clean. The push-record hook never fires for the build's pushes, which name their repo (`as-push-hook-scope`), so SonarCloud evidence comes only from PR checks. The deviation from CLAUDE.md rule 3 is listed for Sam to accept or refuse (C-042) | inc-021, inc-022 |
| 17 | Work built on Codex before the proof is weaker | This programme builds on Claude until inc-028 passes | inc-028 |
| 18 | A red integration proof is misread as an acceptance failure, or a proof runs on another session's mid-spike checkout | The proof is its own increment (inc-041), ahead of acceptance, with a record of every service's source and ref; untouched repos come from images or clean checkouts, never a working tree (C-020, C-021) | inc-041, inc-021 |
| 19 | A step agent alters a generated command | The prompt forbids it, commands are short with tilde paths, every result is observed, and a push to the wrong ref leaves the expected ref unmatched and holds `land-failed` (C-074, C-076) | inc-017, inc-022 |
| 20 | The span walker diverges from `advance` | A contract test on fixture graphs; the next `advance` re-derives the span; the audit re-derives every skip (C-077, C-081) | inc-019 |
| 21 | A relayed span graph is large | Compact index encoding; count and sha checked (C-070); one re-call, then a hold | inc-019 |
| 22 | Outward calls are allowlisted, so the allowlist does not gate them | tim refuses before generating (branch proof, fast-forward, expected head, every PR green, a person's merge decision), guard-bash sees each call, and observation records it (C-075, C-076) | inc-022 |

### 12.2 Prove first, in this order

1. **The requirements themselves.** Before inc-001, the bootstrap verification record maps every sentence of Sam's requirements to an atom or a checked dismissal, and a fresh critic checks the questions (11.1 item 0). Nothing is built on a requirement nobody but its author has read.
2. **The runtime.** A zero-agent Workflow canary with args as an object and as a string, and no fallback (inc-003). Everything else rides on args.
3. **The writer core.** The parity core moves with every existing test unchanged and the EUDPA-328 page identical, and keeps every id stable on a requirements-v2 fixture (inc-004, C-043). If this fails, the design falls back to a sibling writer that imports parity's modules without moving them. That is one decision to reverse, and it does not touch the backlog.
4. **The Codex adapter, alone.** `tim backlog codex run`'s vitest suite against `test-support/fake-codex.js`, including a rejected draft corrected on resume, a live and a stale lock, and a grandchild process (inc-015). Then one real Codex task in each sandbox mode, a live canary that asserts (C-009):
   - the effort and model read back from the `--json` event stream match the pipeline's `config.toml`;
   - a resumed read-only session refuses a write;
   - `-o` is written under read-only;
   - the session id comes from the `thread.started` event and matches the one resumed.
5. **The drain, without models.** A fixture programme drained by step agents only through `tim backlog advance`, with one step agent per gap: stops, holds, resume and the agent ceiling, and the same fixture driven by a scripted handover loop reaching byte-identical run state (inc-017, C-002, C-074).
6. **Local git writes in tim, on fixtures.** Branch cut, clean and conflicting merges of base, commits found again by trailer, and a push reported failed that in fact reached the remote, recorded as pushed and not pushed again (inc-017, inc-022, C-075, C-076).
7. **R7 on the fixture.** The multi-repo fixture programme (a provider, a consumer and a tests repo as small local repos, with a real contract test) is drained with one branch name in every repo. On it, the plan adds a repo and sets it up (inc-018), the consistency review names a renamed field at its seam (inc-019), the integration proof records what ran on what (inc-041), and the merge plan puts the provider first (inc-022). Every later full-stack claim rests on this step (C-036).
8. **The requirements, verified.** inc-010 follows inc-014 and verifies every atom in a registered run before the new path builds anything from them.
9. **Self-hosting.** inc-025 is built by the new path with pinned tools and `checkout: clones`.
10. **Real product work on Claude.** Three full-stack canaries with integration proofs, then a walkthrough (inc-027).
11. **Agent counts per increment.** From the journal of the first canary builds, cheap agents and judgement agents per increment by tier, against `consolidation/decision.md` section 6 (inc-027; inc-042 proves the count on the fixture first).
12. **Codex parity.** The same canaries on Codex and a second time on Claude, measured against the Claude-to-Claude spread, with cross-examination and the reading audit, then a walkthrough (inc-028).
13. **The distiller.** The walking skeleton first (inc-029), then the plants canary against the known digest, counting vertical increments against the digest's layer-split rows, then a walkthrough (inc-038).
